# Source ingest conventions

## Scope
Source ingest: the provider registry and its adapters, entry validation, the per-board health sidecar, and related job-lifecycle mechanics. The board catalog itself is `internal/ingest/boardcatalog`.

## Always true
- **The board catalog lives in Postgres, not in `sources/`** (#2357). `cmd/ingest <provider>` reads the `boards` table through `internal/ingest/boardcatalog`; a board is added with `cmd/add-board --provider=… --board=… --company=… --apply`, which reports by default and writes only under `--apply`. **The argument is a PROVIDER KEY, not a path** — pass a file path and the run reads no boards, logs `providers=0` and exits 0, which looks exactly like an empty catalogue.
- **The `sources/` directory is gone, and so is every board file.** `freehire-ops' provision/host2/gen-ingest-timers.sh` used to generate one systemd timer per `sources/*.yml`; it now enumerates `SELECT provider FROM boards WHERE status IN ('pending','active')`, so a provider is SCHEDULED because it has live boards. Adding a provider is an adapter, a line in `sources.All`, its boards via `cmd/add-board` — and a run of that generator on the host, which nothing deploys for you.
- **`sources.All` maps each `provider` string to a registered adapter** (`Source` interface) over a shared HTTP client. Adding a platform is a new adapter + one line in `sources.All`.
- **Run-once-and-exit worker** meant for cron (one schedule per provider, so providers crawl independently). No long-lived process. **What decides that a provider is due lives in [internal/ingest/ingestsched](../ingestsched/AGENTS.md)** — the `ingest_schedule` / `ingest_run_state` tables and `cmd/ingest-scheduler` — not in a systemd unit file. The unit name is built from the same `boards.provider` string that selects the boards, so a provider cannot be scheduled under a name no adapter answers to; that is what `habr_career` was, crawling nothing for a day behind a timer named after the retired file `habrcareer.yml`.
- **A 429 holds back the whole HOST, not just the request that drew it** (`hostbackoff.go`). `Client.do` records every direct-egress 429 per host and makes every later request to that host wait — the server's `Retry-After` when it names longer, otherwise 1s, doubling to a 60s cap for each refusal that arrives after the previous hold expired (the 429s of one in-flight burst count once, or the first hold would jump straight to the cap), reset by the next 2xx. Honouring `Retry-After` per REQUEST was not enough under eight board workers: the request that drew the refusal waited and the other seven fired straight into it. Measured 2026-10-18 on workable (direct IP + refusal-retry proxy, which returns on the second refusal with no wait at all): once its budget tripped ~30 minutes into a run, the rest of the fleet (~1,500 boards) failed inside three minutes at ~1,000 boards a minute, 18,520 refusals in two days (9,311 board listings, each a recorded board failure, and 9,303 company-description fetches). Held instead, the run slows to what the host admits and the boards it never reaches keep their last state. The hold also stops the client's own retries going out beneath a pacer (the workstream and join notes below). The refusal fallback is a different IP and is not held.
- **Adapters are read-only over public ATS JSON APIs.** Per-board crawl is independent: one failing board is counted (`stats.Failed`) but does not abort the rest.
- **Sources are keyless by default.** The exceptions are `usajobs` (`USAJOBS_API_KEY`), `reed` (`REED_API_KEY`) and one WhatJobs adapter per market — the vendor issues a separate publisher account per country, so `whatjobsMarkets` in `internal/ingest/sources/whatjobs.go` lists one `{code, provider, country}` row per onboarded market (`whatjobs` for the US, `whatjobs-<code>` for the rest, e.g. `whatjobs-br`), and every account's credential lives in the single `WHATJOBS_PUBLISHER_IDS` variable (`"code:id,code:id,..."`, e.g. `"us:7065,br:7076"`) rather than one env var per market. A market with confirmed but thin inventory (`whatjobs-au`, `whatjobs-sv` etc.) is still onboarded. A thin account whose WHOLE pool fits the page budget (`whatjobs-cr`, `whatjobs-tz` and the rest added 2026-10-19, each under ~2k postings, nearly none of them IT) is crawled through the single board `*`, which sends the empty keyword and keeps every posting — keyword slices find almost nothing there, and the catalogue is broader than IT. A blank board is still refused; `*` exists so that asking for a whole pool is written on purpose rather than reached by a typo. The credential lives in the env, never in the catalog.
- **`All` is the crawl registry, `Taxonomy` is the classification one — the credential gates only the first.** `sources.All(client)` omits a keyed provider whose variable is unset, so its boards fail validation before any request goes out. `sources.Taxonomy()` (`All(nil)`, no transport, never Fetch through it) is **total**: what kind of source a provider is cannot depend on whether this host can crawl it. Everything that classifies rather than crawls — `FilterableProviders`, `ProviderKind` on the status page, `AggregatorProviders` in `cmd/reindex` and `cmd/ghost-crosscheck` — reads `Taxonomy`. Conflating the two silently reclassified `whatjobs`, a reseller of first-party ATS postings, as an ATS on every keyless host, so none of its copies were suppressed.
- **A board is not always a tenant.** For `hh` it is a `professional_role`, for `trudvsem` an OKATO region code, for `whatjobs` a SEARCH KEYWORD, for `edjoin` a JOB-TYPE id. Such a provider is an `aggregator` (many employers, company read per posting) but NOT `boardless` — the board is what selects the slice to crawl. Note that a central searchable index does not by itself make a provider boardless: `edjoin` has exactly one, and the board still earns its keep, because what the slice buys is not reachability but a catalogue the ingest filter cannot police on its own (see the EDJOIN traps below).
- **`sweepGrace` overrides the unseen sweep window in either direction.** `whatjobs` reads at most 40 pages of a keyword from a feed thousands of pages deep, so a posting that drifts past that depth reads as unseen; on the 48-hour default it would be closed and reopened repeatedly, each cycle writing a phantom removal into `job_daily_stats`. The adapter declares 14 days instead (`sources.SweepGraceWindows` → `cmd/ingest`'s `sweepWindowFor`). Widening is sound only where liveness cannot be probed — anything verifiable should close on evidence. The opposite case is `gem`: its crawl lists a board's whole posting set every run, so an unseen posting already IS the evidence, and 48h only adds latency — it declares 24h instead, comfortably above its hourly cadence including a missed run or two. Narrowing the SHARED default itself, rather than one provider's override, would falsely close a quarter of workday's and smartrecruiters' live postings to real crawl jitter (measured in `cmd/ingest/coverage.go`).
- **Which page failed decides whether the board failed.** Every paginated listing walk obeys one rule: the FIRST page failing is a board-level error, a LATER page failing ends the walk with the links gathered so far. A partial crawl survives a mid-listing hiccup; a board that cannot be reached at all is reported. The canonical statement is `crawlPagedLinks` in `html.go`, and `added == 0` is what ends a walk that has run out of new links.

  **The exception is the one that matters.** A `fullCatalog` provider is swept by SOURCE, so a silently-truncated walk looks like a shrunken catalogue and would mass-close every posting past the failed page. There the rule inverts: ANY page failing must fail the whole crawl, steering the sweep back to the safe company-scoped close. `crawlAllPagedLinks` is that variant. Both `fullCatalog` providers honour it — `geekjob` through the helper, `habrcareer` in its own JSON loop with the reasoning written at the branch — which is the point: **what must not drift is the rule, not the helper.**

  Eight adapters restate the walk by hand rather than call it (`peopleforce`, `bayt`, `teamtailor`, `neogov`, `hh`, `gusto`, `edjoin`, `workstream`), and that is deliberate, not an oversight: they differ in transport (`GetHTML` / `GetTextWithHeaders` / embedded page state), in what they dedup on (URL, extracted job id, int64 vacancy id), in extra stop conditions (`neogov` reads a `total`, `workstream` reads a `totalPages` and starts from a page already in hand, `teamtailor` retries `/jobs`→root on a 404 and then follows the path it landed on), and in what a page must YIELD (`gusto` and `workstream` need each item's whole listing row — title, location, pay, employment type — not just its link, because that row is everything the platform states about a posting bar its body; `edjoin` needs the same, and additionally stops on an exact `totalRecords`). A generic over those eight would be harder than the fifteen-line loop it replaced. When touching one, check its first-page branch — and note that **`hh` counts from page 0**, so its check is `page == 0`, not `page == 1`.

  Seven of the eight (`neogov`, `edjoin`, `workstream`, `peopleforce`, `gusto`, `teamtailor`, `bayt`) no longer follow the "later page ends the walk with what was gathered" half of the rule above: they earned `fullBoardListing` (below) by turning a later-page failure, and reaching their own page-cap safety ceiling without ever finding the board's proven end (a genuinely empty page, or — for `neogov`/`edjoin`/`workstream` — the source's own declared total/page count), into a hard `Fetch` failure instead. The empty-page proof for all seven is now the RAW per-page item count, read before cross-page dedup — not the count of newly-kept items — because a page whose rows are all already-seen duplicates (a sort tie spanning a page boundary, a re-served page) is not itself proof the board has no more pages beyond it; each has a regression test for exactly that shape. `peopleforce`'s and `bayt`'s `detail()` additionally gained the `unreadableDetail` marker (see the entry below) on a failed-but-not-gone fetch, since neither has a `HydratingSource` fallback and every crawl re-fetches every listed posting's detail — a plain drop there would have defeated the very completeness promise the marker exists to make. `teamtailor`'s own gap was live, not hypothetical: a probe against real prod boards found `ttMaxPages=100` silently truncating a real one — `tantor.teamtailor.com` (2000 open postings in the catalogue) still returned full listing pages at 100 and 101, with its true end only past page 120, a confirmed ~20-25% under-collection on a live board — closed by raising the cap to 1000 (`openspec/changes/teamtailor-listing-cap-fix`). `bayt` was initially excluded from the first wave over a documented throttling risk, but that risk is tied specifically to the CONCURRENT detail fan-out (`baytDetailWorkers`, already narrowed for exactly this reason) — the listing walk itself is sequential, one request at a time, and carries no evidence of the same risk, so excluding it on an unexamined worry stopped being warranted once examined (`openspec/changes/bayt-fullboardlisting-fix`). `hh` was audited alongside these seven and deliberately does NOT earn the marker: hh.ru's own search UI caps a query's reachable depth at ~2000 results independent of a role's true count, confirmed live against a currently-configured board (`hhru.go`'s `crawl` carries the measurement) — treating that cap as a Fetch failure would fail a busy board's crawl on every run, not surface a genuine truncation. `apploi` — then the largest source in the WHOLE registry by real prod volume (~1.47M open postings, larger than `workday`'s own ~1.05M) — was the first of the ~163 previously-unaudited adapters to be checked, found to share the identical defect, and fixed the same way (`openspec/changes/apploi-fullboardlisting-fix`); **that adapter has since been deleted outright** (see the apploi entry below — the volume was 99.81% duplicates and the real catalogue is entirely healthcare), so what follows describes it in the past tense: unlike the eight, its "last page" proof (a page shorter than the requested size) needed no dedup-vs-raw-count correction (it never deduplicated across pages to begin with) and it needed no `unreadableDetail` treatment (descriptions are inline, no per-posting detail fetch exists). The remaining ~162 adapters in the registry are still fully unaudited; ranked by real prod open-job volume, `oracle` (~459K) is next.

- **`sources.fullBoardListing` marks an adapter fit for the post-run sweep's board-scoped close** (`CloseUnseenJobsForBoard`, freehire#2328 — see `docs/agents/job-lifecycle.md`'s mechanism (1a) for why that scope exists). The bar is structural, not documented: `Fetch` must, for every board it crawls, either verify a fetched count against the source's own reported total or paginate to the source's natural end. A page/offset safety cap in the adapter's own code is permitted, but only as a ceiling that FAILS the crawl when reached before the end is proven — never as an alternate, silent way to end the walk as a success; that distinction is what lets `hh`/`neogov`/`edjoin`/`workstream`/`peopleforce`/`gusto`/`teamtailor`/`bayt` all keep a safety cap and still earn the marker, while a cap that could be reached and still report success would not. `habrcareer.go`'s `fullCatalog` `Fetch` (any page error is a hard error, never a partial result) is the model to follow; `solidjobs.go` is the negative one it was written against — it caps a board at the first 500 of a much larger catalogue and reports that as an unqualified success, which is exactly what closed 110 live `solidjobs` postings in #2337's 18-minute production incident before the revert. Self-declaration by code review is not enough to earn the marker, because that is solidjobs' own failure mode: its cap was documented in a comment nobody's code read. `sources.FullBoardListingProviders(reg)` is the registry helper `cmd/ingest` consults; `TestSolidJobsIsNotFullBoardListing` pins the negative control. As of this writing the marker covers a first, impact-ordered wave of large ATS platforms — `workday`, `ukg`, `greenhouse`, `lever`, `ashby`, `workable`, `paylocity`, `careerplug`, `smartrecruiters`, `personio`, `recruitee`, `bamboohr`, `jazzhr`, `icims`, `successfactors`, `jobvite`, `breezy`, `eightfold`, `taleo`, `geekhunter`, `keka`, `gupy` — four of which (`workday`, `careerplug`, `icims`, `taleo`) needed a soft page/depth cap turned into a hard failure before they could earn it — plus a second wave, seven of the hand-rolled walkers named above that shared the identical defect: `neogov`, `edjoin`, `workstream`, `peopleforce`, `gusto`, `teamtailor`, `bayt` — and `apploi`, found and fixed the same way outside that set, as the first of the registry's remaining unaudited adapters to be checked, though that adapter has since been deleted outright and no longer carries the marker or anything else. `hh` was audited alongside the seven and found to still fail the bar, for a structural reason of its own (see above); the rest of the registry is unmarked, not because it fails the bar, but because nobody has audited it yet.
- **Dedup key is `jobs.UNIQUE (source, external_id)`.** `UpsertJob` is `ON CONFLICT` on it.
- **An Indian salary LABEL goes through one parser, `inrSalary` (`salary_inr.go`)** — use it
  in any adapter whose structured salary field is a display string ("₹12L – ₹18L", "12-18
  Lacs P.A.", "INR 50,000 per month"), never on prose. A lakh or crore figure is annual CTC,
  as Indian usage fixes it; a plain rupee figure is read only beside a stated period, because
  "₹10,000 – ₹15,000" is a year's pay for nobody and a month's stipend for an intern, and a
  grouped figure beside a lakh one ("₹75,000 – ₹1.5L") does not borrow its unit. "Not
  Disclosed" and prose read as no salary. Wired into `wellfound` (2026-10-25: 572 of 905
  Indian postings gain a range; dollar ranges stay display text).
- **A detail request that FAILED and a posting the platform says is GONE are different answers, and an adapter whose detail is a posting's only source must not spell them the same way.** `fetchDetails` drops a posting whose fetch returns `ok=false`, and for the non-hydrating link-only ATS adapters — `jazzhr`, `icims`, `careerplug`, `jobvite`, `successfactors`, `breezy`, `bamboohr`, `freshteam` — that drop was the whole reporting of a timeout or a refusal: `Fetch` came back shorter, `Stats.Failed` stayed 0, the board was recorded HEALTHY, and a posting unreadable for 48 hours was closed by both sweeps as unseen. Those adapters now return `unreadableDetail(...)` with `ok=true` instead, and `sources.detailUnreadable` is the one place the line is drawn: **404/410 is the platform's own answer that the posting is gone** and stays a drop; everything else — a timeout, a 403/429, a 5xx, an undecodable body, a 200 carrying no posting — is the ABSENCE of an answer and becomes a `Job{Unreadable: true}` marker the pipeline counts (`Stats.Unreadable`) and acts on (see [internal/ingest/pipeline/AGENTS.md](../pipeline/AGENTS.md)). A link with no posting id at all stays a drop too: nothing was ever stored under it, so no close can reach it. **This is NOT a board failure and must not become one** — a dropped detail is a per-posting hole, and failing the board would cool it to the 24-hour ceiling and freeze its whole catalogue. Hydrating adapters (`workday`, `seek`, `gusto`, `applitrack`, `ukgready`, `smartrecruiters`, `gupy` — the last two's unseen postings still get the marker) are outside all of this: a seen posting yields a `SeenRefresh` and costs no detail request, so the "a drop is normal" arguments below do not carry over to the non-hydrating case. `paylocity`, `personio`, `recruitee` and `ukg` are outside it too, for the opposite reason — their listing is authoritative for a posting's existence and a failed detail costs only the body.
- **The same distinction exists one level up, at the BOARD, and there it does not arrive as a status code.** A platform's own "no such board" answer can be a well-formed page under HTTP 200, so nothing below the adapter can tell it from a working response and the adapter fails further along, on whatever it could not parse. Paycom serves `Job board does not exist.` in place of the portal shell, and ApplicantPro/iSolved Hire serve `This career site has been disabled.` or `You may have typed the url for this website incorrectly.` at `sitemap.xml`. Both surfaced in `board_health` as an adapter bug — a missing session token and an XML syntax error on line 3 — under which **92 boards sat gone for two months** until an audit went looking. An adapter that can recognise the platform's answer wraps `sources.ErrBoardGone`; the crawl still fails and `BoardHealth` still counts it, but the failure is now nameable, so these boards are selected by a query instead of read out of error prose. **Nothing retires a board automatically**: a platform-wide outage that started serving one of those pages would read as its whole fleet dying at once, so the sentinel informs the decision rather than taking it. Match a stable FRAGMENT of the notice, never the whole sentence — vendor copy gets reworded, and a check that silently stops matching puts us back where this started.

  **BambooHR is the third form, and it is not a body at all: it REDIRECTS.** The careers-list request 302s, the client follows it, and the JSON decode fails on the landing page's HTML — `invalid character '<' looking for beginning of value`, an adapter bug for the third time. It reached 135 boards. The adapter therefore takes `JSONResolvedGetter` (`GetJSONResolved`, the JSON counterpart of the existing `GetHTMLResolved`) and reads the FINAL url: **leaving `<board>.bamboohr.com/careers` is the verdict, not any particular destination.** That is deliberate — the destination encodes WHY (`www.bamboohr.com` no tenant, `/login.php` public board switched off, `/settings/account/expired.php` lapsed, `/settings/account/temporarily_suspended`), and those reasons keep their own vocabulary, so a rule naming them would miss the next one. The destination rides in the error message instead, because they do not mean the same thing to whoever decides on retirement — `temporarily_suspended` is a board to leave alone — and learning that distinction the first time cost 138 hand-run probes. An empty final url is never a verdict: a request that got no response at all must keep reading as a transport failure.
- **`sources.SelfClosingProviders`** lists providers whose adapters implement the `selfClosing` marker — they emit `Job{Removed: true}` for taken-down postings and are excluded from the unseen-job sweep.
- **`board_health` holds ONLY runtime state** — the catalog itself is the `boards` table (first bullet), and a row left behind for a board no longer crawled is inert. Retiring a board does not delete its health row, so `consecutive_failures` and `last_error` stay readable as the record of why it was retired.
- **An adapter MAY yield the platform's application form (`Job.ApplyForm`), but only when its list endpoint already carries one.** Today that is `recruitee` alone: its `/api/offers/` response holds `open_questions` and the `options_*` standard-field flags beside the posting, so the form costs no extra request and `applyform.FromRecruitee` maps it in place. Every other adapter leaves it nil, which is not a failure. **An adapter must never issue a request to fill it** — a form that costs a request would make a crawl's duration a function of board size, and the adapter cannot tell which postings are new anyway (that answer exists only after `UpsertJob`'s `ON CONFLICT` resolves). Those platforms — `greenhouse`, `ashby`, `workable`, `lever` — are captured after ingest by `cmd/capture-apply-form`; `applyform.NeedsRequestCapture` is the gate, and it and the fetcher registry read one map so the queue cannot fill with work nothing can drain.
- **An adapter MAY implement `CompanyDescriber` to yield its board's own company-level description**, fetched once per board crawl — never once per posting, since it is a fact about the board, not about any one job. `greenhouse` and `workable` implement it today. `greenhouse` fetches `boards-api.greenhouse.io/v1/boards/{board}` (distinct from the `/jobs` listing `Fetch` calls), which carries an employer-authored "about us" `content` field for a meaningful share of boards (confirmed live: Coinbase, Figma, Asana). `workable` fetches its OWN `apply.workable.com/api/v1/widget/accounts/{board}` endpoint again but WITHOUT `?details=true` — that flag only controls whether each job's own full body is inlined, so dropping it shrinks the response substantially (~11x on a sampled board) while leaving the account-level `description` field unaffected, so the second request is cheap rather than a duplicate of `Fetch`'s own (potentially much larger) payload; a first design spike sampled only two large, atypically-run boards (GitLab, Canva) and found the field empty on both, but a follow-up spike against 40 boards drawn at random from the production catalog found it filled on 32 (80%). Both adapters sanitize through the same `sanitizeHTML` helper posting bodies use, with no `html.UnescapeString` (both platforms serve this field as raw HTML, not entity-encoded). The pipeline (`Runner.fillCompanyDescription`) calls whichever capability an adapter implements at most once per board — right after the adapter lookup, independent of whether that board's own `Fetch`/`FetchStream` succeeds, and **only when the Store says the company is due** (`CompanyDescriptionDue`: no `company_info.summary` yet AND no answered fetch recorded in `companies.company_description_checked_at` within `pipeline.CompanyDescriptionRefreshWindow`, 7 days). Until 2026-10-19 it was asked on every run, which measured as half of Workable's ~63k requests a day and half of its 18,520 refusals in two days, for a field the fill can never change once stored. An answered fetch is recorded even when blank (only the stamp is written), so a board with no "about us" costs one request a week rather than one a run; a failed fetch records nothing and is retried next run. The pipeline applies a non-empty result to `companies.company_info.summary` via the optional `CompanyDescriptionFiller` Store capability — never `tagline`, which stays reserved for the short, curated-style values the YC directory and the Wikipedia backfill produce. Neither a missing capability on either side nor an adapter error propagates: a blank field, an unimplemented interface, and a fetch failure are all "no description, no write," exactly like an optional `ApplyForm` an adapter chooses not to fill. Ashby and Pinpoint expose no such field at all.
- **`adzuna`'s API description is a snippet, not the posting.** Adzuna's own docs say so — "we currently only provide a snippet of the job description" — confirmed live at exactly 500 characters, ellipsis-terminated, no parameter unlocks the rest. The full text lives only on Adzuna's own hosted job page (`schema.org` JobPosting ld+json, read via `sources.LDJobPosting`) — but only the `/details/...` URL shape reaches it; the `/land/ad/...` ad-network tracking-redirect shape answers Adzuna's own branded "Access Denied" page to a non-browser request, browser-shaped headers included (confirmed live 2026-08-08). `internal/ingest/adzunadesc` is the same after-ingest capture-queue shape as apply-form (`adzunadesc.Eligible` is the gate, mirroring `NeedsRequestCapture`), except the payload overwrites `jobs.description` directly via the existing `UpdateJobDescription` query rather than living in a new table — `adzuna_description_hydrated` exists purely as the queue's "don't ask again" marker, since apply_forms' own payload table played that role for free. `cmd/hydrate-adzuna-description` drains the ongoing queue; `cmd/seed-adzuna-description-queue` is the one-off pass that queues the ~21k rows already in the catalogue from before this shipped. **Verified live in prod (2026-08-09):** ~6.5k fetches over the first ~8h, no blocking from Adzuna at this pace — the ~40% failure rate that first run showed was NOT blocking. Two causes, both diagnosed from `last_error`: genuinely-gone content (404, or a 200 page whose ld+json carries no JobPosting block — Adzuna's own page, nothing to fix) and a real bug, now fixed: **a job's stored `url` can drift between the two shapes for the SAME posting across re-crawls** (confirmed: enqueued eligible at 03:03, re-crawled to the `/land/ad/` shape by 08:25, claimed and 403-ed three times by 10:18). `Eligible` is checked once at enqueue; the runner now re-checks the URL shape on the CLAIM (which reads `jobs.url` fresh) before fetching, and `Store.Discard`s a drifted claim — deleted, not dead-lettered, so a later crawl that restores the eligible shape can re-enqueue it — rather than burning retries against a page it already knows will 403.

## How it works

**Board registry:** each adapter implements the `Source` interface and speaks a common normalized `Job` shape. `sources.All` is the registry map. Adapters are in `internal/ingest/sources/` — one `.go` file per provider (`greenhouse.go`, `lever.go`, `ashby.go`, …) with matching `_test.go` files. `config.go` holds `Config.Validate` (the registry check) and `BoardDedupeKey` (the identity two catalog rows are one crawl target under).

**Ingest pipeline:** `cmd/ingest` loads the provider's live boards from the catalog, then delegates to the `pipeline.Runner` which fetches each board once, normalizes postings, and `UpsertJob`s them (idempotent on the dedup key). New postings are enqueued into `enrichment_outbox` in the same transaction (transactional-outbox).

**Per-board health:** `board_health (provider, board, region)` tracks `consecutive_failures`, `cooldown_until`, `last_error`, timestamps, `last_ingested_count`. `region` (`text NOT NULL DEFAULT ''`, migration `0077`) disambiguates a board id that repeats across independent regional slices — Adzuna's `it-jobs` exists once per country — so each slice gets its own health row; every region-less provider passes `''`, matching the column's default. The `pipeline.Runner` uses an optional `BoardHealth` port (nil = feature off). It **skips a board whose `cooldown_until` is in the future** (counted `Cooled`, not `Failed`). Backoff (`pipeline.CooldownFor`): no cooldown below **3** consecutive failures, then `6h·2^(f-3)` capped at **24h**. A success self-heals. The backoff is Go-owned and unit-tested.

**Incremental search indexing:** `cmd/ingest` queues each crawl's **new or content-changed** open jobs into `search_outbox` (`job_id` only, `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`), atomically in the same transaction as the upsert — the worker never talks to Meilisearch. The change signal is `jobs.content_hash` returned by `UpsertJob` as `inserted`/`changed`. The enqueue is unconditional on `MEILI_MASTER_KEY` — an unconfigured deployment simply never drains the queue. Draining is `cmd/search-drain`'s job, on its own schedule (see [internal/search/searchdrain/AGENTS.md](../../search/searchdrain/AGENTS.md)). Full reindex (`cmd/reindex`) stays the source of truth — it owns settings, compaction, and removing closed-job documents.

**Proxy egress (opt-in, IP-blocklisted providers):** some ATS edges IP-blocklist the prod datacenter IP (e.g. eightfold 403s every prod-IP request while a residential IP is served). `SOURCES_PROXY_URL` (form `http://user:pass@host:port`) routes only the providers in the `proxiedProviders` allowlist through that egress proxy; everything else stays on the direct IP. Unset = no-op; set-but-invalid fails the run at startup. `cmd/ingest` calls `sources.ApplyProxyEgress(registry)` after `All`. The SEEK network (`seek`, `jobstreet`) is a third, narrower case: it stays direct and gets the proxy only as a fallback lane for its GraphQL detail after SEEK refuses the direct IP — see the JobStreet / JobsDB traps. The proxy endpoint + credentials live entirely in env — nothing is hardcoded. **SSRF caveat:** on the proxied path the guarded dialer vets the *proxy's* IP, not the ultimate target (the proxy resolves that), so `proxiedProviders` must list only trusted, fixed-host providers — never the link-following/liveness paths, which keep the direct target-guarded client.

**Link-following (`internal/ingest/linksource/`):** resolves a single outbound job-detail URL into a vacancy under the destination's own identity. A `LinkSource` adapts a single detail page (unlike `sources` which adapts a whole ATS board). Matching by link host. The resolved job dedups against the same posting if another source also has it.

**Job lifecycle — soft-close via `closed_at`:**
1. **Ingest sweep** (`CloseUnseenJobs`): post-run sweep closes a provider's jobs unseen for 48h. A reappearing posting reopens via the upsert. Self-closing sources are excluded.
2. **Stream-driven self-close** (`CloseJobBySourceExternalID`): self-closing sources emit `Job{Removed: true}`; the pipeline routes this to the Store's optional `closer`.
3. **Liveness probe** (`cmd/liveness`): URL-probes orphan jobs from non-board sources. Closes after two consecutive `expired` reads (the `liveness_strikes` counter).

**Telegram ingest** is a two-stage queue (crawl then LLM-extract): `cmd/tg-ingest` crawls the active `telegram_channels` rows into `telegram_posts`; `cmd/tg-extract` drains via the LLM. Both are run-once-and-exit cron workers.

**JobStreet / JobsDB traps** (live-verified 2026-09-12):

- **It is the SEEK frontend protocol on different market hosts.** `sg.jobstreet.com`, `my.jobstreet.com`, `id.jobstreet.com`, `ph.jobstreet.com`, `hk.jobsdb.com` and `th.jobsdb.com` all serve the same `/api/jobsearch/v5/search` schema and `POST /graphql` `jobDetails` operation as SEEK AU/NZ. Keep one implementation: `NewSeek` and `NewJobStreet` differ only in provider identity, market map and paging policy.
- **The market tuple is load-bearing.** SG = `SG-Main` + `Singapore`, MY = `MY-Main` + `Malaysia`, ID = `ID-Main` + `Indonesia`, PH = `PH-Main` + `Philippines`, HK = `HK-Main` + `Hong Kong`, TH = `TH-Main` + `Thailand`. Do not mechanically prepend `All`: `where=All Singapore` returned zero while `where=Singapore` returned the live catalogue.
- **The ICT taxonomy is shared with SEEK.** Classification `6281` is Information & Communication Technology and all 22 subclassifications `6282..6303` were live in each of SG/MY/ID/PH/HK/TH on 2026-09-12. Those subclassifications are the board slices; a keyword search is not a substitute — `software` alone returned ~10k SG jobs and would destroy deterministic coverage.
- **JobStreet does NOT share SEEK AU/NZ's ~550-result window.** SG subclassification `6290` (Engineering - Software) returned 100 rows through page 16, 98 on page 17 and a natural empty page 18 (~1.7k rows). The same natural-end behaviour was rechecked while adding PH/TH: PH `6287` returned 49 rows on page 14 then empty page 15; TH `6290` returned 19 rows on page 5 then empty page 6. The JobStreet walk therefore has a generous 100-page safety cap and FAILS if it reaches that cap without a natural end; accepting the cap as success would silently throw away inventory. SEEK keeps its historical six-page capped-window behaviour.
- **One GraphQL budget for the whole network.** The registry constructs one `seekDetailNetwork` (`seekdetail.go`, over one `pacedSeekPoster`) and passes it to both providers; `ApplyProxyEgress` rebuilds both over ONE replacement network (`seekNetworkProviders`). Building it twice would create two independent token buckets, double the per-IP rate production measured as safe, and let one provider keep hammering a network the other has seen banned. Search GETs remain unpaced; only detail POSTs go through the network.
- **SEEK's rate limit is a 200, not a 429 — and it is a cumulative budget the ~2 req/s pace does not stay under.** On 2026-10-15, 110 new JobStreet boards (MY/ID/PH/HK/TH) went live; the first run hydrated ~4.3k bodies at the shared ~2 req/s for 50 minutes, and then SEEK banned the prod IP on `jobDetails` across the WHOLE network (`www.seek.com.au`, `sg.jobstreet.com`, `th.jobsdb.com` alike) while a trivial `query{__typename}` still answered. The refusal is HTTP **200** carrying `{"errors":[{"message":"Too many requests.","path":["jobDetails","job"],"extensions":{"code":"RATE_LIMITED"}}],"data":{"jobDetails":null}}`. The adapter read that as "no content", deferred the posting and carried on: the next two runs each sent **~8k refused requests for 50 minutes** (the run timeout), stored **0** new postings, logged one line per posting, and very likely kept the ban alive. The same request through `SOURCES_PROXY_URL` was served in full. So the network now reads `errors[].extensions.code == "RATE_LIMITED"` (and an HTTP 429) as a refusal and acts on it once for the whole run: the first refusal on the direct IP moves every later detail request to a proxy lane with its OWN pacer (`seekProxyDetailInterval`, ~1 req/s — the proxy is one static address the `proxiedProviders` share); a refusal there too, or a refusal with no proxy configured, stops hydration for the rest of the run. A run also spends at most `seekDetailRunBudget` (1,500) detail requests across both lanes — about a third of the low end of the 4k–7k the ban arrived after — so a bootstrap accretes over several 2-hourly runs instead of sustaining 50 minutes of traffic. Every posting left without a body is deferred, never stored body-less, and the switch and the stop are each ONE log line with the per-lane counts. The state is per process (one provider per `cmd/ingest` run), so each run re-probes the direct IP and the budget bounds what that costs.
- **Apply aggregator coverage before detail hydration.** JobStreet/JobsDB listings already carry the employer name, so the adapter implements `CoverageGated`: employers that freehire already covers through a first-party ATS are emitted list-only and spend no GraphQL detail request. The ordinary pipeline gate still makes and counts the discard decision. This is important on a first JobStreet crawl, where a large share of the raw catalogue is already direct-ATS covered and the shared GraphQL budget is only ~2 req/s.
- **JobStreet reaches a natural listing end, so it does not need SEEK's 14-day drift grace.** It uses the ordinary 48h sweep window. It still does not claim `fullBoardListing`: the shared crawler preserves the repository-wide rule that a later listing-page transport failure returns the partial rows gathered so far, so that run is not structural proof of full coverage.
- **The shared GraphQL budget was live-checked against all six markets before rollout, not just the two the contribution's own smoke test covered (SG, PH, TH).** On 2026-09-15 the search endpoint answered 200 with a full page of postings on all six hosts for ICT subclassification `6282`, and the `jobDetails` GraphQL operation was additionally verified end-to-end (real posting id in, non-empty `content` back) on MY, ID and HK — the three markets the original review had not exercised through detail. Requests were spaced at roughly the adapter's own `seekDetailInterval` (~2 req/s); none were refused. This does not replace watching the first real crawl, since a bootstrap run sustains that rate far longer than a handful of manual probes, but it rules out an immediate per-market block before the 132 board rows go live.

**Japan Dev traps** (live-verified 2026-09-12):

- **Use the sitemap, not Algolia.** `/sitemap.xml` is a public sitemap index pointing at `/cdn/sitemaps/sitemap.xml`; the leaf carried 290 canonical `/jobs/<company>/<slug>` URLs while `/jobs` reported 294 live jobs. The sitemap is therefore the cheap discovery layer but NOT structural proof of the whole catalogue. `japandev` must never claim `fullCatalog` or `fullBoardListing` while those counts can differ.
- **Hydrate by the sitemap slug.** The final URL segment is the external id because it is known before fetching the posting page. Seen postings cost no detail request; a first bootstrap is ~290 pages and steady state is only new postings. A failed new detail is deferred rather than stored body-less, so it is retried on the next crawl.
- **The page's primary job is structured Nuxt SSR state.** `#__NUXT_DATA__` is JSON but devalue/reference-indexed: object values are indexes into the top-level table. Resolve the semantic object carrying `job`, then that job's scalar/company/skills references. Do NOT pick the first object with job-looking fields: the same page also embeds related/featured jobs.
- **Keep the employer's official apply URL when stated.** `application_url` commonly points at Lever, Workable, HRMOS, etc.; use it as Job.URL and preserve Japan Dev's attribution query parameters. Fall back to the Japan Dev detail URL when the source uses email/internal apply and states no external URL.
- **Structured source labels stay structured, not guessed.** `remote_level`, `employment_type` and `seniority_level` map only to exact freehire vocabulary counterparts. Japan Dev's Japanese/English labels, candidate-location and visa-sponsorship facts have no lossless Job fields, so retain them as a short source-fact paragraph ahead of the body instead of inventing CEFR or work-authorization semantics.
- **Do not force Japan on worldwide-remote jobs.** The board itself is Japan-focused, but `remote_level_full_worldwide` + location `Remote` does not state a workplace country. Leave Countries empty for that shape; other postings use `jp` from the source's Japan market/location scope.
- **No metering observed at `defaultDetailWorkers` concurrency.** Live-checked 2026-09-15: two bursts of detail-page fetches (8 concurrent, matching the adapter's fan-out, then 40 more at the same concurrency drawn from later in the sitemap) all answered 200, none refused or throttled — 60 requests total against a catalogue of 290. The sitemap itself still listed exactly 290 posting URLs, matching the 2026-09-12 count. This does not rule out a limit only a full ~290-page bootstrap would reach, but it rules out an immediate block on the first real crawl, so `japandev` ships without a dedicated pacer like the other unmetered `defaultDetailWorkers` adapters.

**vagas.com.br traps** (`vagas`; live-verified 2026-10-23):

- **The whole catalogue is the EMPTY search, `/vagas-de-?pagina=N`.** The site's search path is `/vagas-de-<query>`, so every `/vagas-de-<area>` listing is a keyword search, not a partition: `vagas-de-tecnologia` stated 453, `vagas-de-todas-as-areas` 2 431, and the empty query 3 981 — the figure the page's own facet counts (state, hierarchy) sum to. The adapter crawled three tech keywords (~800 postings) until the catalogue became general. `/sitemap.xml` is no help: its newest `lastmod` is 2023-06-08. `/vagas/pesquisas` is disallowed by robots and not used.
- **Page N is not a fixed slice — the listing reshuffles on every request.** Even sorted `mais_recentes` (which orders by day only) the same page fetched twice from one IP came back different: two full walks read 3 260 and 3 240 distinct postings, overlapping in 2 734. So the walk does NOT use `crawlPagedLinks`: stopping at a page that adds nothing new, or at the first page that fails (7 of 100 through the production fetch path), read ~100 postings a run. It reads the heading's total on page 1 and fetches every page that total implies; a later page failing costs that page alone. Page 1 failing, or stating no total, is still the board failing. One walk reads ~78% (3 093 of 3 981 through Scrapling, 4m13s) and successive walks are close to independent, which is what keeps the ordinary 48-hour sweep safe — a posting must be missed by every run in two days. Never mark it `fullCatalog` or `fullBoardListing`.
- **Hydrating and coverage-gated.** The listing card carries the title and employer (`a.link-detalhes-vaga[title]`, `span.emprVaga`), so `FetchNewGated` yields a covered or rejected posting body-less instead of buying its detail page. Without the gate those postings — never stored, so never seen — would take hydration slots on every run (a quarter of the tech listing was covered). `vagasMaxNewPerRun` is 1 000: 30 detail pages took ~30s through the one-per-second pacer.
- **Production crawls it through the Scrapling tier** (`scraplingtier.go`, impersonating fetcher over the rotating proxy exit); `proxiedProviders` is only the fallback. The prod datacenter IPs are refused outright.

**ITviec traps** (`itviec`; live-verified 2026-10-15):

- **Reachable only from the crawl host.** From a residential IP and from a US datacenter, every path — `robots.txt` included — answers 403 with `cf-mitigated: challenge`, an INTERACTIVE Turnstile that neither `fingerprintHTTP` nor a headless Chrome clears. From the prod crawl host, plain HTTP answers 200 on the listing (`Mozilla/5.0` and `Go-http-client/2.0` UAs, ten sequential requests, no refusal). Validate from the crawl host; a local 403 says nothing about prod. `robots.txt` disallows only `/subscriptions/new`.
- **Boardless: one listing is the whole catalogue.** `/it-jobs?page=N` serves ~20 cards a page; the header said "658 IT jobs" and pages 1-33 held exactly 658 cards (32×20+18), page 34 none. Not `fullBoardListing`: that match is one snapshot, not a proven bar.
- **Read CARDS, not posting links.** Every page, including the one past the end, also links two "company spotlight" postings (`?lab_feature=company_spotlight`) that are not cards. A link-based walk never sees the end and reads the decoys as postings. The card element carries `data-search--job-selection-job-slug-value` and `data-job-key`.
- **The external id is `data-job-key`, not the slug's trailing number.** The suffix is four digits (`-0103`) — 10,000 values, so a catalogue that accumulates thousands of stored postings collides by construction. The key is the posting's own (the posting page's save-job element carries the same value as its id); 57 of 58 sampled are UUIDs and one is `e9661b73-ffd`, so it is treated as opaque.
- **The body is the ld+json `description`, and it is not HTML.** It is newline-separated lines: the four fixed section headings ("Top 3 Reasons To Join Us", "The Job", "Your Skills and Experience", "Why You'll Love Working Here") and the "top 3 reasons" as bare text, with each employer section as HTML. `jobBenefits` repeats the last section and is not read.
- **The ld+json `title` strips punctuation** ("AI Software Engineer Python/Go/C/C++  AI Agents"); the page's `<h1>` keeps it, so the title is read from there.
- **Salary is structured but often withheld.** The page hides pay behind sign-in, yet the ld+json `baseSalary` carries `minValue`/`maxValue`/`unitText` when the employer published a range (`800`-`2000` USD/MONTH). A withheld one carries the phrase "You'll love it" and no bound, and yields nothing.
- **`addressLocality` is the literal "Not Available"**; the city is `addressRegion` ("Hà Nội", "Hồ Chí Minh"). The work arrangement is the header badge (`At office`/`Hybrid`/`Remote`), not ld+json. `employmentType` was `FULL_TIME` on both sampled pages and `monthsOfExperience` read 10 and 37, so neither is mapped until a wider sample shows they mean something.
- **"ITviec Recruitment Consulting" is ITviec headhunting for an unnamed client** (4 of 58 sampled cards). It is stored under that name; the real employer is not stated anywhere on the page.
- **Paced at 1 req/s (`itviecRequestInterval`), unmeasured.** A third party saw 429 on back-to-back sequential listing sweeps (career-ops, 2026-08-23); nobody has measured the budget from prod. A cold run is ~690 requests (~12 min); steady state is the 34 listing pages plus new postings. The first 403/429 defers the rest of the run's hydration.

**Tech in Asia traps** (`techinasia`; live-verified 2026-10-15):

- **The edge refuses any client that names itself.** `www.techinasia.com` (AWS ELB) answers 403 to `freehire/0.1 (+https://freehire.me)`, to `Mozilla/5.0 (compatible; freehire/0.1)` and to a bare `Mozilla/5.0` — `robots.txt` included — and 200 to a full desktop Chrome string, so the provider is a `browserUASource`. `robots.txt` disallows nothing under `/jobs`. The static bundle host and Algolia answer any UA.
- **The listing is the public Algolia index `job_postings`, and the key lives in the app bundle.** `/jobs/search` names `static.techinasia.com/assets/v5/app.<8 hex>.js` (~2.5 MB), whose config carries `algolia:{apiKey:"…",appId:"219WX3MPV4"}`. Both rotate with deploys, so they are read on every run. The key answers with no Referer; it refuses `/settings` (403), so nothing about the index's configuration is readable.
- **One hit is the whole posting, body included** — 274 postings in one 1,000-hit page, `exhaustiveNbHits: true`, 250 in Indonesia. A run is three requests, no hydration. The walk still pages to `nbPages` and FAILS unless the distinct ids read equal `nbHits`, which is what earns `fullCatalog` (the provider is swept by source).
- **Not an IT board.** 37 employers; by the index's own `position`, ~140 of 274 are software/IT (Software Engineer 34, QA & Testing 23, Web Development 22, IT Engineer 20, Enterprise Software & Systems 10, Mobile 7, DevOps 2) and the rest marketing, sales, teaching. The catalogue filter judges each title; seven unambiguous positions set `IsTechHint`. `Data & Analytics` and `Hardware & Embedded Systems` do not — they held a newsroom "Researcher" and a "Pilot Drone". One outsourcing firm (SIGMATECH) is a third of the board.
- **The salary fields are money, not flags** (a third party read them as flags on 2026-09-23): 161 of 274 carry a real range (`7500000`-`8000000` IDR, `8000`-`11000` SGD for a senior PM), 113 an all-zero range meaning "not stated". No period is stated; the site's own salary copy and every observed amount are monthly, so the period is `month`.
- **`external_link` is set on 18 of 274, and not all of them are the posting's own.** Darwinbox, Trakstar and Airtable links are per-posting; one noteforms.com form serves several roles and `forms.gle` is a redirector. A link more than one posting shares, or one through a shortener, keeps the Tech in Asia page instead — a shared URL would read as one posting to every URL consumer.
- **`published_at` is a zone-less `2006-01-02 15:04:05`**, read as UTC. A remote posting may carry `city: null`; it is stored as "Remote" with no country.

**Kalibrr traps** (`kalibrr`; live-verified 2026-10-15):

- **The board is a COUNTRY MARKET, always read through the "IT and Software" job function.** `GET /kjs/job_board/search?country=<Country>&function=IT and Software&limit=&offset=` is the site's own search, keyless, any UA; `robots.txt` disallows only `/root` and `/candidate/profile`. Indonesia held 1,553 postings (249 IT), the Philippines 812 (190 IT). Function and country names are case-sensitive and spelled as the platform spells them.
- **An unknown country or function is IGNORED, not refused.** `country=indonesia`, `country=Thailand` or `function=it-and-software` answer the default market (842 rows, mostly Philippine, every function) with HTTP 200. So every row is checked against the board's country (`google_location.address_components.country`) and the function, and one row outside the slice fails the crawl. Without a `country` the search answers that default mix rather than everything; it is not a board.
- **Offset paging under the default relevance sort repeats and skips rows**: Indonesia paged by 500 came back as 1,420 distinct of 1,553, while one `limit=2000` request returned all 1,553 distinct. A board is therefore ONE request, checked against `count` (exact) and for duplicates, and a board over `kalibrrLimit` fails rather than paging — which is what earns `fullBoardListing`.
- **A row is the whole posting**: `description` + `qualifications` are HTML, so there is no hydration. The canonical page is `/c/<company code>/jobs/<id>/<slug>` (the page's own `rel=canonical`).
- **`apply_redirect_url` is rare and not always the posting's own**: 1 of 249 Indonesian IT rows (BCA's career site), 4 of 190 Philippine ones, all four the same `forms.gle` form. A redirector or a link shared by several postings keeps the Kalibrr page.
- **A hidden salary still carries its figures**: `salary_shown: false` rows can hold `20000000` IDR/month. Only shown ones are published; `salary_interval` is `month` on almost all, with `day`/`hour`/`year` rare.
- **Both `is_work_from_home` and `is_hybrid` false is silence, not on-site**, and is left to the description. `work_experience` (100-500) and `education_level` (200-550) are undocumented codes and are not mapped.
- **The function is the platform's, not a guarantee**: a few rows filed under IT are not IT ("Sales Associate", "Audio Visual Equipment Operator II"); `IsTechHint` follows the dedicated-category-board rule all the same. One transient 502 was seen in ~40 requests; a failed board is simply retried next run.

**WUZZUF traps** (`wuzzuf`; live-verified 2026-10-18, transport re-measured 2026-10-21):

- **Everything is behind Cloudflare now, so the crawl reads the site's search pages in a browser.** Until 2026-10-20 the site was challenged but `api.wuzzuf.net` (an AWS load balancer serving the JSON:API the front end calls) was open, and the adapter used it. From that afternoon the API answers the same "Just a moment..." 403 to every plain request, direct or through the proxy, Chrome fingerprint or not. The Scrapling tier's browser solves the challenge from the crawl host's own IP (over the proxy it timed out), so `wuzzuf` is a `scraplingProviders` row in `browser` mode. **The search pages are rate-limited**: the first run read nine pages ~6 s apart and the tenth answered 429, so the row paces them (`wuzzufPageInterval`, one per 12 s, ~6 minutes a run).
- **A search page carries everything; there is no detail fetch.** `https://wuzzuf.net/search/jobs/?filters[roles][0]=<role>&start=<page>` embeds `Wuzzuf.initialStoreState`, the store its front end boots from: `jobSearch.sets[<page path>]` holds the search (`totalResultsCount`, `resultsOrder`, and a `resultsSet` row per id whose `computedFields[company_name]` is the employer) and `entities.job.collection` the full JSON:API job document of every row — the same shape `GET /api/job` returned. `start` is a PAGE index, not a row offset, and a page is 15 rows (`wuzzufPageSize`, the site's, not ours): on 2026-10-21 the IT role was 437 postings, `start=29` showed 436-437 and `start=30` nothing. A row listed without its document yields an `unreadableDetail` marker; a document whose status is not `active` is gone.
- **The board is a job ROLE.** On 2026-10-18 the whole active catalogue was 5,266 postings and the IT role 436. **An unknown filter KEY is ignored** (`categories`, `workRoles`, `job_category` all answered the full 5,266 through the API), so every job document is checked against the board's role and one outside it fails the crawl. An unknown role VALUE matches nothing, so a misspelled board fails as "matches no postings". The browse pages (`/a/IT-Software-Development-Jobs-in-Egypt`) are NOT the role: that one answered 3,343 postings, accountants included.
- **The employer is padded with spaces on some rows and `""` when the employer hid it** (51 of 436 IT postings) — those name no company and are not yielded.
- **`postedAt` is zone-less Cairo wall time** (`10/18/2026 20:19:47` came back at 18:00 UTC), read in `Africa/Cairo` — Egypt observes DST, so a fixed offset would be wrong half the year; hence `time/tzdata`.
- **The page is `https://wuzzuf.net/` + `uri`**, and `uri` is not always `jobs/p/<slug>`: internships are `internship/<slug>` and Saudi postings `saudi/jobs/p/<slug>`.
- **Geography is structured and not all Egyptian**: of the 385 named IT postings, 367 Egypt, 5 Saudi Arabia, 2 UAE, 2 Cyprus, 2 UK and one each in Canada, Switzerland, India, Jordan, Japan and the US. The country is `location.country.code` through `NormalizeCountry`; the place is `area, city, country`.
- **Structured fields that say nothing are not mapped**: 14 of 436 postings name several work types at once (`full_time, part_time, freelance_project`), so an employment type is taken only from a single one. Career level "Experienced" spans middle and senior and "Manager"/"Senior Management" a management track, so only "Student" (intern) and "Entry Level" (junior) set a seniority. `hideSalary` withholds a salary (46 of 436 shown, all "Per Month"); an unpaid internship carries `isPaid: false` and a 0-0 range.
- **A posting can carry several roles.** A second role board would store a posting filed under both twice, under two board-namespaced ids; the duplicate markers collapse them, but the IT role is the only board today.

**ApplicantStack traps** (`applicantstack`; live-verified 2026-10-20 from a residential IP, not yet from the crawl host):

- **A board is the tenant subdomain, lower-cased.** `<tenant>.applicantstack.com/x/openings` is the listing and `/x/detail/<id>` a posting; hosts are case-insensitive and the same tenant is linked as both `SmartAutoCare` and `smartautocare`. A tenant on its own domain (`careers.<employer>.com/x/detail/<slug>`) is the same application, but no URL rule can name its board, so `atsboard` declines it. `www`, `www2` and `public` are the vendor's own hosts.
- **The listing pages by `?pge=N`, 100 rows a page, and its pager states the whole count** ("Showing 1 - 100 of 282"). The walk fails unless the distinct postings equal that total, which is what earns `fullBoardListing`. A board with no openings renders the table (`#data-table`) with no rows and no pager; a page past the end answers "Invalid page number" with no table; an unknown tenant 302s to `www.applicantstack.com/job-not-found/`, a 200 marketing page with no table. Only the first is read as empty — a page without the table fails the crawl.
- **The listing's columns are the tenant's choice** (Location, Department, Salary Range, County - Territory …), so only the posting link and its title are read there. The posting page's ld+json carries the body, `datePosted` and a structured address; its `title` is a separately configured field that is often null, so the listing's title (what the page itself shows) is used. A gone posting answers 404.
- **`robots.txt` declares `Crawl-delay: 5` and the adapter honours it per board**: every tenant is its own host, so one board's requests are spaced 5 s apart while other boards run alongside. 88 posting pages at 8-way concurrency answered no refusal, so the delay is courtesy, not a measured limit. Hydrating: steady state is one listing request per 100 postings plus a page per new posting; a 282-posting board's first crawl is ~24 minutes.
- **Not an IT platform.** Tenants are mostly small US employers — home care, restaurants, clinics, field services; the catalogue filter judges each title.

**Empregos.com.br traps** (`empregos`; live-verified 2026-10-24 from the fsn host, and a full run of the adapter from a residential IP):

- **The catalogue is ~90k postings, not the 231k the page headline says.** The listing's "231.197 vagas" is the API's `sum` — openings, a posting may offer several — while `total` (the postings) stood at 90,691-90,731. It is a general board: ~1% "Informática/TI", the rest everything else, and the catalogue filter judges each title. Every posting's `source` is `empregoscombr`.
- **The sitemap is gone and the listing pages are capped.** `robots.txt` still names `sitemap-index.xml`, which answers 404 (as do `sitemap.xml` and the obvious variants). The SSR listing `/vagas/N` serves 20 postings a page and stops at page 2,500 — 50,000 results, short of the catalogue — and `/buscar-vagas*` is disallowed. robots.txt otherwise allows everything for `*`; `gatewayb2c.empregos.com.br` has no robots.txt (404).
- **The crawl reads the search API the pages themselves call** — `POST gatewayb2c.empregos.com.br/searchjob/v2/elastic/search` with `{keyword:"", filters, page, size, order, distance}` (the endpoint is named in the page's public Nuxt config; `keyword` must be a string). Each result is the whole posting, body included, and the body is the same text the posting page's JSON-LD carries (compared on a sample; the page has nothing richer), so **no posting page is ever requested** and there is no per-posting detail cost to bound. The default client UA is served; curl's default UA drew the GoCache "Acesso Bloqueado" 403.
- **Paging that API loses postings, so it is not paged.** `page × size` is capped at 50,000 (404 past it) and `order` is ignored; worse, consecutive pages overlap — walking a 29,027-posting slice in pages of 10,000 returned 28,636 distinct ids, the SAME 28,636 on a second walk, and in pages of 1,000 only 26,988. One answer never repeats an id. So the catalogue is cut into slices that each arrive in ONE answer: by level (facet 10 — its 21 values add up exactly to the total, which the cut checks), then a level too large by the 27 federative units (facet 1, which takes lowercase codes: `sp` matches, `SP` matches nothing), then a cell still too large by workplace type (facet 17). Filters of different facets AND, two values of one facet OR. An answer carries at most `empregosLeafMax` (15,000, ~35 MB uncompressed, ~12 s — the largest cell measured 10,356); a slice that no cut brings under it fails the crawl. A full run is ~103 requests, ~2 minutes.
- **Not `fullCatalog`, deliberately.** A posting with no location (~0.1-0.3%: 269 of 90,731 in the live run, spread across levels) is in no state, so the state cut cannot reach it and nothing can prove it was read. The run logs the shortfall and carries on; the sweep stays company-scoped, and `cmd/liveness` probes the stale tail (`probeDespiteRegistered`): a removed posting redirects to `/404`, which answers 404.
- **A hidden employer is skipped.** `Confidencial` and `Empresa confidencial` (any case) are the board's label for an employer it does not name — 6,858 of the live run's 90,462 postings — and, as on OCC, such a posting has nobody to file it under.
- **What is structured, and what is not.** Location is `city`/`stateAbbreviation`/`country` per entry (a posting may list several; `country` is `br` on all of them, so it is a structured `Countries`). `workplaceTypes` (Presencial/Remoto/Híbrido) is the work mode. `contractForm` is `A Combinar` (to be agreed) on 99.96% of postings, so an employment type is set only for `Estágio` and the PJ/autonomous forms. **Salary is not published**: `salary.min`/`max` carry no currency or period field, 0 stands for absent, and over half are empty — the posting page's JSON-LD carries no `baseSalary` either. `createdAt` has no offset; it is Brasília time (UTC-3, the hourly peak is business hours there and the newest stamps run a few minutes ahead of BRT's clock). The description is HTML on most postings and plain text with HTML entities on the rest.
- **The posting URL is `/vaga/<referenceId>`** — the slug the site appends is optional (200 without it), so the stored URL cannot drift with a retitled posting.
- **New postings are written newest first, `empregosMaxNewPerRun` (5,000) a run, after the refreshes.** Every run lists the whole catalogue whatever it holds, so the bound is on database work, measured from the crawl host on 2026-10-24. The first prod run wrote 14,119 new postings at ~7 a second before its 40-minute crawl budget (the 3,000 s run timeout less the 600 s sweep reserve) ran out — it was capped at 20,000, so the run failed, wrote none of its refreshes and recorded no board health. A stored posting's refresh (`TouchJob`, one UPDATE) measured ~190 a second at the pipeline's 8 connections, so refreshing the whole ~90k catalogue costs ~8 minutes a run; listing costs ~4. At 5,000 new writes (~12 minutes) a run uses about 60% of its budget. The refreshes are yielded first so that a run cut short loses new writes, not liveness. With ~10k postings created a day (the API's "today" filter), a run every 4-6 hours keeps up once the ~70k backlog is through.

**Civil Service Jobs traps** (`civilservicejobs`; live-verified 2026-10-23 from a residential IP and from the fsn host):

- **Every path answers an ALTCHA proof-of-work page first** ("Quick check needed", HTTP 200, `id="captcha-form"`). It is a computation, not a person check: `GET /csr/index.cgi?ProtectCaptcha=1` returns `{algorithm, challenge, maxnumber, salt, signature}`, the answer is the number in `[0, maxnumber]` whose `SHA-512(salt + number)` equals `challenge` (`maxnumber` 300000, a fraction of a second), and it is POSTed back as `altcha` = base64 JSON together with the page's `originalRequestToken`. The verdict is the `protectVerification` cookie — bound to the client IP, valid ~24h — so the adapter runs on a cookie-jar client and solves once per run. `robots.txt` allows everything except `JoobleBot`.
- **The pages are Windows-1252 and declare no charset.** `£` is the byte `0xA3`, quotes and dashes `0x91`-`0x97`; the shared HTML getters parse UTF-8 and turn all of them into U+FFFD. The adapter reads raw bodies (`TextGetter`, `FormTextPoster`) and transcodes before parsing.
- **The empty search is the whole catalogue, and it is complete.** The home page's quick-search form (`#ID_context_search_form`, its hidden fields read rather than restated) POSTed with empty `what`/`where` and `overseas=1` answered "1,412 Search results"; 57 pages of 25 yielded exactly 1,412 distinct postings. A posting published mid-walk shows as one extra (1,413 against a stated 1,412), so the walk fails only when it reads FEWER than the first page's total — which, with any page failing, is what earns `fullCatalog`.
- **The posting id is `jcode`, read from the title link's `aria-describedby` (`dref-<ref>-<jcode>`).** Every href on the site is a signed, session-bound `SID`; `/csr/jobs.cgi?jcode=<id>` is the stable address and is what is stored. A closed posting does not 404 there — it answers 200 with the sign-in page, which carries no `#id_common_page_title_h1`.
- **The origin is slow, so the run is bounded.** A posting page took ~4 s to answer; one limiter paces every request at 1/s (`csjRequestInterval`, unmeasured as a limit — no refusal or second challenge across the full walk) with four detail workers so latency does not set the pace. The first run faces the whole catalogue, which fits the 50-minute run timeout only while the origin stays fast, so a run hydrates at most `csjMaxNewPerRun` (600) postings and the rest wait for the next run unstored.
- **The card is the source of employer, place and pay.** Department (the employer), location and salary are read from the listing card; the posting page's location is a prose section kept in the body. A truncated card location ends with "... see the job advert for full location information", which is cut. Of 1,409 cards with a salary every bound was annual bar two (£475, £1,000), so a range is published as GBP per year only from £5,000 up.
- **"Working pattern" lists every schedule the role allows** ("Flexible working, Full-time, Job share, Part-time" on 22 of 38 sampled), so an employment type is set only when it names one. Contract type, grade and role type have no lossless vocabulary counterpart and stay in the body's fact list. Countries are a `FallbackCountries` `gb`: `overseas=1` includes embassy postings abroad.
- **Not an IT board.** 4 of 38 sampled postings were "Digital" by type of role; the catalogue is broader than IT and the filter judges each title.

**InfoJobs Brasil traps** (`infojobsbr`; live-verified 2026-10-24 from the fsn host, including one full listing pass of every board):

- **It is not `infojobs`.** That key is infojobs.net, the Spanish site — a different catalogue, stack and transport (the Scrapling browser tier). This one is www.infojobs.com.br and is keyed `infojobsbr` so the two can never be confused in the catalogue, the facet or a board row.
- **No sitemap, and every listing is capped.** robots.txt names none (`/sitemap.xml` redirects to the 404 page); the listings it allows are `/empregos-em-<state>.aspx`, 20 cards a page. Paging is the listing script's own pager, `GET /mf-publicarea/VacancyList/GetVacancyListFragment?url=<listing>?page=N` → `{eof, listFragmentHTML}`, and it answers **page 1 again to any request without the `ab_session_id` cookie** the listing page sets, hence a cookie-jar client (one session serves concurrent walks of different listings). A listing stops serving new results at ~10,000 (around page 490 it repeats one page), so a listing whose meta description states more than 9,000 is cut by its own filter options — category (a path), then level (`im`), contract (`tipocontrato`), hours (`wo`) — read from that page rather than restated. São Paulo needed category and level cuts. A slice with no result states no number in its meta description and shows recommendations; it is skipped.
- **A board is a federative unit, plus `todo-brasil`.** Every state listing serves the state's own postings FIRST (contiguous: measured on Roraima, Amapá, Tocantins and Acre), then the same ~1.9k padding — ~930 postings of other states and ~840 that name no place ("Todo Brasil"). A state board keeps only cards whose location ends in its UF and stops at the first page with none; `todo-brasil` walks Acre's listing (the shortest) to `eof` and keeps only the "Todo Brasil" cards. A full pass found 115,140 postings and no posting under two boards.
- **The run needs 4500 s.** All 28 boards, eight at a time through one limiter at 3 requests a second (~1,000 listing requests at ~3.2/s and then the whole pass drew no refusal; the origin is the same Azure stack as Pandapé, which 403s a burst at ~6-7/s), took 36.6 minutes to list — São Paulo alone 26 minutes for 60,139. That leaves no room for writes inside the default 3000 s run, so the provider is scheduled with `--timeout=4500s`, unsharded (each shard would carry its own limiter). Each board hydrates at most 80 new postings a run (`infojobsBRMaxNewPerBoard`): ≤ ~2.2k detail requests and ~5 minutes of writes at the ~7 new writes a second measured from the crawl host.
- **Pandapé duplicates are the pipeline's to drop, not the adapter's.** InfoJobs Brasil republishes its own ATS's career sites; on a 50-posting sample 27 were confirmed on a Pandapé tenant. The adapter is an `aggregator` and a `CoverageGated` source: the card names the employer, so a company the Pandapé crawl already covers is yielded body-less without its detail request and counted `ATSCovered` by the coverage gate; the rest are marked by the periodic aggregator-ATS dedup like every other aggregator's.
- **A hidden employer is dropped at the card.** "Empresa confidencial" (6 of the 50 sampled) is not a company, as on Catho and Empregos.com.br, and since the card says so the posting costs no request.
- **The posting page is the body; its JobPosting is optional.** A posting with a place carries a schema.org JobPosting (`datePosted` with no offset — Brasília time —, `hiringOrganization`, `jobLocation.address` with `addressCountry` BR); a "Todo Brasil" posting's page carries NONE, so the body is read from the page itself for every posting: the `white-space: pre-line` text, the fact paragraphs ("Tipo de contrato e Jornada", "Área Profissional") and the "Exigências"/"Valorizado"/"Habilidades" lists. Emoji arrive as `??` from the source in every rendering. UF codes are ISO country codes, so a place is written "City, UF, Brasil". A closed posting answers **200** with "Esta vaga não está mais disponível" and no header — not a 404 — so liveness cannot use the status, and closes go through the ordinary company-scoped sweep (not `fullCatalog`: nothing proves a walk complete). An UNSEEN posting whose page answers that notice (closed between listing and hydration) is not stored and counts as an answer, so a board whose new postings all closed does not fail.
- **Card facts.** Work mode is an icon-led picklist (Presencial / Híbrido / Home office). Salary is "A combinar" or "R$ 1.625,00 [a R$ 2.000,00]" with no period; the figures on 300 sampled São Paulo cards cluster at R$ 1.6k-3k, a monthly wage, so it is published as monthly BRL from R$ 1.000 up. Employment type comes from "Tipo de contrato e Jornada" ("Efetivo – CLT - Período Integral"): Estágio is an internship, PJ/Autônomo/Temporário/Cooperado a contract, CLT full or part time by its hours; Jovem Aprendiz, Trainee and Noturno map to nothing.
- **"Vagas semelhantes" groups hide postings the walk never sees.** A card may carry a collapsed group of similar postings, expanded by `POST /mf-publicarea/VacancyList/ListCollapsedVacancies`; on Acre 25 groups held 70 members and none of them appeared anywhere in the walk. Expanding every group would roughly double the run, so they are not read — a known gap.
- **A run that cannot finish all 28 boards loses only the boards it did not reach.** Each board is an independent fetch whose postings are written when it returns, and `cmd/ingest` starts the boards waited on longest first, so the next run picks up where this one stopped. The sweep stays company-scoped with the default 48 h window: a posting on a board the run never reached is not refreshed, but it is closed only if NO run reaches its board for 48 h — at a 6 h cadence that is eight consecutive runs, while rotation reaches every board within two. A company the run DID reach through another board does not change that: the close is by `last_seen_at` against the 48 h cutoff, not by this run's listing.
- **Cadence 6 h.** A run is ~37 minutes of listing plus at most ~17 of detail and writes, so four a day keep the hydration backlog moving (~2.2k new a run) without overlapping, and leave the 48 h window eight runs deep.
- **Onboarding** is 28 `cmd/add-board --provider=infojobsbr --board=<uf|todo-brasil> --company="InfoJobs Brasil" --apply` rows and `cmd/schedule-board --provider=infojobsbr --manage --heavy --timeout=4500s --cadence=6h --apply`.

**Catho traps** (`catho`; live-verified 2026-10-23 from the fsn host):

- **Only the posting pages refuse the project's bare User-Agent.** `freehire/0.1 (+https://freehire.me)` reads `robots.txt` and the sitemaps but gets HTTP 403 on every `/vagas/<slug>/<id>/` page, while `compatibleUserAgent` is served (so is a browser string, not needed). curl's default UA is refused even on `robots.txt`. `robots.txt` disallows `/buscar/vagas/` (the search) and allows the posting pages.
- **The sitemaps are the listing.** `sitemap-index.xml` names five `sitemap2/sitemap_vagas_<n>.xml` files (50,000 URLs each, the fifth 13,535): 213,535 distinct postings, ~6.3 MB per file, ~1.3 s each. Every `lastmod` is the regeneration day (the files are rebuilt daily, ~05:00 UTC), so it says nothing about a posting. Any file failing fails the crawl. The rest of the index lists search and profession pages.
- **The sitemap is NOT the whole catalogue, so no `fullCatalog`.** The home page claims "mais de 400 mil vagas" against 213,535 sitemap URLs, and nothing states a count the walk could be checked against; closes go through the ordinary company-scoped sweep.
- **The id is the identity; the slug is cosmetic.** `/vagas/x/38959057/` serves the same posting. A closed (or never-public) id answers **301 to the slug's search page** `/vagas/<slug>/`, never 404, so a page whose final URL lost the id is gone. The daily sitemap lags: 2 of 40 postings sampled from that morning's files had already closed by the evening.
- **Posting pages carry a schema.org JobPosting**, but two of its fields lie. `jobLocation.address.addressLocality` is the STATE's name ("Rio Grande do Sul"), so the city is read from the page header ("1 vaga - Eldorado do Sul", with a "+ N cidades" note cut); `addressRegion` is the UF. `baseSalary` is a coarse band that contradicts the figure the page shows (header "A partir de R$ 2.000,00" vs ld+json 2001-3000; "R$ 1.947" vs 2000-2000), so the salary is parsed from the header instead, monthly BRL. `datePosted` names a day as its last second (`T23:59:59Z`); the day is kept at its start. The ld+json `description` keeps the employer's line breaks, so it is the body's text, followed by the page's contract/hours facts and benefit list.
- **UF codes are ISO country codes.** "Porto Alegre, RS" parses as Serbia, "Serra, ES" as Spain, "Salvador, BA" as Bosnia, so the location is written "City, UF, Brasil" and the structured `addressCountry` ("Brasil") sets `Countries`, which overrides the parse; `FallbackCountries` `br` only when it is missing.
- **A hidden employer is dropped, after the page is bought.** "Empresa Confidencial" on 8 of 50 sampled postings and a name masked as asterisks ("****") on 2 more — ~20%. Neither is a company, and filing them would build one bogus employer holding tens of thousands of postings (the SEEK "Private Advertiser" rule). The sitemap names no employer, so this is only known after the detail request, and a dropped posting is never stored, so it stays unseen and is re-read on later runs; hydrating newest-id first keeps that re-reading behind every fresh posting rather than in front of it. The cost is real: once the backlog is stored, each run spends whatever budget the fresh postings leave on re-reading those pages. A persistent record of declined ids would remove it; nothing in the pipeline keeps one today.
- **The run is bounded.** A posting page is ~170 KB and took ~0.75 s from the crawl host; 52 probe pages at ~1/s drew no refusal. One limiter paces every request at 2/s (`cathoRequestInterval`, courtesy, not a measured limit) with three workers, and a run hydrates at most `cathoMaxNewPerRun` (3000, ~25 minutes) — the first ~70 runs work through the backlog. A live run of 15 hydrations took 11 s including the sitemaps. ~26k new ids reached the sitemap in the week to 2026-10-23.
- **`employmentType` is the Brazilian contract regime.** "Estágio" maps to internship; "Prestador de serviços (PJ)", "Temporário" and "Autônomo" to contract; "CLT (Efetivo)" (a permanent hire, hours unstated) and a two-regime value map to nothing.
- **Not an IT board.** Retail, logistics, sales and cleaning dominate; nothing sets `IsTechHint` and the catalogue filter judges each title.

**XING traps** (`xing`; live-verified 2026-10-25 from the crawl host, over 400 randomly sampled postings):

- **The sitemaps are the listing, and they are gzip FILES.** robots.txt disallows `/jobs/search` and `/graphql/`; `/jobs/sitemap.xml.gz` names eleven `<n>-sitemap.xml.gz` files of 50,000 URLs each — 519,661 distinct postings on 2026-10-24. They are served as `application/gzip` with no `Content-Encoding`, so the transport does not inflate them: the adapter reads them through `GetStream` and gunzips itself. The index also lists role/skill/city landing sitemaps, which are not postings. Plain HTTP passes from host2 under the project's own User-Agent; no Scrapling, no proxy.
- **Not `fullCatalog`.** Nothing states a total to check the walk against, and the sitemap lags: 3.4% of sampled URLs already answered **410 Gone**, whose page caches an `UnauthorizedJob` instead of a `VisibleJob`. Such a posting is not yielded; a stored one stays open until the sitemap drops it.
- **The posting is read from the page's Apollo cache**, the `window.crate=` assignment in `<script id="runtime-config">`. It is a JavaScript object literal, not JSON — `undefined` appears in value positions and is rewritten to `null`. The ld+json is NOT read: its `description` for a TemplateData posting is the sections concatenated with a literal `null` and bare headings, and its `baseSalary.unitText` is `YEAR` on every salary, hourly ones included. The cache has everything the ld+json has plus the fields below.
- **Two body shapes.** `HtmlDescription.content` (80%), or `TemplateData` (20%): a `genericDescription` and/or sections each with its own heading, kept as `<h3>`. The `companyDescription` section is the employer's standing blurb and is left out.
- **`remoteOptions`: FULL_REMOTE → remote, PARTLY_REMOTE → hybrid, NON_REMOTE → nothing.** NON_REMOTE is the form's default, not a statement: 17 of 321 NON_REMOTE postings offered home office in their own text, one titled "100% Remote deutschlandweit". Shares in the sample: NON_REMOTE 83%, PARTLY_REMOTE 17%, FULL_REMOTE 0.6%.
- **`salary.__typename` says whose figure it is.** `SalaryEstimate` (72%) is XING's guess for the role and is never stored; `SalaryRange`/`Salary` (8.5%) is the employer's ("Unternehmensangabe" on the page). XING states no period for it — "14 € – 18 €", "2.800 € – 3.400 €" and "65.000 € – 80.000 €" sit in the same field — so only a figure of at least 20,000 is stored, as yearly; nothing in DACH pays that per hour, day or month, and no full-time year pays less.
- **Country comes from `locations[].country.countryCode`** (DE 91%, CH 6%, AT 3% of places). It is not always right — a Belgian city came back `DE` — but it is right on every DACH place sampled, and the location string `City, Region` ("Werthenstein, Kanton Luzern") is what the dictionary reads for the city. A posting may name up to ten places.
- **`discipline` 1011 ("IT und Softwareentwicklung", 7.7%) sets `IsTechHint`**: its titles were developers, admins, SAP consultants, IT apprenticeships. The suffix after the dot in a cache id (`Discipline:1011.6cf3f7`) is a query hash, not part of the value.
- **~42% of postings are applied to on XING itself** (`JobXingApplication`/`JobXingCustomApplication`); the rest carry a `UrlApplication` to an ATS or a job ad network.
