They are NOT filtered: the sitemap names no employer, so nothing is known before the page is bought, and the pipeline's aggregator-coverage gate already discards a posting whose employer we crawl from its own board — measured, 105 of 387 sampled postings (27%; arbeitsagentur 47, join 33, personio 8, successfactors 8). The rest, ATS-applied ones included, come from boards we do not crawl.
- **That gate is why the run has two lanes.** A discarded posting is never stored, so it stays unseen and is bought again every run. Newest-first alone would stall: once the discarded postings above the backlog outnumbered the budget (~8,000 hydrations in), no older posting would ever be reached. So 1,200 of a run's 2,000 detail requests take the newest unseen ids (~7,000 new ids reach the sitemap a day) and 800 take a window of the rest that moves every hour. One limiter paces every request at 1/s; a page is ~360 KB and answers in ~0.5 s.
- **Not an IT board.** Production, healthcare, engineering and sales dominate; recruiters post one job per town (one agency listed the same tax role under fifteen consecutive ids, one village each).

**Taleo Business Edition traps** (`taleobe`; live-verified 2026-10-20 from a residential IP, not yet from the crawl host):

- **It is NOT Taleo Enterprise.** `taleo` crawls `<tenant>.taleo.net/careersection/<n>` through a session-bound search POST; `taleobe` crawls `<pod>.tbe.taleo.net/<pod><nn>/ats/careers/…?org=<ORG>&cws=<n>`, a different product with nothing in common but the name. Neither adapter can read the other's boards.
- **The board is `<ORG>:<cws>`, never the pod.** The org code names the employer and `cws` one of its career sites (an org may run several; a number it does not have is gone). Org codes are case-insensitive at the platform and stored upper-case. The pod (`phf02`, `tre01`, …) is left out because orgs move: the dispatcher (`tbe.taleo.net/dispatcher/servlet/DispatcherServlet?org=…&act=redirectOrg&redirectUrl=…`) resolves an org to its current pod from any TBE host, and a Common Crawl link on `chp02` already answers from `phg02`.
- **One RSS request per board, body inline.** `/ats/servlet/Rss?org=&cws=` lists the whole career site with `taleo:html-description`, `taleo:reqId`, a display `taleo:location` and (only when the employer attached an address) city/state/country parts. The feed matched the HTML site's "Open Positions" total on every board checked, up to 365 of 365, which is what earns `fullBoardListing`. The largest feed measured is 7 MB raw but ~440 KB gzipped (~2 s), well inside the standard client timeout.
- **"Gone" is a redirect, never a status**: an unknown org lands on `/dispatcher/login.jsp`, a deactivated org (`INACTIVE…` in the redirect chain) on `tbe.taleo.net/errorpages/page_not_found_404.html.en` under HTTP 200, and an unknown `cws` on `rssErrMsg.jsp`. All three wrap `ErrBoardGone`. An org whose harvested `cws` is gone may still be live on another: `DispatcherServlet?act=redirectCws&org=<ORG>` (no `cws`) redirects to its default career site.
- **`taleo:locationState` is not consistent** (`Louisiana` on one org, `US-DC` on another), so only the country code is mapped as a structured fact; the location string is the display label (`Remote, United States`, `Nairobi, Kenya`).

**HR-Manager traps** (`hrmanager`; live-verified 2026-10-19 from a residential IP and the prod host):

- **A board is the customer ALIAS, not the cid every posting URL carries.** A posting is `candidate.hr-manager.net/ApplicationInit.aspx?cid=316&ProjectId=…`, but the public job-portal API is `GET api.hr-manager.net/jobportal.svc/<alias>/positionlist/json/` and answers a numeric cid with HTTP 400 `"Value cannot be null. Parameter name: connectionString"` — the same answer an unknown alias gets. The alias is case-insensitive and echoed lower-case as `CustomerAlias`. To map a cid to its alias, read `candidate.hr-manager.net/vacancies/list.aspx?cid=<cid>`: the page embeds the API's own JSON, `"CustomerAlias":"<alias>"`. Do NOT read the alias out of the page's other `customer=` links — on energinet's page one of them was `customer=kimdemo`, a template leftover. A customer with no open posting embeds no alias at all (18 of 171 cids harvested from jobnet's outbound links).
- **`atsboard` recognises only the alias-carrying URLs**: `vacancies/list.aspx?customer=<alias>`, `Agent/Subscription.aspx?customer=<alias>` and the API's `JobPortal.svc/<alias>/…` (which a customer's own careers page calls). A cid-only posting link is declined, since no network-free rule maps it.
- **`incads=1` inlines each posting's advertisement HTML**, so the listing is the whole posting and there is no detail request. Without `take` the API answers the first 25 rows; `take=2000` is honoured in one response (`skip` pages too). A board is ONE request; a customer over 2,000 fails rather than paging. The largest customer, `mercuryeng` (663 postings), is a 12 MB answer that took 5 s from the prod host and 25 s from a residential line, hence `NewLongTimeoutClient`.
- **`PositionCountSearch` can count a posting no public view serves.** `statensrekrutteringsloesning_tr` (the Danish state's shared recruitment) counted 414 and served 413 — through the JSON at any take, through the RSS variant and on the platform's own HTML listing alike; paged with `take=1`, the server answers an empty page at the hidden row's position. The other 300 customers measured served exactly their count. So the walk tolerates a shortfall of at most `hrManagerMaxHidden` (2) and fails beyond it; the rows must still be distinct and equal the response's own `PositionCountList`.
- **The list carries one `OpenApplication` row per customer that runs an unsolicited-application intake** (`ProjectType`); it is not a posting and is skipped. Every other row was `RecruitmentProject`, and every row measured carried exactly one advertisement.
- **Location is free text, the country rarely stated.** `WorkPlace` ("Fredericia / Ballerup") is the posting's own; the customer's location picklist and the department's city are the fallbacks. `Department.Country` is filled on some customers ("Danmark") and empty on most, and nothing else states a country, so none is guessed. Timestamps are .NET `/Date(<ms>+0200)/`; `Published` is the posting date.
- **A board's company is the catalogue's, and one customer is many employers.** `statensrekrutteringsloesning_tr` lists postings for 107 state agencies (Skattestyrelsen, Statens IT, Forsvarets Efterretningstjeneste…), named only in `Department.Name`; the adapter does not read that as the employer, because on other customers the department is a store ("REMA 1000 Gram") or the customer itself.
- **Not an IT platform.** Customers include retail (REMA 1000, Flying Tiger), municipalities and regions; the catalogue filter judges each title. Nothing sets `IsTechHint`.

**Tenstreet traps** (`tenstreet`; live-verified 2026-10-20 from a residential IP):

- **The board is the carrier's numeric `company_id`**, which every posting link carries (`pulse.tenstreet.com/share_app_show_active_jobs.php?active_job_id=…&company_id=774&portal_user_id=1`). The listing is the carrier's Pulse profile, `share_app_index.php?company_id=<board>`: one unpaginated server-rendered page (357 postings for the largest carrier sampled) whose cards link every open posting, some carded twice. An unknown id answers HTTP 200 with an empty profile — no carrier name — which the adapter reports as `ErrBoardGone`. `portal.tenstreet.com/share_app_index.php`, which the posting page's back button names, is a 404. The carriers' other apply host, `intelliapp.driverapponline.com/c/<slug>` (2 of 40 sampled links), names no company id, sits behind a Cloudflare 429, and is not recognised.
- **The posting is a JSON array in an inline script** (`var active_job_array = [...]`), one row per hiring location, each repeating title, description and requirements — a nationwide posting is ~1.8 MB. **`portal_user_id=1` is load-bearing**: without it, and for a posting asked for under another carrier's id or closed, the page answers `active_job_array = null` under HTTP 200. A null page is deferred, never stored.
- **Some postings have no body at all** (`job_description` and `job_requirements` both null — 4 of Dupré Logistics' 64, one an "EDI Integration Analyst"). That is the platform's answer, so they are yielded body-less rather than deferred forever. `job_requirements` is otherwise often an empty `<strong>&nbsp;</strong>` placeholder and is dropped; `job_pay` was null on every posting sampled.
- **Geography is US states, stated structurally**: a row is `select_states` (state code in `value`) or `zip_radius` (state in `state`, a ZIP prefix in `value`), and the page plots every row on a US map as `US-<state>`. `Countries` is therefore `us`, and `Location` lists the distinct codes plus `US` — the bare codes alone would be misread, since `IN`, `GA`, `DE`, `IL`, `AR` also spell countries. No posted date, work mode or employment type is published.
- **Slow and uneven, never refused**: 357 posting pages at four in flight averaged ~2.2 req/s with no refusal, but 7 took over 10 s and one 78 s. Hence `NewLongTimeoutClient`, four detail workers and a run-wide pacer at ~2 req/s. A HydratingSource, so steady state is one listing request per board.
- **Not an IT platform**: almost entirely trucking carriers (drivers, mechanics, dispatch). Nothing sets `IsTechHint`.

**IdealTraits traps** (`idealtraits`; live-verified 2026-10-20 from a residential IP):

- **The board is the employer's numeric business id, and a posting link does not carry it.** The career site is `app.idealtraits.com/<slug>/<base64 of the id>/careers` (`MTE2MjI5Mg==` is `1162292`); the slug is decoration and a wrong one 301s to the right one, an unknown id answers 403 `Access denied`. A posting link (`/career/<slug>/<posting id>CPG`) names only the slug, so `atsboard` recognises the career-site URL and declines the posting link; `boardresolve` gets from a posting to its board through the career-site link every posting page carries (39 of 40 sampled posting links resolve that way, one of them through a 301 to another posting of the same employer; the 40th is a closed posting answering 410, which `boardresolve` reads as a failed fetch).
- **The listing is the career site's own pagination XHR.** The same URL with `X-Requested-With: XMLHttpRequest` answers JSON `{"joblist": <cards' HTML>, "jobcount": <total>}` for any slug and without the redirect; past the end it answers an empty `joblist`, where the plain HTML page 404s. All 39 sampled boards (1-5 postings) fit one page, so a second page carrying cards has never been observed — the walk reads until it holds `jobcount` postings and fails the crawl otherwise, which is what earns `fullBoardListing`. The 39 boards' 77 cards matched their `jobcount` exactly.
- **The posting page's ld+json `hiringOrganization.name` is the slug** (`Comma-Insurance`), so the employer comes from the catalogue. Its body joins the benefits picklist with `###` (`Paid Time Off (PTO)###Health Insurance###`); the adapter turns each run into a list. The work arrangement is a page field outside the ld+json, `#remote-type`: `No` (onsite), `Hybrid Remote`, `Fully Remote` — an office address with `Fully Remote` is common and is what the employer stated.
- **One host, metered per IP, with a long penalty.** 4 req/s was served for ~120 requests and then every request got 429 with `Retry-After` ~240s; 1.5 req/s held for 200 requests with no refusal. Listing and posting requests share one 1.5 req/s limiter (`pacedIdealTraitsGetter`), and the adapter hydrates only unseen postings.
- **Not an IT platform.** The customers are almost entirely US insurance agencies (Farmers, Allstate, State Farm agents); the catalogue filter judges each title.

**Emply traps** (`emply`; live-verified 2026-10-19 from a residential IP and fsn):

- **One keyless listing is the whole crawl.** `POST https://<host>/api/integration/vacancy/get-page` with `{"count","offset","filters":[]}` answers `{"vacancies":[...],"count":<total>}`, and each vacancy carries its full HTML body in every language it is written in — no detail request. **`filters` is required**: without it the endpoint answers 500. The career site's own script also sends a `sectionId`; it is not needed, and omitting it only adds the talent-pool entry, which the adapter drops anyway.
- **The board is a slug OR a host.** Most sites are `<slug>.career.emply.com`, but a large share run on the customer's own domain (`midtjob.dk` — Region Midtjylland, the biggest at 305 — `job.frederiksberg.dk`, `jobs.bdo.dk`) with the same endpoint. `emplyHost` tells them apart by the dot and `BoardDedupeKey` folds `<slug>` and `<slug>.career.emply.com` into one target. Only the `career.emply.com` form is recognised by `atsboard`; a customer domain is a vanity host. `*.emply.com` without `career.` and `*.emply.net` are other Emply products (an HR suite, an ADFS login), not career sites.
- **A gone tenant redirects to `https://emply.com/`** (302, also for the POST), so it surfaces as a JSON decode error on the marketing page, not as a status. Old tenants are often renamed with a `-v2`/`-1` suffix rather than reused; the bare name then 500s or redirects.
- **`talentPool: true` is the standing "unsolicited application" entry**, not a vacancy. Some tenants publish an open application as an ordinary vacancy instead; nothing structured tells it apart.
- **`languageKey` is the tenant's language setting, not the text's.** `init` labels Danish postings `en-GB`. The adapter prefers an `en-*` translation and otherwise the first (primary) one, and the URL's trailing `/<lang>` opens the page in the language read. The ad page is keyed by `shortId` alone — any slug resolves.
- **Facts are tenant-defined.** Employment type, workplace, contact and so on live in `factDatas` under titles each tenant names itself (`Beskæftigelsestype`, `Stillingstype`, ` Position type`), so none is mapped.
- **`fullBoardListing`**: the walk pages by offset to `count` and fails unless the distinct ids equal it. The endpoint honours large page sizes (305 in one response), so paging is a safeguard, not a limit.

**HCS Hiring traps** (`hcshiring`; symplr, formerly HealthcareSource; live-verified 2026-10-20 from a residential IP and the prod crawl host):

- **The board is the tenant subdomain of `<tenant>.hcshiring.com`.** The career center is an Ember app over a keyless REST API on the same host: `GET /api/jobs?page=N` (1-based) answers `{"jobs":[...],"meta":{"totalJobs","totalPages"}}` and `GET /api/jobDescriptions?id=<id>` carries the HTML body. The app calls both under a `/<build-hash>/api` prefix taken from the page; the bare `/api` answers identically and does not move with a release. Ember pluralises and camelises the model name, so the detail path is `jobDescriptions`, not `job-descriptions` (that one 404s).
- **Ten rows a page, fixed.** No parameter (`perPage`, `per_page`, `limit`, `pageSize`) widens it, so a 259-posting tenant is 26 listing requests. The listing carries everything but the body, which is why the adapter hydrates (`HydratingSource`) — the body is fetched only for a posting the catalogue does not hold. A failed body read ingests the posting list-only (the listing is authoritative for its existence); a 404 drops it. 8 concurrent body requests drew no throttling.
- **`fullBoardListing`**: the walk reads to `totalPages` and fails unless the distinct ids equal `totalJobs`; any page failing fails the crawl. The order was stable across two full walks of the largest tenant measured.
- **An unknown tenant answers 404** with an HTML "The account does not exist." page. The shared client does not hand the body back, so it is reported as a plain listing failure rather than `ErrBoardGone`.
- **US only.** The app's own JobPosting markup states `addressCountry: "US"` and `currency: "USD"` for every posting, so `Countries` is `us` and a salary is USD. The location is built as `City, ST ZIP`: without the ZIP the location dictionary reads `Hershey, PA` as Panama.
- **The structured compensation and `googleEmploymentType` fields are declared but empty** — null on all 845 postings across 31 tenants. What tenants fill in are `publicFieldValues`, named by each tenant. Two are read: the field mapped to the platform's standard `Status` slot (only a lone "Full-Time"/"Part-Time"; "PRN", "Per Diem" and combined values are left to the parser), and a field named `Salary`, whose value the platform renders as `38.50 - 55.71 Hourly` / `110000.00 Annually` (195 of 845; "Weekly" has no `salary_period` and is dropped).
- **The vendor's other hosts are other products**: `<tenant>.hctsportals.com` (behind a captcha) and `pm.healthcaresource.com/CS/<tenant>` (the older Position Manager) share no API with this one and are not recognised by `atsboard`.

**AppOne traps** (`appone`, Paychex; live-verified 2026-10-20 from a residential IP):

- **The board is the job-portal slug, and a posting URL does not carry it.** `jobs.appone.com/<slug>` is the portal; `GET jobs.appone.com/api/portal/v1/companyjobposts/<slug>` lists it (title, location, job type, `workplaceType`, a structured `salary`, `datePosted`) and names the employer. A posting is `apply.appone.com/job/<24-hex id>`, whose record `GET apply.appone.com/api/apply/v2/jobposting/<id>` carries the plain-text `description` and the portal as `jobPortalUrl` — which is the ONLY place the portal is named. So `atsboard` recognises the portal URL and declines the posting URL, and `cmd/harvest-boards appone` resolves a posting-URL seed with that one request (`seedResolver`). 2 of 40 sampled postings had an empty `jobPortalUrl` and belong to no board at all. Slugs are case-insensitive; the stored board is the platform's own spelling.
- **The listing stops at 100 postings** and ignores every paging parameter tried (`page`, `pageNumber`, `pageSize`, `skip`, `offset`); one sampled portal held exactly 100. That is why `appone` does not claim `fullBoardListing`.
- **The edge answers 403, never 429, when it judges a client too fast** — a bare `Forbidden` from Azure Front Door, on both hosts. ~100 requests back to back turned into 38 refusals in 40; at one request a second refusals still came in bursts a few minutes in, and at one every two seconds too (0-14 per 20 over seven minutes), so no measured pace removes them. The run is paced at 1 req/s across all boards (`pacedAppOneGetter`), the adapter retries a 403 twice with a doubling pause, and a detail still refused is an `Unreadable` marker that the next run asks for again. Measured: a cold run over six portals (one of them at the 100 cap) left 0 of 100, 5 of 51, 5 of 12, 3 of 36, 6 of 18 and 1 of 2 details unreadable; a later run over six small portals, 2 of 42.
- **The legacy AppOne product is not this one and is not crawled.** `www.appone.com/MainInfoReq.asp?R_ID=…&SearchScreenID=…` (also served as `recruiting.myapps.paychex.com/appone/…`) is a classic ASP site: its search page is an ASP.NET postback keyed by a `ServerVar` (`<company>.appone.com`) that the posting URL does not carry, nothing public lists by `SearchScreenID`, and the "other openings" block on a posting page is capped at five.

**Feishu Jobs traps** (`feishujobs`; live-verified 2026-10-15):

- **A board is a tenant's careers HOST**: `jobs.bytedance.com`, or a customer's `<tenant>.jobs.feishu.cn` (MiniMax is `vrfi1sk8a0`). Every tenant's site calls the same keyless `POST /api/v1/search/job/posts`, and a row is the whole posting — `description` and `requirement` as plain text — so there is no hydration. `robots.txt` disallows only `/referral` on ByteDance; the tenant host has none.
- **The edge filters on the OPERATING SYSTEM in the User-Agent.** Both hosts answered 405 with an empty body to our own string, to `browserUserAgent` (Linux), to Windows Chrome and to Go's default, and 200 to `Mozilla/5.0 (Macintosh; Intel Mac OS X 10_15_7)` alone — hence `macBrowserUserAgent`. No cookie, token or Referer is needed.
- **The count stops at 10,000.** ByteDance's unfiltered search answers `count: 10000` over ~20k postings (11,965 experienced + 8,003 campus by category sum); a filtered count below that is exact (R&D experienced walked to 5,088 distinct of 5,088). A count at the window fails the board.
- **A search with no recruitment filter answers EXPERIENCED postings only.** Campus (`recruitment_id_list: ["2"]` — 201 new graduates, 202 interns) has to be asked for; `1` is experienced. Both are real postings and both are read, one walk each.
- **The crawl is scoped to the tenant's `R&D` (研发) category, because `classify` cannot read Chinese titles.** Over the first 100 experienced ByteDance titles of each category it resolved a category for 7 R&D titles (13 Corporate Function, 8 Design, 4 Marketing, 2 Product, 1 Operations, 1 Sales), `IsTech` for 3 R&D and 1 Product, and `ConfirmedNonTech` for NONE in any category — the ingest gate would admit everything and `is_tech` would stay unknown, leaving the rows out of search and out of enrichment. Over all 5,088 experienced R&D titles: category 410, `IsTech` 249, `ConfirmedNonTech` 4 (data-centre mechanical/electrical engineers). R&D is the platform's own technical bucket, so it sets `IsTechHint`. Of ~20k ByteDance postings, 9,541 are R&D (5,088 experienced, 4,453 campus).
- **The category tree has no fixed depth and its ids are per tenant.** ByteDance's R&D is a top-level node with disciplines under it; MiniMax's sits under an industry root (`互联网 / 电子 / 网游`) with a third level. So R&D is found by NAME in the tenant's `GET /api/v1/config/job/filters/2`, and a row belongs when its category or an ancestor is that node. The search IGNORING a filter would answer a larger mixed slice, so one row outside the asked-for category or hire type fails the board.
- **Geography is English and structured, the dictionary is not.** `location.Parse` reads no Chinese city name (北京, 上海 resolve nothing) and `NormalizeCountry` does not know "Chinese Mainland" or "Hong Kong, China". Location is built from each city's `en_name`; the country from `job_post_info.address_list[].country.en_name`. "Hong Kong (China)" loses its suffix, or the parser places it in mainland China too. ByteDance R&D is not all China: San Jose, Seattle, Singapore, London, Riyadh and more.
- **The posting page depends on host and hire type**: ByteDance serves `/experienced/position/<id>/detail` and `/campus/position/<id>/detail`; a customer tenant serves `/index/position/<id>/detail` and 404s `/campus`. Only the campus route server-renders the title; the others are a client-rendered shell.
- **Throughput, not a rate limit, sets the duration**: from a residential IP a 100-row page took ~6 s and a 500-row page ~5 s; nothing was refused across ~200 requests. ByteDance is ~20 requests (~2.5 min), MiniMax 2.

**Tencent traps** (`tencent`; live-verified 2026-10-15):

- **careers.tencent.com is the mainland-China experienced catalogue, and it re-lists Tencent's Workday.** Of 804 postings in the 技术 (Technology, `parentCategoryId=40001`) category, 96 carry `SourceID: 4` and a `PostURL` on `tencent.wd1.myworkdayjobs.com` — Tencent's international tenant, which the `workday` adapter already crawls (245 open postings under `company_slug=tencent`). They are skipped; the 708 `SourceID: 1` rows are all in China. **Every campus posting here is such a copy**: the 11 graduate (`attrId=2`), 48 intern (`3`) and 6 青云计划 (`5`) postings are all `SourceID: 4`. Mainland campus hiring is a separate site (join.qq.com), not crawled.
- **Keyless JSON, any User-Agent, no `robots.txt`** (the path 302s to the site's 404 page). `GET /tencentcareer/api/post/Query` honours `pageSize=2000`: the category is ONE request, checked against `Count` and for duplicate ids, which earns `fullBoardListing`; a category over 2,000 fails rather than paging. The whole site is 2,230 postings (1,908 native).
- **The listing has the responsibilities, not the requirements.** `GET /tencentcareer/api/post/ByPostId?postId=` adds `Requirement`, a team `Introduction` and the sub-category (`OuterPostTypeID`), so the adapter hydrates new postings only (`HydratingSource`). An unknown id answers HTTP 500 `{"Code":500,"Data":"E1005"}`; a failed detail defers the posting. 120 details at 8 concurrent answered 200 with no refusal; a cold run is ~710 requests, ~1 minute.
- **`language=en-us` translates the facets, not the posting**: country, city and category come back in English ("China", "Shenzhen"), the title and body stay as written (Chinese). `LastUpdateTime` is "October 15,2026" in en-us (`2026年10月15日` in zh-cn) and is the only date stated. A listing row names ONE city even when the title names several ("（深圳/北京）").
- **The Technology category is the tech scope, because `classify` cannot read the titles**: over the 708 native technology titles it resolved a category for 5, `IsTech` for 2 and `ConfirmedNonTech` for 0; over the other ten categories (1,200 titles) it resolved 3 categories and confirmed nothing non-technical. The category sets `IsTechHint`; of its sub-categories only Quality Control (`qa`) and Security (`security`) name one discipline.

**MokaHR traps** (`mokahr`; live-verified 2026-10-15):

- **A board is a tenant careers SITE, `<orgId>/<siteId>`** — the two path segments of `app.mokahr.com/social-recruitment/high-flyer/140576` (DeepSeek), `moonshot/148506` (Kimi), `zphz/148983` (Zhipu). Moka's international deployment is `hire-r1.mokahr.com` (region `r1`, RedotPay `redotpay/100008889`); a tenant of one host is unknown to the other. `robots.txt` disallows two tenant sites (`lingjuninvest/46355`, `shopee/74378`), which the adapter refuses.
- **`app.mokahr.com` answers the listing ENCRYPTED.** `POST /api/outer/ats-apply/website/jobs/v2` returns `{"data": <base64 AES-128-CBC>, "necromancer": <the 16-byte key, new each request>}`; the IV is the `aesIv` every tenant page publishes in its init-data, `de7c21ed8d6f50fe` on every tenant checked. A padding failure means the IV moved, and fails the board. `hire-r1` answers the same body as plain JSON. The tenant HTML sits behind an Aliyun WAF cookie challenge; the API does not, and needs no cookie or special UA.
- **`limit` is capped at 50** (51+ answers code 102) and the pagination is `limit`/`offset` — `page`/`pageSize` are ignored and repeat page 1. **There is no total**: `jobStats.total` is 0 on every tenant. The walk reads until a short page and fails on ANY failed page, which is what earns `fullBoardListing`.
- **Half the tenants list rows with NO body.** Probed 2026-10-25 over 81 tenant sites: on 45 (SHEIN, Tesla, Zhihu, Vanke, Trip.com…) a listing row carries neither `jobDescription` nor `zhineng`. `POST /api/outer/ats-apply/website/job` (`{orgId, siteId, jobId, locale}`) answers both, encrypted the same way; an unknown or closed id answers `success: false`, code `703015`. So the adapter is a `HydratingSource`: a row with a body is used as is, a body-less row the catalogue holds is a `SeenRefresh`, and only a NEW body-less row costs a detail request. A failed detail defers the posting; `703015` drops it. 1,221 detail requests at 8 in flight drew no refusal, and both fsn and host2 are served.
- **The whole tenant is read; the tech hint comes from the tenant's own job function.** `zhineng` is each tenant's vocabulary: Zhipu files 117 of 134 postings under 其它 (other), so no function filter can scope a crawl. `classify` over the mainland tenants' titles resolved a category for 0 of 37 (DeepSeek), 13 of 100 (Kimi), 11 of 134 (Zhipu) and `ConfirmedNonTech` for none. `mokahrTechFunctions` lists the function names that state technical work outright, as seen on these four tenants (DeepSeek 21 of 37 postings, Kimi 47 of 99, Zhipu 11 of 126, RedotPay 2 of 67) — **adding a tenant means reading its function names first**, or its Chinese postings stay unplaceable. The 2026-10-25 roster pass added the IT/software names of ~40 more tenants; a bare `技术类`/`研发类` stays OUT because a pharma, property or battery tenant files lab and building work under it, and the map is shared across tenants. Names are matched with whitespace removed (`SW/ 软件开发`).
- **A location is a DISTRICT** (`cityName: "海淀区"`) under a province, with a Chinese country name; Hong Kong is country 中国, province 香港. The city is read from the administrative code's first four digits (`110108` → `1101` Beijing), the country from a small name map, a SAR from its province.
- `status: "pause"` postings are listed (7 of 339) and are skipped. Dates are zone-less China time; `openedAt` is the one every row carries. The posting page is `<host>/social-recruitment/<org>/<site>#/job/<id>` — it rendered the posting in a browser on both hosts, Kimi's `/apply/` site included. Four tenants, ~10 requests, none refused.

**Alibaba traps** (`alibaba`; live-verified 2026-10-15):

- **One portal, every business.** `POST https://talent.alibaba.com/position/search` with `channel: "group_official_site"` lists the experienced-hire (社招) postings of Alibaba Cloud, Taotian, Cainiao, Lazada, Amap and the rest — 4,165 on the day; the business is only the title's prefix ("阿里云智能-…"). Campus hiring is campus-talent.alibaba.com, a separate site, not read. No `robots.txt` (a JSON 404). Any User-Agent.
- **The CSRF check is stateless double-submit**: a self-minted `XSRF-TOKEN` cookie matching the `X-XSRF-TOKEN` header is served; no session or bootstrap request.
- **`pageSize` is clamped to 500 while the offset still advances by the size ASKED for**: `pageSize=1000, pageIndex=3` answers rows 2000-2499, so a walk asking for 1,000 silently skips half the catalogue. The adapter asks for exactly 500.
- **Most rows state no category, so the row cannot be the filter.** 2,391 of 4,165 carry `categories: null`, including plainly technical ones ("AI Agent研发工程师"). The `subCategories` filter still selects them: the 18 codes under 技术类 (130) from `POST /category/list` match 1,488 postings, of which 627 state no category. The parent code alone (`categories: "130"`) matches only 5. A row that DOES state a category must be a 技术 one, or the crawl fails as a filter the search stopped honouring.
- **The category is the platform's, not a guarantee**: it includes data-centre and warehouse operations roles (仓储运营专员, 高级消防工程师) the business files under technology. `IsTechHint` follows the dedicated-category rule all the same, because `classify` cannot read the titles: over the 1,488 technology titles it resolved a category for 29, `IsTech` for 9 and `ConfirmedNonTech` for 1; over the other 2,677, 35 / 1 / 2.
- **Every talent.alibaba.com deep link redirects to the portal home**, in a real browser too. `talent-holding.alibaba.com/off-campus/position-detail?lang=zh&positionId=<id>` — the same platform's holding-company site — renders any posting id, a Cainiao or Taotian one included, so that is Job.URL.
- **Locations are Chinese city names only** (`workLocations: ["杭州","北京"]`, `regionEnNameMap` empty, in `language: "en"` too). `cncity.go` maps the ~90 names the catalogue uses to English and a country; Tokyo, Madrid, Seattle and Seoul are among them, so an unmapped name keeps its spelling and states no country rather than being assumed Chinese.
- **The bodies are plain text in the row** (`description`, `requirement`); `experience.from` is a structured minimum in years. Three requests read the technology category, ~15 s.

**Meituan traps** (`meituan`; live-verified 2026-10-15):

- **Keyless JSON POST, any User-Agent**: `POST https://zhaopin.meituan.com/api/official/job/getJobList`; `robots.txt` 302s away (no file). A row is the whole posting — `jobDuty`, `jobRequirement`, `highLight` as plain text — so there is no hydration.
- **Pagination is NESTED**: `{"page": {"pageNo": N, "pageSize": 500}}`. A flat `pageNo` is ignored and always answers page 1 (career-ops, confirmed). `pageSize: 500` is served.
- **Three hire types, all real postings**: `jobType` `3` experienced (2,577 on the day), `1` campus graduate (197), `2` intern (364); the three sum to the unfiltered 3,138. One walk per type, so a posting's type is stated (`junior`; `intern`/`internship`).
- **The tech scope is the job family**: `jfJgList: [{"code": "11001", "subCode": []}]` is 技术类 in `GET /api/official/job/search/enum?enumType=JF` — 351 + 121 + 193 = 665 postings. An unknown code answers 0, and each row states its `jobFamily` and `jobType`, so a filter the search stopped honouring fails the crawl. `classify` over the 665 titles: category 3, `IsTech` 2, `ConfirmedNonTech` 0; over the other 2,473: 81 / 0 / 7 — the family sets `IsTechHint`.
- **Cities are Chinese names with the 市 suffix** ("北京市"), plus a few abroad (旧金山, 西雅图, 香港特别行政区) — read through `cncity.go`. The non-technical families carry Keeta's Gulf and Hong Kong delivery operations (科威特城, 吉达, 多哈), which is why an unmapped name never defaults to China.
- **A third party saw the site answer a mid-walk page with an empty list** (career-ops, read as rate limiting). Here a page that comes back empty before the count is read FAILS the crawl rather than ending it. Nothing was refused across ~20 requests; a crawl is three requests, ~3 s.
- The posting page `web/position/detail?jobUnionId=<id>` is a client-rendered shell (HTTP 200 for any id).

**Paradox traps** (`paradox`; live-verified 2026-10-20 from a residential IP):

- **The pages are walled, the API is not — but only with `X-Requested-With: XMLHttpRequest`.** Every `/co/<site>/…` page answers 202 with an AWS WAF JavaScript challenge. The front end's own JSON API (`/api/job-list-site-ui/search-job`, `/api/job-posting/get_widget_extra_settings`) answers any User-Agent, ours included, with no cookie — and 404 to the same request without that header. `robots.txt` disallows nothing.
- **A board is `<host>/<site>`, lower-cased.** Sites live on one of several instances — `olivia.paradox.ai`, `olivia.eu1.paradox.ai`, per-customer hosts like `darden.paradox.ai` — and only there. Site slugs are case-insensitive. McDonald's `www.mchire.com` runs the same software under its own domain and is not recognised.
- **Not every site has a list.** 8 of 34 sites sampled from real apply links answer the listing 404 "Site X is not found" while their postings resolve one by one; an unknown company answers 404 "Company slug X is not found". Neither is crawlable.
- **The listing is slow and paged; the total is exact.** `limit`/`page` (1-based); the server spends ~50 ms per row (100 rows 4-11 s, 2,500 rows timed out server-side), hence 100-row pages on the long-timeout client. A page past the end states `total_jobs: 0`, so the total is read from page 1, and the walk fails unless the distinct ids equal it (`fullBoardListing`). InspireBrands2 walked to 2,064 distinct of 2,064 under the default sort; a total can move by one between runs, which fails that run's board rather than truncating it.
- **The listing body is a 250-character preview**, so the adapter hydrates new postings from the detail resource (`job.description`, HTML). An unknown `job_id` answers 200 with no `job.description` and is deferred. The external id is the `job_id` of the row's own `original_apply_url` — which is the requisition id (`P1-6592464-1`) on some sites and the posting token (`PDX_…_<n>`) on others, never the row's own `job_id` field.
- **Date and country only where the site embeds a JobPosting.** `google_json_ld` is a whole `<script>` element as a string; 4 of 6 sampled sites carry it (BurgerKing2, InspireBrands2 do not). Its `datePosted` has a two-digit year (`26-09-23`).
- **A per-IP request budget, refused with 403.** Unpaced at 8 detail workers, one site read ~840 details in under two minutes and was refused the remaining 688; it lifted within minutes. Every request is paced (`paradoxRequestInterval`, ~1.4 req/s), and the first refusal defers the rest of that board's hydration. A first crawl of a 2,000-posting site is therefore ~25 minutes; steady state is the listing pages plus new postings.
- **Not an IT platform.** Restaurant franchisees, retail, care: the catalogue filter judges each title, and nothing sets `IsTechHint`.

**Gradcracker traps** (`gradcracker`; live-verified 2026-10-18 from the crawl host, fsn and a residential IP):

- **Cloudflare twice over: a fingerprint gate, then a per-IP RATE limit.** curl and Go's default TLS get a managed challenge (403 "Just a moment...") on every path but `robots.txt`; `fingerprintHTTP` (Chrome_144) is served 200 keyless. Then, at one request per 3 s (or 0.5 s, cookie jar or not) the edge served ~35 requests and refused every path from that IP for **55–65 minutes**, identically on all three IPs; at one per 10 s it served 60 posting pages from one IP and the whole 37-page listing walk from another without a refusal. `gradcrackerRequestInterval` is that 10 s, through one limiter for every request of a run; the first refusal ends the run's hydration, since the IP is blocked for the next hour anyway.
- **One boardless catalogue, not a board per discipline.** A posting is listed under every discipline it accepts — 1,018 of electronic-electrical's 1,350 were also among computing-technology's 1,455 — and `external_id` is namespaced by board, so per-discipline boards would store them twice. The adapter walks both disciplines (`gradcrackerDisciplines`) and keeps each posting once: 1,787 postings in all on 2026-10-18.
- **`fullCatalog`, proven by the listing's own count.** `?order=dateAdded` pages carry 80 cards, a page past the last answers 404, and the raw card count of a full walk equals the listing's `Show N results` exactly. Any page failing, or a walk short of that count by more than a few cards, fails the run instead of letting the source-scoped sweep close what it missed.
- **The listing carries a honeypot card**: a visually hidden `<article wire:key="hp">` linking a "featured graduate employer" (`/hub/9000001/.../graduate-job/1/...`). Only an article whose `wire:key` is the posting id in its link is a posting.
- **No JSON-LD anywhere.** The card is everything but the body (title, employer as the logo's `alt`, Location/Salary/Deadline/Degree/Duration/Starting, accepted disciplines); the body is the posting page's `.body-content`, which embeds video/insight panels (`not-body-content`), a feedback-pledge badge and a content hash that are stripped. The card's facts are prepended to the body as a list, since the start date, duration and degree appear nowhere else. The sitemap (one 2 MB request) names every live posting but no discipline, so it cannot discover anything and the adapter does not read it.
- **Grades come from the title dictionary.** The opportunity type in the URL sets only `EmploymentType=internship` for `work-placement-internship`; seniority is `internal/dict/classify`'s, which learned the UK vocabulary for this source ("Graduate" → junior, "Industrial Placement"/"Year in Industry"/"Summer Placement"/"Vacation Scheme" → intern). The rest of the unresolved titles are mostly not jobs at all (webinars, insight evenings, open days) and the catalogue filter decides those.
**Built In traps** (`builtin`; live-verified 2026-10-15, from a residential IP):

- **Boardless, one host.** Every market host (builtinnyc.com, builtinsf.com, builtinchicago.org, ...) serves the same platform and the same numeric posting ids; builtin.com with `allLocations=true` lists all of them. **`allLocations=true` is load-bearing**: without it the listing is narrowed to the requesting IP's region (from a Brazilian IP the 24-hour window held 17 pages instead of the whole world's).
- **The whole catalogue cannot be walked, so a run reads a window.** Every listing stops at page 1000 (page 1001 of any filter answers "No job results") at ~12-25 cards a page, and engineering alone fills those 1000 pages within a week. A run walks each technical category (`builtinCategories`, the listing's own category filter) restricted to postings updated in the last 24 hours (`daysSinceUpdated=1`, the narrowest option) to its natural end: ~326 pages for engineering, ~60 data-analytics, ~58 ai-machine-learning, ~49 product-management, ~42 cyber-security, ~28 designer by the pagination each first page renders. One live run read 4,001 distinct postings across the six windows in 10 minutes of paced listing requests (~300 pages). A walk that reaches page 1000 is truncated, and `crawlPagedLinks` logs it as such. Never `fullCatalog` or `fullBoardListing`. **Since 2026-10-22 the window spans all 14 top-level families** (`/jobs/<family>`: dev-engineering, data-analytics, cybersecurity-it, product, design-ux, project-management, operations, customer-success, sales, marketing, finance, hr, legal, content), technical ones first since the hydration budget is spent in that order: the catalogue is broader than IT. The first prod run is what measures their daily size. The crawl host's IP was served directly at the 2 s pace (15 of 15), so Built In needs no proxy or Scrapling row.
- **Hence the 30-day `sweepGrace`.** A posting is re-listed only inside its first day and whenever Built In reposts it, so on the 48h default nearly every live posting would close two days after it was seen. Every sampled JobPosting states `validThrough` exactly 30 days after `datePosted`, which is the window used. A posting withdrawn early lingers up to that long; a closed posting's page answers 404, so `cmd/liveness`' `probeDespiteRegisteredGET` could close it on evidence, but every probe spends the same per-IP budget as the crawl and is not wired.
- **`robots.txt` disallows `*?page=` and `/jobs*?search=`.** The bare listing's pagination (`/jobs?page=2`) is therefore off limits, and the adapter never uses it or the keyword search. It walks the category FILTER's pagination, whose links the site itself renders as `?allLocations=true&daysSinceUpdated=1&page=N` — the page parameter last, which the pattern does not match. `/apply/` and `/job/sitemap.xml` (a 404 anyway) are disallowed too; posting pages (`/job/<slug>/<id>`) are not.
- **The employer's ATS link is not public.** The JobPosting carries no apply URL and the page's `applyUrl` is empty for every one of 168 sampled postings: "Apply" opens a name/email form that posts to Built In before redirecting through the disallowed `/apply/`. The stored URL is the Built In posting page, and the dedup pass pairs a copy with the first-party posting.
- **Most of the listing is already crawled first-party, so the adapter is `CoverageGated`.** Of 168 sampled postings (99 employers), 118 (70%) belong to an employer freehire's public search already carries from a first-party ATS (workday 49, greenhouse 32, ashby 10, icims 9, smartrecruiters 5, ...), and 36 more to one it carries only from an aggregator — a lower bound, since the probe read at most 100 postings per employer and the gate's own answer is broader. Their bodies are never bought.
- **Read the CARD, not the posting link.** `div[data-id="job-card"]` holds the employer (`data-id="company-title"`), the title link (`data-id="job-card-title"`, whose `data-builtin-track-job-id` is the external id) and the work-arrangement badge beside the `fa-house-building` icon: `Remote`/`Hybrid`/`In-Office`, plus `Remote or Hybrid`, which states two arrangements and is not mapped. One posting can sit under two categories and is yielded once.
- **The body is the ld+json JobPosting** (the `type` attribute is entity-encoded, `application/ld&#x2B;json`, which the HTML parser decodes). `jobLocation` is one Place or a list (a PwC posting names six offices), countries are alpha-3 (`USA`, `AUS`), and `jobLocationType: TELECOMMUTE` marks a fully remote posting. `applicantLocationRequirements` reads `USA` even on a Melbourne posting and is not read.
- **Salary is structured, with one known data error**: an annual range filed as `HOUR` (a PwC internship states 29250-48000 USD/HOUR, rendered "29K-48K Hourly" on its card). An hourly USD figure above 500 is dropped rather than reinterpreted.
- **Rate limit: a Cloudflare managed challenge (HTTP 429, `cf-mitigated: challenge`).** One request every 0.5s was refused from the 44th request, ~0.85 req/s from the ~140th, after which roughly one in three was served; one every 1.5s (200 requests) and every 3s (100 requests) were never refused. Paced at one request every 2s (`builtinRequestInterval`); the first refused posting page defers the rest of the run's hydration. A run is ~10 minutes of listing plus at most 500 posting pages (`builtinDefaultMaxNewPerRun`, ~17 minutes), under the scheduler's generic 3000s timeout. **Nobody has measured the budget from the crawl host.**

**startup.jobs traps** (`startupjobs`; verified live 2026-10-14 unless dated otherwise):

- **It is not a startup board.** It re-lists corporate ATS postings (Oracle, ABB, Illumina, McGill University) beside the startups — ~278k postings, ~6.5k created a day. It is therefore an `aggregator` and `CoverageGated`: the postings of an employer freehire already crawls first-party are yielded list-only and cost no posting page, and so are the ones whose title the catalogue filter will reject (`DetailGate.Rejected`, see [pipeline/AGENTS.md](../pipeline/AGENTS.md)). Both are never stored, so without the gate every run would re-buy their bodies.
- **Three hosts, three budgets, and the design is the budgets.** Pages (`startup.jobs`) sit behind a Cloudflare JS challenge; the sitemap (`cdn.startup.jobs`) and the catalogue (Algolia) do not. Each is read for what only it has: the sitemap for the whole catalogue's identity, Algolia for metadata, the posting page for the body.
- **Only `fingerprintHTTP` passes the challenge.** The homepage and 40 sequential posting pages at ~1 req/s all answered 200 through the Chrome_144 profile. The 2026-09-24 spike found Chrome_133/Chrome_124 refused, Chrome_131/Safari_16_0 served, plain HTTP and headless Chrome refused, and the edge 403-ing an IP after a long run of requests. Every Cloudflare-fronted request is paced through one 1 req/s limiter, and the first 403/429 stops the run's hydration — the refusal is about the IP, and every further request would be refused alike.
- **The Algolia search key allows about 100 queries per IP per HOUR, and that rules out walking the index.** Measured: refused with 429 after ~100 queries, cleared ~an hour after the first. The multi-query endpoint (`/1/indexes/*/queries`) answers several queries in one request but each is counted — 19 batches of five were refused at the 20th. `hitsPerPage` and `length` are both clamped to 100, so the 278k index costs ≥2,786 queries: ~28 hours of quota. A run therefore reads from Algolia only (a) the WINDOW — the postings created in the last six hours (180-340 are created an hour, so 1-2k postings, 11-25 queries) — and (b) a randomly chosen chunk of the never-stored backlog with what the window left, both inside `startupjobsQueryBudget` (80). Reaching the window back to the sitemap instead would have been a literal proof of completeness, and would cost ~90 queries in the hours before 03:00. **The provider must not be crawled more often than every two hours** (the scheduler default), or the runs spend the IP's hour twice over.
- **The key is a SECURED key whose `validUntil` is ~3 hours out**, so it is read from the homepage's `<meta name="current-algolia-…">` tags on every run; a hardcoded key would die the same day. A missing tag fails the run.
- **A query reads at most 500 hits (`paginationLimitedTo`), and `browse` is not allowed with the search key.** Past the 500th hit Algolia answers 400 ("you can only fetch the 500 hits for this query"). The window is read in slices of `created_at_i`, each narrowed until its `nbHits` fits and then read until the distinct hits equal it; a one-second slice holding more than 500 FAILS the run rather than reading the first 500 and calling it complete — the peopleforce lesson, a capped page is not the end.
- **Slice on `created_at_i`, never on `published_at_i`.** `published_at_i` is re-stamped in daily batches at midnight UTC: 3,078 postings carried exactly 2026-10-13T00:00:00Z and 1,004 the next midnight, so no filter can split them under 500. 149 carry `published_at_i = 0`. `created_at_i` is when the row was created and does not move.
- **`nbHits` is an ESTIMATE on a large filtered result** — 241,643 for a `created_at_i>=0` filter matching all 278,516, flagged `exhaustiveNbHits: false`. The walk refuses any count that is not exhaustive; the unfiltered count is exhaustive and is what the sitemap is checked against.
- **The sitemap is the only affordable statement of the whole catalogue.** `robots.txt` names `cdn.startup.jobs/sitemaps/startupjobs/sitemap.xml.gz`; six `posts*.xml.gz` files (~10 MB each, served as plain XML despite the suffix) listed 277,702 posting URLs against the index's 278,516. It is regenerated once a day at 03:00 UTC, and a posting URL ends in the index's `objectID`. Its URLs carry no title or employer, so it serves liveness (a stored posting it lists is refreshed by identity) and completeness — **which is what earns `fullCatalog`**: every stored posting is refreshed by every run until it leaves the site, by the window while it is new and by the sitemap from the next generation on, and the gap between the two stays far inside the sweep's 48 hours while the sitemap is at most 36 hours old. The run fails rather than refresh less: any file or query failing, a sitemap older than that, or a sitemap listing under 95% of the index's pre-window count (a generation that lost a file loses a sixth of the catalogue, and a source-scoped sweep would close that sixth).
- **The backlog is mostly postings already judged and discarded**, which come back every run because they are never stored. Read newest-first it would spend every run on the same freshly-discarded top, so the run reads one chunk at random — not by the clock, because a chunk named by the hour aliases with the two-hour cadence and skips every other chunk.
- **There is no link to the original ATS posting.** Applying goes through the site's own `/apply` (disallowed in `robots.txt`); the JobPosting's `hiringOrganization.sameAs` is the employer's homepage. So the URL stored is the startup.jobs page, and the dedup pass is what pairs a copy with the first-party posting.
- **Neither date means what it says.** The page's `datePosted` carries 2013 dates on live postings and `validThrough` is stamped a month ahead of every crawl (2026-09-24 spike); the index's `published_at` is the midnight re-stamp. `PostedAt` stays nil and nothing reads either for lifecycle.
- **The index's facets are clean and closed.** `workplace_type_id` is `on-site`/`hybrid`/`remote`, `employment_type_id` is four values (`full-time` 256k, `contractor`, `part-time`, `internship`), `country_code` is ISO alpha-2 (empty on ~3%), and salary is whole units with an ISO currency. `salary_interval` was `year` 3,463 / `hour` 450 / `month` 95 / `week` 1 / `biweekly` 1 over 4,010 recent postings; the last two have no freehire period and drop the salary.
- **One end-to-end run against the live site** (2026-10-14, local, real transports, hydration capped at 5): sitemap 277,702 postings, window 1,062, backlog chunk 3,889 found of 4,000 ids asked (111 removed since the sitemap), **54 of 80 Algolia queries**, the whole listing in 7 s; 5 posting pages read. The catalogue filter turned away only 253 of the 4,951 candidates (5%) — `ConfirmedNonTech` is deliberately narrow, so on this source it is the COVERAGE answer that will bound hydration, and that can only be measured against prod's database.
- **The posting page is 128-167 KB and its body is the ld+json `description`** (one JobPosting block per page, Trix HTML). A page that answers without one defers the posting, like any unreadable page — a stored body-less row would never be hydrated again once past `pipeline.HydrationRetryWindow`. Hydration is capped at 1,000 pages a run (~17 minutes at the pace), so a cold start accretes over runs, newest first.

**SEEK traps** (all verified live on 2026-08-16, AU and NZ):

- **The bot protection guards the pages, not the API.** A plain request to a SEEK search page or to a `/job/<id>` page answers **403** behind a Cloudflare interstitial ("Just a moment..."), and browser-shaped headers do not clear it. `GET /api/jobsearch/v5/search` — the site's own frontend endpoint — is not gated at all: it answers 200 JSON with no cookie, no credential and no browser-shaped User-Agent (confirmed with no UA, `curl/8.7.1`, `Go-http-client/2.0` and the project's own `freehire/0.1`). Descriptions come from `POST /graphql`, operation `jobDetails`, on the same terms. The consequence for the lifecycle: SEEK postings **cannot be liveness-probed**, because that path fetches the gated job page — which is why the adapter leans on `sweepGrace` instead.
- **`totalCount` is a function of `pageSize`.** The same query answered **36** at `pageSize=1`, **688** at `pageSize=20`, 680 at 50 and 666 at 100. It can neither drive pagination nor detect truncation, so the adapter's response struct does not even declare the field and the walk stops on the repo's usual `added == 0`. This is the same class of bug as WhatJobs' `limit=1` below — a listing's self-reported total is not evidence.
- **The result window ends near 550.** `pageSize=100` serves pages 1–5 and answers page 6 empty; `pageSize=50` serves through page 11 (offset 550) and page 12 empty. `pageSize` above 100 returns nothing. A slice larger than the window has a tail no crawl can reach — five of Australia's 22 ICT subclassifications, the largest at ~746 postings.
- **`where` is load-bearing and omitting it does NOT mean "everywhere".** Dropping it collapsed a 688-posting slice to 36. It travels with the market's host and `siteKey` as one unit.
- **`www.seek.com.au` now 308-redirects its HTML routes to `au.seek.com`**, and the older `chalice-search` API paths are gone. `api/jobsearch/v5/search` is the live one, and it answers on the `www.seek.com.au` host without a redirect.
- **`companyName` is empty on roughly one posting in thirty**, where `advertiser.description` carries the name the employer typed — including SEEK's **`"Private Advertiser"`** placeholder, which is not a company. Such a posting is dropped rather than filed under a placeholder that would otherwise collect anonymous postings from both markets.
- **`salaryLabel` is free text, and about a tenth of the postings that fill it use it for marketing copy** ("Strong remuneration", "Optional 9 day fortnight | Career Growth") rather than a salary. It is folded into the description verbatim; it never populates the structured salary fields.
- **SmartRecruiters meters per caller, and 5,204 boards through one host spent most of the budget on retries.** Measured on production 2026-09-16: **5,165 of those boards took a 429 inside two days** while the same 5,165 also succeeded, and 2,246 sat in a failed state at any one moment — the crawl was getting through by repeating itself. `last_error` alone does not prove this and nearly misled the investigation: it holds the LAST error ever, so a board can be healthy now and still carry a 429 from July. `last_error_at` is what separates a live refusal from a remembered one. The ceiling was measured rather than guessed — from a clean egress IP the API served **30 of 30 requests at 10 req/s with zero 429s** — and `smartRecruitersRequestInterval` sits at half of it (~5 req/s, ADP's rate for the same shape of problem), because production shares one egress address with the rest of the fleet and that measurement cannot see the contention. Tune it as ADP's is tuned: downward while boards still 429, upward only while none do — which is what happened: **raised to ~8 req/s on 2026-09-17** after six hours at 5 req/s produced zero 429s and lifted the healthy count from 2,958 to 3,355. Coverage was the argument: a 50-minute run reached ~200 of 5,170 boards before `TimeoutStartSec` killed it, and the limiter is the wall — the adapter fans out across boards concurrently but every request queues behind one shared bucket, so boards-per-run scales with this number and nothing else. It sits at 80% of the measured ceiling, not at it, because that ceiling was measured from a CLEAN egress IP and production shares one address with the rest of the fleet. **The next move is not a higher number** — it is sharding the provider the way paylocity is sharded, which buys coverage without asking SmartRecruiters for more. **It hydrates since 2026-10-19** (`FetchNew`): it had re-read every posting's detail on every run, so on 2026-10-19 a 50-minute run reached 230 of 7,988 live boards (boschgroup alone is 5,068 detail requests, ~10.5 minutes of the bucket), 2,879 live boards had never been crawled, and 1,298 still carried a 429 from the 2026-09-16 storm because no run had reached them since — a stale refusal, not a live one (zero 429s in the journal for the two days before). A seen posting now costs nothing past its listing page; `BODY_REFRESH_DAYS` is what re-reads an edited body.
- **A third party's query parameter is a claim, and apploi's stopped being true without saying so.** `?employer=<id>` is how that API is meant to scope a board. Measured 2026-09-15 it scoped nothing: a real id, a nonsense id and no parameter at all returned byte-identical pages — the whole global catalogue. The adapter believed it, so each of **5,833 boards stored that catalogue under its OWN company**: 1,565,701 rows standing for 3,024 real postings, **99.81% duplicates**, every posting filed under 3,898 different employers. Unrepairable, because the true employer was never written to any column, and the provider was retired whole.

  **The adapter is now GONE, and the reason is yield rather than the bug.** The bug was in fact fixable: the API hands each posting its own `brand_name`, so a boardless adapter crawling the one global catalogue once would attribute everything correctly, with no duplicates and no board list — the original defect was `firstNonEmpty(e.Company, j.BrandName)` preferring the board's claim over the posting's own answer. Measured 2026-09-18, that is not worth building. The catalogue runs to roughly 100 000 open postings and every one of them is healthcare: of 9 529 titles sampled across the whole offset range, `classify.IsTech` accepted **2**, and reading them by hand found no IT job at all — nursing homes, ABA therapy, caregivers, dietary cooks. Worse, **61% DO resolve a category** (healthcare, hospitality, personal services), and a resolved category alone admits a posting to the index (`search.CategoryUnresolved` returns false the moment `category` is non-empty), so a working adapter would add on the order of 60 000 nursing postings to an IT catalogue and approximately zero engineering ones. **If apploi is ever reconsidered, re-run the YIELD measurement first** — attribution is the solved half and is not what makes this a bad trade. The deleted code (including a `proveEmployerFilter` guard that made the API demonstrate its own filter before any board was trusted) is in git history; what killed the provider is in this paragraph.

  One thing that measurement paid for on its way out: apploi's healthcare titles were the corpus that exposed the bare `mobile` category alias reading "mobile" as travelling work — 18 483 open postings across the whole catalogue, unrelated to apploi. A foreign source's corpus is a mutation test of our own dictionary even when the source itself is worthless. See `internal/dict/classify`'s `TestParse_MobileIsAPlatformNotAnErrand`.
- **The GraphQL detail endpoint meters by a per-IP request BUDGET, and the first prod run walked straight into it.** Unpaced, one crawl fired **3,267 detail POSTs in 95 seconds** (43 boards, each detail pool bursting to `defaultDetailWorkers`) and was answered **429** on essentially all of them — while a single identical request from the same host returned **200 after two minutes idle**. An immediate refusal that clears on idle is a window, not saturation, so the fix is a rate limiter (`seekDetailInterval`, ~2 req/s shared across every board) and NOT the in-flight cap `trudvsem`/`emagine` use: eight fast requests a second from one worker is still eight a second. The listing was never refused, so only the detail path is paced.
- **seek and ukgready are the hydrating adapters that DROP a posting whose detail failed** instead of ingesting it list-only. Most others keep it, and that rule is right where its premise holds — a detail failure is rare, so keeping the posting beats losing it. SEEK breaks the premise twice over: refusals arrive in bursts of thousands, and the retry that normally absorbs a failure is bounded — a stored row with no body is re-offered for hydration only while it is younger than `pipeline.HydrationRetryWindow` (14 days), after which `seen` reports it like any other row (the same trap `pacer.go`'s emagine note calls out). A backlog of thousands does not clear inside that window. A DROPPED posting stays new with no deadline at all, which is why seek does not rely on the window. The first prod crawl stranded **2,593 of 2,982 postings (87%) with no description**, none of which any later crawl could repair; they had to be pruned. Deferring a posting by one crawl is recoverable, storing it body-less is not — that asymmetry, not a preference, is why seek differs. **ukgready breaks the premise differently and worse**: the fallback it would store is not an empty body but the listing's 250-character preview, which is a description as far as `pipeline.HydrationRetryWindow` is concerned, so nothing ever re-offers it — the stub is permanent from the first crawl, not after 14 days. Dropping keeps the posting unseen and the next crawl retries it.

**TriNet Hire traps** (`trinethire`; verified live 2026-10-24):

- **Only the numeric id addresses a company.** The board is the path segment `<id>-<slug>` as a posting link spells it, but `/companies/16150/jobs` and `/companies/16150-anything/jobs` serve the same page, so `boardIdentity` folds the slug off. The posting id is global across the platform.
- **The listing's location column is a NAME the employer chose, not a place** ("Automox Inc. HQ", "Remote in the Midwest", a recruiter's name). The place is the posting page's `City, ST` line, which takes wildcards (`*, NY`, `All, All`). The adapter keeps the address, drops a wildcard part, and lets the label contribute only the word "Remote".
- **The salary line prints a unit only for hourly pay** ("$45.00 - $50.00 per hour"). A line with no unit is the annual type, except where an employer typed an hourly figure into it ("$23 - $25") — so an annual figure under 1,000 is dropped.
- No posting date, no JSON, no schema.org markup; one unpaginated listing table (largest of 100 tenants: 30 rows).

**GoHire traps** (`gohire`; verified live 2026-10-24):

- **It meters a request QUOTA, not a rate, and remembers.** From the crawl host one request every ~1.3s was served 400 times and refused with 429 from the 401st; a residential IP refused at ~270 requests was still refused 30 minutes later. So `gohirePacedGetter` paces at one request per 10s (under 400 an hour), and the first 429 latches the adapter for the rest of the run (`gohire.refused`) instead of letting the shared client's retries spend the quota. Harvest with `-pace 0.1`.
- **Only the trailing 8-character code addresses a site.** `jobs.gohire.io/<anything>-de5jlhjo` and the bare code 301 onto the canonical slug, so `boardIdentity` folds onto the code. An older numeric form (`dexerto-19265`) still appears in crawled links and answers 404.
- **Any listing page failing fails the board**, not just the first — the opposite of `crawlPagedLinks`' salvage rule, because here a later page failing is the quota running out mid-board, and a salvaged first page would leave the rest unseen run after run until the sweep closed it.
- The listing states its own extent (`Page 1 of 3, Total 21 jobs`), ten cards a page under `?page=N`; the posting page's JobPosting carries `jobLocationType` (TELECOMMUTE), `applicantLocationRequirements` as country NAMES, and a structured `baseSalary`.
- `recooty` (`jobs.recooty.com`, seen beside these two in the same remote-job feed) was measured and NOT onboarded: 58 tenants, ~1.3k postings, dominated by insurance-agency "work from home sales agent" listings repeated per tenant, and 25 of 55 sampled postings dated 2023.

**UKG Ready traps** (all verified live 2026-09-02):

- **`ukg` and `ukgready` are two different products, not two spellings of one.** `ukg` is UKG Pro Recruiting (formerly UltiPro) on `*.ultipro.com` / `*.rec.pro.ukg.net`, board `<host>/<tenant>/<guid>`; `ukgready` is UKG Ready (formerly Kronos Workforce Ready) on the SaaSHR-era hosts, board `<host>/<tenant>`. They share nothing but a vendor — different API, different id space, different board shape.
- **The career page is a SPA and its REST API is keyless.** `https://<host>/ta/<tenant>.careers` renders client-side (the same URL without `ShowJob` answers a SIGN-IN page on the legacy UI, so it is not a listing to scrape). The API behind it is `/ta/rest/ui/recruitment/companies/|<tenant>/job-requisitions` — note the percent-encoded pipe — with the body at `.../job-requisitions/<id>` and the tenant's locale at `/ta/rest/ui/bootstrap/companies/|<tenant>/format-settings`.
- **`offset` is a 1-based PAGE NUMBER, not a row offset, and nothing about the response says so.** It is echoed back in `_paging` verbatim and an out-of-range page is answered `200` with an empty `job_requisitions` — no error, no clue. Confirmed on an 84-posting board at `size=50`: `offset=0` and `offset=1` both serve rows 1-50, `offset=2` serves the remaining 34, `offset=3` is empty, and the union of those pages is the whole board. Advancing it by the row count — the shape the name invites — reads page 1 and then page 200, so every board past one page truncates in silence; a 259-posting board ingested exactly 200 that way before the first smoke run caught it.
- **`_paging.total` is trustworthy, unlike SEEK's `totalCount`**: it answered the same figure at `size=1` and `size=200` on every board tried, so it can bound the walk and one row settles liveness (which is what `cmd/harvest-boards`'s prober spends). `size` above 200 is a 400.
- **The host is the ENVIRONMENT, and an environment's hosts are branding.** A tenant lives in exactly one UKG environment (`US-4-PROD`, `US-61-PROD`, `AU-1-PROD`, …) and every host outside it answers "Company not found" — including hosts in the same country, so this is not only data residency: `secure4.saashr.com` (US-4) does not know a US-61 tenant. Inside one environment the white-label hosts are interchangeable: `secure.saashr.com`, `secure4.saashr.com` and `secure2.entertimeonline.com` all serve the same US-4 tenant, and `secure.workforceready.com.au` and `aus-secure.prd.mykronos.com` the same AU-1 one. So the host must be in the board id, and two board ids differing only in the host are ONE crawl target — `sources.boardIdentity`'s `ukgready` entry folds them, the icims lesson applied before it cost anything. It earned its keep immediately: a candidate spelled `aus-secure.prd.mykronos.com/6183838` was recognised as a board already held under `secure.workforceready.com.au`.
- **The listing's `job_description` is a ~250-character ellipsis-terminated preview.** The body is on the detail resource and is heavy: employers paste base64 data-URI images into it, and one board measured a **100 KB median** (the images are stripped by `htmltext.Sanitize`, which allows no `<img>` — the cost is the transfer, not the stored row). That is why the adapter is a `HydratingSource`. A steady-state re-crawl is then free, but note the non-tech rejection re-buys what it discards: these are payroll tenants, so a board is often 90% blue-collar, a rejected posting is never stored so it is never `seen`, and its body is fetched again every run. Schedule the provider accordingly.
- **Pay is a bare number; the currency belongs to the TENANT.** `base_pay_from`/`base_pay_to` carry no currency and no symbol — the career page reads it from the tenant's `format-settings` (`locale.currency_code`), and so does the adapter, once per board and only when a posting states a publishable figure. `base_pay_frequency` is exactly `YEAR|MONTH|WEEK|HOUR`; `WEEK` has no value in `vocab.SalaryPeriodValues`, so that amount is dropped rather than published half-qualified.
- **`employee_type` is a tenant-authored picklist, not an enum**, and its most common values are FLSA pay classes (`Exempt`, `Non-Exempt`, `FT Non-Exempt`) rather than schedules. Only an explicit full-/part-time marker is mapped; everything else is left to the description parser.
- **There is no posting date on either resource**, so `PostedAt` is nil and freshness falls back to first-seen. `job_preview` exists but is board-level marketing boilerplate repeated verbatim on every posting (12 of 12 identical on two boards), so it is not part of the description; `job_requirement` is, and is non-empty on roughly two postings in five.

**remote.com traps** (all verified live 2026-08-16):

- **The RSC flight is the API, and it carries different halves of a posting at each URL.** remote.com is a Next.js App Router app: there is no REST endpoint and no Pages-Router `_next/data` one, and every guessed path answers 404/500. Sending `RSC: 1` makes a page answer `text/x-component` instead of HTML — the LISTING inlines a `jobsData` object as plain JSON (identity, employer, geography, seniority, compensation, and the `totalPages` that bounds the walk exactly), a POSTING PAGE carries the body. `Next-Router-State-Tree` is not needed; the flight comes back complete without it.
- **The listing has no description field at all, and the posting page's description is not in an `application/ld+json` script.** The RSC response has no HTML to hold one — the schema.org JobPosting arrives as a flight TEXT ROW (`<id>:T<hexlen>,{…}`), which `flightJobPosting` reads with the existing `nextFlightTextRows` + `jobPostingNode` pair. That split is the whole reason this adapter is a `HydratingSource`: a body-less row never enters the search index (`search.DescriptionMissing`), so a list-only crawl would ingest ~5.7k postings nobody can find.
- **`compensation.minimum`/`maximum` are MINOR units.** The listing states one posting as `1000000-1500000` USD/yearly while that same posting's `baseSalary` states `10000-15000`. Reading them verbatim inflates every salary a hundredfold. Related: `currency.symbol` is `"$$"` for USD (a format-template artifact), which is why the adapter reads `currency.code` and never the symbol.
- **`hiringLocation.timezone.name` is a zone label, not a place.** It is the city the zone is named after (`america-chicago` → `"Chicago"`), and passing it through bare makes `location.Parse` file a work-from-anywhere-in-this-zone posting under `Cities:[Chicago] Countries:[us]`. The adapter appends `" timezone"`, which is both what the posting says and — because the dictionary matches whole phrases — what stops the city from being invented.
- `hiringLocation` is null on roughly one posting in ten, `includedLocations` entries are `country` OR `region` (`north-america`, `latin-america`, `europe`, `africa`, `asia-pacific` — remote.com's own vocabulary, resolving to no country code), and `workplaceLocation` carries the country and city only when the posting is not remote.
- **No rate limiting observed**: 48 posting pages at 8-way concurrency (~12 req/s) all returned 200, so no pacer is wired. If that changes, `pacer.go` is where it goes — a `HeaderTextGetter` variant of `rateLimitedHTMLGetter`.
- **Most of the board is already ATS-covered, and `seen` cannot bound that** — which is why remotedotcom is the one `CoverageGated` adapter. Prod's first two runs both crawled 5,745 postings and kept 1,417: **4,123 were discarded by the aggregator gate** (the employer runs its own ATS, which freehire already crawls first-party) and 205 as non-technical. A discarded posting is never stored, so it is never `seen`, so the plain hydrating crawl bought and threw away 4,123 bodies EVERY run. `FetchNewGated` asks the pipeline which employers are covered before hydrating and skips their bodies. Measured across the twelve hydrating aggregators on prod, this is remote.com's problem alone — every other one wastes 0-3%, except aijobs (44% of a 500-posting board) and echojobs (12%), whose detail endpoint is dead anyway.

**Welcome to the Jungle traps** (live-verified 2026-10-15):

- **The board is a profession SUB-CATEGORY reference**, not a country and not the whole index. The index holds ~90.6k postings (60k with a French office, 18k US, 5.2k GB); its `tech-engineering-3NjUy` category ~19.6k, split into sub-categories that partition it — `software-web-development-iMzA4` 7.5k, `data-business-intelligence-yZjY1` 3.9k, `network-engineering-and-administration-yZjhm` 1.7k, `cloud-computing-and-devops-mZTM2` 870, `cybersecurity-2MDhm` 800, `it-support-and-services-3Njcy` 384, `hardware-engineering-lMWZl` 272. `engineering-disciplines-4YWVi` (3.7k) is mechanical/civil engineering and is not a board. A reference is self-checking: one that matches nothing FAILS the crawl, because this adapter is swept board-scoped and a renamed reference must not read as a board whose postings all closed.
- **The listing is WTTJ's own Algolia index, and the key is fetched, not configured.** `/api/env` publishes the app id and search key as a `window.env = {...}` script; the key is referer-locked. CloudFront 403s a bare `Mozilla/5.0` User-Agent on that path while the project UA and a full browser UA are served.
- **Algolia reads at most 1,000 hits per query, and `distinct` is configured on the index.** Paging past 1,000 is an error, so the walk partitions a board by `published_at_timestamp` into half-open windows of at most 1,000 and checks each window's hit count against its exact `nbHits`, and the windows' union against the board's unwindowed count — which is what earns `fullBoardListing`. Every query sends `distinct=false`: with it on, a 7,577-posting board answers 1,531.
- **The hit carries no description**, so the adapter is a `HydratingSource` over `api.welcometothejungle.com/api/v1/organizations/<org>/jobs/<slug>` (~5-40 KB), whose `description` + `profile` + `recruitment_process` are the body and whose `apply_url` is the employer's own (present on 91-95% of the two boards smoked, though some of those point at WTTJ's own `ats.welcometothejungle.com`; a posting without one keeps the WTTJ page). The API is keyed by slugs and the index by uuid, so `wttj_reference` must equal the hit's `objectID`. A few postings (4 of 7,557) carry an empty body and are deferred, not stored body-less.
- **About a third of a technical board applies through an ATS we already crawl** (greenhouse, ashby, smartrecruiters, lever, workday, workable — 1,000 data postings sampled), so the adapter is `CoverageGated`. The rest is real reach: Thales, Capgemini, NTT Data, CGI, Safran and the WTTJ-native employers have no first-party board here.
- **No metering observed**: 1,000 posting-API requests at 16-way and a full 7,557-posting board in 8 minutes answered no 429, only 16 client timeouts; the fan-out stays at the shared 8. A first crawl of the seven boards hydrates ~15k postings; steady state is one request per new posting.

**SchoolSpring traps** (all verified live 2026-09-02):

- **The board is a SEARCH KEYWORD because crawling the whole index would poison the catalogue, not because the index is unreachable.** It is entirely reachable: `api.schoolspring.com` is keyless, `GetPagedJobsWithSearch` pages the national board exactly (80,611 postings, page 807 at size 100 returns the last 11 rows, and `GetJobsCountWithSearch` agrees to the row), and an employer is a `organization` FILTER over that one index rather than a tenant — so the per-employer board shape does not exist. What makes an unscoped crawl wrong is the two numbers freehire's own dictionaries give over the live index: `classify.IsTech` fires on **36 of 80,611 titles (0.04%)**, while `classify.ConfirmedNonTech` — the gate that decides what a crawl may store — turns away 39,068 and **admits 41,543 (51.5%)**. It is a non-tech TITLE dictionary written against tech-company ATS boards, with no term for K-12 classified staff, so school psychologists, bus monitors, crossing guards, football coaches and cafeteria workers all pass it. **The gate does not hold against K-12 vocabulary, and no education board should ship as a whole-index crawl on the assumption that it does.**
- **The platform's own `category` taxonomy cannot select the technical slice.** It is the obvious thing to scope with — a server-side facet on the same index, the shape `hh` and `trudvsem` use — and it was measured before the keyword list was: 37% of postings carry no category at all (the union of all 239 categories is 50,644 of 80,611), the densest categories reach about a quarter of the technical postings, and the categories overlap so heavily — Administration/Technology and Student Services/Educational Technology share 335 of their 418 and 419 — that one board per category would store 40% of its rows twice. A keyword is the facet that does work here, and unlike a numeric facet id it is self-describing, so a typo cannot masquerade as an empty slice the way an unknown id would.
- **`keyword` filters rather than ranks, and it matches the EMPLOYER name as well as the title, as a bare substring.** "software developer" answers 0 rather than padding with near matches (the opposite of WhatJobs' feed), but "it manager" matches "Unit Manager", "it director" matches "Pit Director", and bare "network" pulls in 235 stale teaching postings from an employer called "Acero Schools Network". Every `schoolspring` board is a whole phrase for that reason, and the guard against a phrase going bad later is the per-board rejection line `cmd/ingest` already logs — which is exactly the "too broad a term" signature that line exists to surface.
- **TEDK12 is a different product, not another spelling of this board.** The per-tenant sites (`pembinatrails.tedk12.ca`) are PowerSchool Applicant Tracking: per-district hosts, server-rendered ASP.NET with a `__VIEWSTATE`, no JSON, board = the tenant host. They are also largely already here — 332 of the 377 distinct hosts this crawl's postings link back to are `*.tedk12.com` tenants, carrying 622 of its 1,261 postings — so a US TEDK12 adapter would mostly re-crawl the same postings under a second identity. What one WOULD add is the fleet outside the US index: no `*.tedk12.ca` host appears among those 377 and Pembina Trails is absent from the national index entirely.
- **The keyword slices overlap, so 35 of the 383 rows the first crawl ingested (9%) are one posting stored under two boards.** It is stable rather than churning — both boards list their copy on every crawl — and the duplicate markers collapse the pair in search. Accepted rather than fixed: folding the whole keyword list into one board would make the external_id namespace change whenever the list does.
- **17,700 of the 80,611 postings display a date before 2025**, some as far back as 2010, and nothing in either resource says they are stale — the platform serves them as open (33 of the 383 rows the keyword crawl stores, 9%). **Nothing downstream closes them either**: they are re-listed on every crawl, so the unseen sweep never fires, and `cmd/liveness` skips registered providers. Only the ghost signal marks them. A verdict would need a prober of this API's own — the detail resource answers `success:false` for a posting that is gone, which a plain GET of the SPA posting page cannot see — the shape `echojobs`/`himalayas` already take in `probeDespiteRegistered`.
