- **The listing carries no body, so this is a `HydratingSource`**, and the detail resource is cheap (~12 KB, median body 6.5 KB). It also answers a **200 with `success:false`** for a posting that is gone, so the envelope's flag has to be read: a caller reading only the payload sees an empty posting rather than an error.
- **The employer is the DISTRICT and only the detail resource states it.** The listing's `employer` is the individual SCHOOL ("Columbia River High School" for a Vancouver Public Schools posting), which would scatter one district across dozens of company slugs.
- **Pay is a bare number and NOTHING states a currency** — the site's own UI just renders a `$`. The only country the platform names is the employer's contact one (`countryName`, filled on ~60% of postings), so a salary is published only for a posting that states the United States; the international schools on the same index (a posting from Athens quotes €900–1,100 in its body) are exactly what that guard is for. `payDisplay` is the employer's own publish switch and is OFF on most postings that carry a figure, and `salaryCode` is employer-typed free text ("BU Salary Schedule", "SY", "Based on Education and Experience") with `payTypeName` as the tidier fallback.
- **`jobTypeName` is a closed enum** (its own dictionary endpoint lists all seven values), unlike `shiftType`, which sits beside it, is what the site actually renders, and is free text ("Varies", "FT - School Year").
- **No metering observed**: 82 listing pages at size 1000, 239 category probes at 6-way concurrency and 1,261 detail fetches at 8-way (~13 req/s) all answered 200, and the whole 40-board crawl takes ~56 seconds. No pacer is wired.

**Dayforce traps** (all verified live 2026-09-02):

- **The listing POST is CSRF-gated and a refusal looks nothing like one.** `POST /api/geo/<tenant>/jobposting/search` answers **403 with the body `Forbidden`** to any request that does not carry next-auth's double-submit pair — the `__Host-next-auth.csrf-token` cookie AND its token half echoed in an `X-CSRF-TOKEN` header. The 403 arrives with the site's own CSP and `server: cloudflare` headers, so it reads exactly like an edge block, and no amount of browser-shaped headers, referer or cookie jar clears it. `GET /api/auth/csrf` mints the pair (and returns the token in its body) in one request that the whole crawl then shares. Every OTHER route under `/api/geo/` is a GET and is not gated at all, which is why the platform looks open until the one endpoint that lists jobs.
- **The listing carries the WHOLE description, so nothing needs a detail request.** `jobPostings[].jobDescription` is the detail endpoint's `jobPostingContent.jobDescription` with its tags stripped — byte-identical on every posting sampled across four sites, never truncated. What it omits is the `jobDescriptionHeader`/`jobDescriptionFooter` pair, which is per-BOARD boilerplate repeated verbatim on every posting of that board (1,236 identical characters on all of dnata's), so the listing body is the better text as well as the free one. It arrives entity-encoded (`&nbsp;`, `&rsquo;`) as newline-delimited plain text, hence `html.UnescapeString` → `plainTextToHTML`.
- **`cultureCode` selects the SLICE, not the language, and an unconfigured one fails two different ways.** A site publishes each posting only in the cultures it was translated into, so asking `en-US` of a Quebec-only tenant answers zero rather than answering in French. Most sites accept a culture they do not publish in and answer an empty list; some reject it with **400** (`myepay/dmrstandard` answers 400 to `en-US` and 200 to `en-GB`, while `abbeyfield/candidateportal` — also en-GB-only — answers `en-US` with its full 115). The English variants that ARE configured do collapse: `en-GB` and `en-US` return the identical set. `sitecontext`'s `isoCultureCodes` lists what the site is CONFIGURED for, not where its postings are, so it cannot pick the culture for you — only a live search can.
- **Unioning the cultures is not worth it, and would double-store.** One posting keeps the SAME `jobPostingId` in every culture it appears in, so two board entries differing only in culture write it twice under two `external_id` namespaces and then close each other's rows on the company-scoped sweep — which is why `boardIdentity` folds the culture off. Measured over 60 sites, the non-English cultures added 104 postings against 5,765 English ones (~1.8%), most of them translations of postings already in the English set, for roughly 3x the requests.
- **Page size is fixed at 25 and `maxCount` is exact** — the opposite of SEEK's `totalCount`. The request body carries only `paginationStart`; `pageSize`/`take`/`count` alongside it are ignored. Confirmed against a 3,508-posting site: the last page is short and every offset past the end answers an empty list with the same `maxCount`.
- **`hasVirtualLocation` is the structured remote flag**, and such a posting usually carries `postingLocations: null` — no place at all. A posting can be both virtual and sited, so the flag is the only structured signal and a place merely NAMED "Remote" stays with the heuristic.
- **No metering observed**: 400 listing POSTs at 32-way concurrency (~14 req/s) all returned 200, so no pacer is wired and `cmd/harvest-boards` needs no `-pace`.

**Workstream traps** (all verified live 2026-09-02):

- **The board is the EMPLOYER, and its id is the eight hex characters a career-site URL opens with** — `www.workstream.us/j/<board>`. Workstream's own legacy careers URL, `/j/<employer-slug>` (`finecasual`, `sarku_japan`, `hdl_management_usa_corporation`), 301-redirects onto it, so the hex is the canonical spelling and the slug is not a second board. A parent runs one or more BRANDS (`/j/<board>/<brand>`), and `/j/<board>/positions` unions every brand's postings — so the employer is the crawl unit and the brand is not. Filing a group's brands under the group is the cost: the brand is in the posting URL and the posting page's breadcrumb but on no listing card, so reading it would spend a request on the `SeenRefresh` path that exists to save one.
- **`/j/<board>/positions` is the employer-wide listing only when the employer runs MORE THAN ONE brand.** An employer running exactly one is 301-redirected to that brand's root, which is a LOCATIONS listing — pages of stores, not of postings — and paging it silently harvests a fraction of the board. Every page states where its positions listing is in an inline `var searchBaseUrl`, so the crawl believes that rather than the URL it landed on; comparing it against the URL asked for is what keeps the multi-brand case at one request instead of two.
- **`var totalPages` is exact** (unlike SEEK's `totalCount`): the last page is short, the page after it renders no card at all, and the `added == 0` rule agrees with it. Ten cards a page.
- **A card carries every field except the body** — title, schedule tag, the store's full street address, the pay line — so the adapter is a `HydratingSource`. The address is the only location the listing states; it is passed through verbatim rather than reconstructed from the URL's store slug.
- **The pay line's `$` is unqualified and Workstream serves Canadian employers beside US ones**, so nothing on either page says which dollar it is — Chop Steakhouse & Bar's postings are Ontario ones under the same `$` as a Texas board's. The grammar is otherwise fixed and platform-rendered (`$15.00 - 18.00 per hour`, `Starting at $11.00 per hour`, `Up to $22.00 per hour`), but the line is stated in the description verbatim rather than parsed into the structured salary fields — SEEK's `salaryLabel` reading, for a different cause. Gusto's opposite call is sound for the opposite reason: Gusto Payroll runs US employers only.
- **The origin is AWS API Gateway and it meters by request RATE per IP — and a short sample reads the ceiling wrong.** Over cold boards, 1.0 req/s was refused nothing across 40 requests, ~1.4 req/s was refused 48% and 2 req/s 71%, which reads as a knee at 1 req/s. A 52-minute harvest paced at one token a second was then refused **524 of 2,138**, sustaining ~0.52 ANSWERED requests a second: the budget is a rate held over the whole run, not a burst the first refusal announces. The penalty also outlives the burst that earned it (a run refused at 2 req/s kept being refused minutes later at 0.8). **And a refusal makes the pace worse than the number it names** — the client's retry lives BENEATH the limiter, so one paced token becomes three requests exactly when the platform is already refusing; an ingest run paced at 1 token/s measured ~1.6 requests/s and lost 302 of 363 boards to 429. Hence `workstreamRequestInterval` at 0.5 req/s, half the observed ceiling, with the headroom left for the retries; `cmd/harvest-boards` wants `-pace 1 -workers 2` and will still see ~25% refused. A retired employer answers **410 Gone**, not 404.
- **Neither page states a publish date in any form**, so `PostedAt` is always nil.
- **There is no seed list and no directory.** `sitemap.xml` is the marketing site's (HubSpot) and carries no `/j/` URL at all, so `workstreamProber` discovers its own candidates from Common Crawl's index of `www.workstream.us/j/*` — the second path segment, filtered to the eight-hex shape. That is why `commonCrawlCandidates` takes a slug extractor: greenhouse and ashby key a board by the first path segment, Workstream by the second.
- **The franchise model makes the provider unshardable, which is what bounds it.** Each operator is its own Workstream account reporting the BRAND as its employer, so 358 of the 777 live boards are called "Chick-fil-A", 67 "Jimmy John's", 60 "Culver's". `sources.Config.Shard` assigns whole COMPANIES to shards (it must — the stale-job sweep scopes closes by `company_slug`), so those 358 are one indivisible lump bigger than any run's budget: `--shard=1/40` over the raw harvest crawled 363 of 777 boards. The catalog therefore carries one `workstream` board per distinct employer name (237), which is both what a run can crawl and what makes the provider shardable at all. The dropped boards would have bought near-duplicate crew postings under a company page that already holds them.
- **This is an hourly/frontline segment and its technical yield is essentially zero.** Two measurements agree. Of 516 postings sampled from 33 boards by title, 170 (33%) were rejected outright by the non-tech dictionary and **none** of the 346 survivors classified as `engineering_design` — they are "Team Member", "Server", "Baker", which the dictionary neither confirms nor rejects. A real ingest of 49 boards then stored 798 postings, of which **exactly one** ("Network Engineer") is an engineering role; the other technical-looking facets are dictionary artefacts of a frontline vocabulary — 107 `security` are physical security guards and 8 `mobile` are mobile patrol officers. Nobody should expect engineering postings from this provider. Note the cost shape that follows: a rejected posting is never stored, so it is never `seen`, so its body is re-fetched every run — at 0.5 req/s that steady-state re-hydration, not the listing walk, is what sizes the board file.

**Profession.hu traps** (all verified live 2026-09-03, over the whole 770-URL IT slice unless a smaller sample is named):

- **The board is one of the platform's own CATEGORY SITEMAPS, and the two IT ones are the whole onboarding.** Profession.hu is one central Hungarian catalogue with no per-employer tenancy; it publishes 23 category sitemaps holding ~12k postings, of which `itdev` (508) and `itops` (262) are IT. That is the AppliTrack/EDJOIN lesson applied before it cost anything: `classify.ConfirmedNonTech` was written against tech-company ATS boards and leaks on a general-population board, so the platform's own facet picks the slice. Over 128 live postings from those two categories it rejected **zero**, and a full crawl of both confirmed it — 709 postings ingested, none rejected, none body-less, none nameless. The same trust now also sets `Job.IsTechHint` on every posting `detail()` returns (issue #2601): the title dictionary resolves a category for only about half of these postings, and before this signal existed a posting it missed sat with `is_tech` unknown forever — `EnqueuePendingJobs`'s `is_tech IS TRUE` gate never queued it for enrichment, and it never became searchable. `cmd/backfill-profession-it-tech` is the one-off that reaches the rows ingested before the signal existed.
- **The other 21 categories are REFUSED, and `list()` says so before it reads anything.** They do hold technical postings — a live audit of all 21 on 2026-09-06 found roughly **30** — but nothing in a TITLE separates them from their neighbours, and that was measured rather than argued. The audit crawled all 21 behind a URL-slug prefilter and a title check, both reading `internal/dict/classify`: **11,403 listed → 227 passed the cheap gate → 159 stored**. What those 159 resolved through is the whole finding:

  | resolved through | n | what it actually was |
  |---|---|---|
  | `data_analytics` (bare `analyst`) | 45 | VAT, credit control, valuation, lease administration |
  | `project_management` | 23 | construction, manufacturing, HR payroll |
  | `business_analysis` | 13 | |
  | `design` + `creative` | 13 | concept artists, illustrators, a furniture showroom designer |
  | `product` | 11 | |
  | `qa` | 10 | a chemical plant's and a pharma plant's quality managers |
  | `solutions_engineering` | 5 | "Sales Engineer" |
  | genuinely technical crafts | ~29 | |
  | **`classify.IsTech(title)`** | **17** | |

  **The gate was not tuned badly — it used the wrong predicate.** `vocab.TechCategories` names the job families of a TECH COMPANY, `product`/`project_management`/`design`/`creative`/`business_analysis` included; that is right on a software employer's board and wrong on a general-population one. Making it work needs three corrections at once — a craft-only predicate, an exclusion for the bare `analyst`/`qa`/`architect`, and two Hungarian aliases the audit disproved (`hálózatüzemeltető` matched three gas fitters, bare `tesztelő` a pump tester) — and 30 postings do not buy a mechanism nobody can keep correct. **If you are about to propose this again, the numbers above are the thing to beat.**
- **`cmd/harvest-boards profession` DISCOVERS its boards and probes them cheaply** — `professionProber`, using the exported `sources.ProfessionCategorySitemaps` / `sources.ProfessionSitemapPostings`. The sitemap index IS the authoritative board list, so there is no seed file and nothing to keep in step by hand; and counting a category's sitemap costs one request, where the `adapterProber` fallback would crawl every posting in all 23 categories to answer the same question. The sitemap URL shape stays in `profession.go` alone: a prober that rebuilt it would be a second answer to "where is a category's sitemap", and the two would disagree silently the first time the platform moved one. A discovered board is labelled `Profession.hu — <category>`; `cmd/add-board --rename` gives it the platform's own wording, and nothing about the crawl depends on it.
- **THE PLATFORM MISHANDLES A REUSED HTTP CONNECTION, and that is what took the crawl down between 2026-09-04 and 2026-09-06.** Over one pooled connection it answers the first request or two and then returns a bare `EOF` for everything after — and one of the answers it *does* give can be **truncated**: measured from the production host on 2026-09-06, `sitemap-listings-itdev-hu.xml` came back as **379 bytes where the document is 76,403**, which parses cleanly as a sitemap that has lost its postings. A dedicated connection per request answered all five test URLs in full. So `profession` is wired through `singleUseConnSource` / `sources.NewSingleUseConnClient` (`safehttp.NewTransportWithoutKeepAlive`), and its `harvest-boards` prober reads over its own copy of that client rather than the run's shared one — the prober reads the same sitemaps, so it meets the same wall. **Do not diagnose this as pacing or as a rate limit.** Three explanations were tried live and all three left the failure exactly where it was: 25-second spacing between requests, `CloseIdleConnections()` between retries, and `ForceAttemptHTTP2: false`. Our three retries are 500 ms apart and reuse the same pool, so a retry can never recover it. `curl` — a new process, hence a new connection, every time — never reproduces it, which is what makes this look for hours like the platform blocking our IP.
- **THE SITEMAP INDEX IS READ ONCE PER RUN, and that is load rather than tidiness.** `resolveSubSitemap` re-reads the index on every call — the right shape for a provider with one board, and invisible while this crawl was two categories. With 23 it is 23 requests for one document, and **the platform does not tolerate it**: measured live on 2026-09-06 it answered the first request and then closed the connection (`EOF` after 3 attempts) on every one that followed, which arrives downstream as every category being unreachable and looks nothing like the crawl asking too often. Both sides now memoize — `professionIndex` on the adapter (one crawl) and `professionCategoryIndex` on the prober (one harvest, whose `probeAll` runs candidates concurrently). Only a SUCCESSFUL read is cached, so one transient failure does not decide the remaining boards, and the lock is deliberately held across the fetch so concurrent boards wait for the one request instead of racing to make their own. **Pace a manual harvest** (`harvest-boards -pace`); this platform rate-limits harder than the ATS fleet does.
- **`jobLocationType: "TELECOMMUTE"` means NOT STRICTLY ONSITE**, and it is set on 48% of the slice. The page shows those same postings as **`Hibrid`**, and the badge above the address is the ONLY thing separating hybrid from remote — the ld+json spends one flag on both. Worse, a posting carrying that flag has **no `jobLocation` at all**, so reading only the structured block loses the city on half the crawl. Badge and flag agreed on 128 of 128, so the page's address line is read for both. The third badge, `Országos lefedettség` ("nationwide coverage"), is neither a work mode nor a place.
- **The address line's postal code and street number are what stop the location dictionary.** `"Budapest"` resolves; `"1087 Budapest, Kerepesi út 21."` resolves nothing, because the code is glued to the city inside one comma-token. Dropping digit runs took a 128-posting sample from 61 resolved cities to 92, and the live crawl lands at 78-80%. The street name is left in — it resolves to nothing, and removing it would mean guessing which token is the city.
- **A taken-down posting answers 200, not 404 or 410**: the URL redirects to the platform's category listing, whose page carries no JobPosting block. **61 of the 770 listed URLs (7.9%)** are already in that state, so a body-less read is the ordinary case rather than a failure — the posting is skipped and never stored. The sitemap is therefore not a liveness list.
- **The ld+json `description` flattens the posting's bullet lists into one run-on paragraph.** The page carries the same text in the sections the employer typed it into (`#tasks` 100%, `#requirements` 98%, `#offer` 94%, `#other` 69%, `#workplace_extras` 17%), list markup intact, so the body is read from those. The section HEADING is the employer's own words — the same `#tasks` is headed "Feladatok", "A pozíció leírása", "Responsibilities" and "MI LESZ A FELADATOD?" — so it identifies nothing and is carried through as text. `#c_info` (the employer's standing blurb, the `employerOverview` call edjoin and gusto make) and `#contact_info` (where a postal address or a recruiter's mail lands) are excluded.
- **`occupationalCategory` is a good SELECTOR and a bad LABEL**, so it picks the board and is NOT mapped onto a category. Its 20 IT leaves were read against the titles of all 709 live postings: the largest, "AI és Automatizáció" (124), holds Senior Java Developer, AWS Cloud Engineer and a Vice President of Government Sales; "IT tanácsadó, Elemző, Auditor" (73) holds a Backend Engineer, an Energy Economist and a Technical Writer. Even the honest leaves lose by being read — a structured `Job.Category` takes precedence over the title dictionary (`jobderive`'s category precedence), and the dictionary already resolves **47%** of this slice, so the best map this vocabulary supports added a category to 132 postings while **flattening 76** the dictionary had placed more precisely (`backend`, `fullstack`, `frontend`, `mobile` → `software_engineering`). Two of its leaves would additionally have mapped onto `vocab.NonTechCategories` members (`IT support, Helpdesk` → `support`, `IT, fejlesztési vezető` → `management`), flipping `is_tech` off for 80 postings the platform's own IT facet had selected.
- **The country is stated in one of two mutually exclusive places and in three spellings.** A sited posting carries `jobLocation.address.addressCountry` (`"HU"`); a not-strictly-onsite one carries `applicantLocationRequirements` instead, naming the country as `"Magyarország"` or `"Hungary"`. The endonym is now in the location dictionary's country names beside `brasil` and `türkiye`. A posting sited abroad (`Külföld`) names no country the dictionary knows and correctly yields none.
- **`employmentType` is multi-valued and mixes a CONTRACT FORM with a SCHEDULE.** `"Alkalmazotti jogviszony"` (employee status) is 600 of 709 and names no schedule at all — the `ukgready` `employee_type` trap in a second language. Only `Teljes munkaidő`/`Részmunkaidő`/`Diákmunka` map. `experienceRequirements` beside it IS a closed picklist, six values across the whole slice, and maps cleanly.
- **The slice publishes no salary at all**: `data-item-variant` read `"salary confidential"` on every one of 128 sampled postings and no ld+json field carries an amount. Nothing to map.
- **No metering observed**: two full board crawls hydrated 709 posting pages at the shared 8-way detail fan-out (~5 req/s) with no refusals — 458 postings in 95s, 251 in 50s. No pacer is wired; `pacer.go` is where one would go. Note that **the origin refuses some residential and non-EU egress** (it times out from a developer machine while answering the prod crawl host in 0.4s), so validate a board from the crawl host rather than locally.

**Gusto Hiring traps** (all verified live 2026-09-02, across every candidate board):

- **The board id is the WHOLE path segment** of `jobs.gusto.com/boards/<board>` — `<company-slug>-<company-uuid>`. The uuid alone 404s and so does the slug alone, so neither half is the key. Everything is server-rendered HTML: no JSON endpoint, no embedded page state, no ld+json on either page.
- **A renamed employer keeps its OLD slug resolving against the same uuid**, so two spellings of one board both answer 200 and list identical postings — and a trailing `.json` answers 200 as well, since the route ignores it. External ids are namespaced by BOARD, so crawling both spellings stores every one of that employer's postings twice. 33 of the 825 candidates were such twins. `gustoBoardIdentity` folds every spelling onto the company uuid (the `boardIdentity` map, beside `icimsHost`, which exists for the same failure), so `boardcatalog.Insert` refuses a twin at the point it would enter the catalog (the unique index folds case, not FORM — see `sources.BoardDedupeKey`); the catalog additionally carries one `gusto` board per uuid, folded onto the spelling a posting's own breadcrumb links back to.
- **Cloudflare answers a managed challenge (403) to Go's default TLS+HTTP/2 fingerprint on every path**, so the adapter is wired with `fingerprintHTTP` alongside meta/uber/bayt/gulftalent.
- The listing serves **25 postings a page** under `?page=N` and renders "There are no open positions currently" past the last one, so the walk ends on a page that adds nothing. It carries every field EXCEPT the body, which only the posting page holds — hence `HydratingSource`.
- **Both pages are small**: a board page measured 9-46 KB and a posting page 13-24 KB across the live sample, so hydration is cheap here in a way it is not on hh (~1 MB a detail page). What bounds the crawl is the posting COUNT, not page weight: ~2.8k postings across 790 boards.
- **Neither page states a publish date in any form**, so `PostedAt` is always nil.
- The pay line is rendered text with a single fixed grammar (`$X - $Y per <period>`) — the platform's own compensation field formatted, not free text, which is what makes reading it structured signal. 2,098 of 2,789 live postings carried one and all but two matched. The currency is STATED as USD rather than read off the `$`: Gusto Payroll runs US employers only. The two exceptions say "per week", which freehire has no salary period for, so they yield no structured salary rather than a wrong one.
- **A posting whose body does not parse is dropped, not stored.** A stored row is `seen`, so it is never hydrated again once it ages past the hydration-retry window — deferring a posting by one crawl is recoverable, storing it body-less is not (the same asymmetry seek's note argues, for a different cause).
- **No rate limiting observed**: 825 board pages at 6-way concurrency (~13 req/s) twice over, plus a 790-board harvest that also fetched every posting page — all 200, no refusals. So no pacer is wired; `pacer.go` is where one would go.

**EDJOIN traps** (all verified live 2026-09-02, across the whole platform):

- **The board is a JOB TYPE, and choosing that over the district is the whole design.** EDJOIN serves one central index, so the numeric ids beside an employer are `districtID` — one more facet of that index, and there are 1,573 of them. What the board selects is the slice to crawl, the `hh` professional_role shape. The reason is measured, not stylistic: the platform holds **16,447 open postings and the catalogue filter rejects only 38.2% of them by title**, because `classify`'s non-tech dictionary was written against tech-company ATS boards and carries no term for K-12 classified-staff vocabulary — so the other 10,163 (paraeducators, instructional aides, yard monitors, campus supervisors, cafeteria assistants) would all be ADMITTED. Job type **25, "Information Tech. / Computer Svcs.", is the single technical entry in EDJOIN's own 66-value job-type vocabulary** and held 71 postings across 59 districts, of which the filter rejected **zero**. The cost argument agrees: the listing carries no body, a rejected posting is never stored so never `seen`, and a whole-index crawl would therefore re-buy ~2.4 GB of 147 KB detail pages every run forever — the ukgready note above, at ten times the ratio.
- **The listing endpoint dereferences three query parameters without a null check.** `GET /Home/LoadJobs` answers a **500 .NET NullReferenceException PAGE — HTML, not JSON** — when `catID`, `districtID` or `recruitmentCenterID` is missing or empty, however valid the rest of the query is. Every other filter may be omitted; omitting one simply does not narrow the search.
- **`totalRecords` is exact and stable across page sizes** (16,450 at `rows=1`, 25, 100, 500 and 1000 alike) — the opposite of SEEK's `totalCount` — so it can bound the walk, and one row settles a board's liveness. But `rows` is not otherwise trustworthy: asking for more than 1,000 is refused with an HTTP 500, and `rows=1` serves **10** rows while reporting a `totalPages` computed from 1. There is no result window; the whole index paginates to the end in 17 requests at `rows=1000`.
- **The same posting can appear on two adjacent pages** (3 of 16,447 did — ties on the `postingDate` sort), so a walk must dedup on `postingID` rather than assume pages partition.
- **An unknown job-type id is answered 200 with `totalRecords: 0`, not an error**, so a mistyped board is indistinguishable from an empty one. That is why every `edjoin` board is added under EDJOIN's own label as its company name. `harvest-boards edjoin <seed>` validates one against `totalRecords` in a single request — and the bespoke prober is not an optimisation here but a guard: the adapter fallback runs `Fetch`, which hydrates every posting in the probed slice, so probing "Teacher Assistant / Aide / Paraprof." (2,814 postings) through it would spend ~400 MB to learn a number the listing states for free.
- **The body exists only on the posting page, as a schema.org JobPosting in `ld+json`** — hence `HydratingSource`. Its `description` SUBSUMES the `experienceRequirements` and `skills` fields beside it (214/214 and 120/120 on the live sample), so appending them would duplicate text; `employerOverview` is district boilerplate and is left out, the call rippling and gusto make for their own company blurbs.
- **The pay fields are employer-typed text boxes, not currency-formatted platform fields.** Alongside `"$25.04"` they hold `"TBD"`, `"Stipend"`, `"UNPAID"`, `"District Salary Schedule"`, `"Range 1: $18.30"` and `"$22 - $26/hr"`. Only the PERIOD beside them is a picklist. So an amount is read only when the whole value is a bare money figure and nothing else — 46% of the platform and 72% of the IT slice qualify. `"Stipend"`, `"Bi-weekly"` and `"Semi-Monthly"` have no `vocab.SalaryPeriodValues` member, so those amounts are dropped rather than published half-qualified.
- **`FullTimePartTime` is a comma-joined multi-select carrying the remote flag too** (`"Part Time, Remote"`), which is EDJOIN's only structured work-mode signal — and it is rare: 3 of 16,447. A compound value names no single employment type, so it maps to `""` rather than picking one.
- **No metering observed**: 300 detail pages at 16-way concurrency (~12 req/s) all answered 200 bar one 410 for a taken-down posting, so no pacer is wired. `pacer.go` is where one would go.
- **Not a trap, but on the record**: 23 of the 71 ingested rows land `is_tech=false` because `classify.Parse` files "…Support Technician/Specialist" under the `support` category, which is in `vocab.NonTechCategories` — that category means CUSTOMER support and swallows IT help-desk work. It is a pre-existing dictionary property with catalogue-wide blast radius, not an adapter concern.

**AppliTrack traps** (all verified live 2026-09-02, across the 2,905 candidate tenants the `applitrack` boards were harvested from):

- **The crawl reads the WHOLE board** through the site's own "All Vacancies" listing (`?all=1`), one request per board. Until 2026-10-20 it read only an allowlist of the district's technology categories, because the catalogue was IT-only and the non-technical gate had no vocabulary for K-12 staff: over the 86,033 postings 2,387 live tenants were listing, the technology slices held only **360**. The catalogue is broader than IT now, so the board is crawled whole and the dictionaries decide what each posting is. Expect the first crawl after that change to hydrate roughly 36 postings per board (the fleet-wide average of that measurement), one detail request each.
- **Nothing on the platform is served as HTML.** `view.asp` is a shell that document.writes a script from `Output.asp` beside it, and `Output.asp` answers `text/javascript` whose `document.write('…')` arguments carry the WHOLE page — listing rows, posting bodies and all. So both halves of a crawl are read as raw text, unescaped, and only then parsed. The adapter asks for `Output.asp` directly: `view.asp` merely re-emits it inside a `<script>`, so reading the shell would mean digging the same payload back out. The only escape the payloads use is `\'`, because the platform's own markup quotes its attributes with the apostrophe that terminates the string it is written through.
- **A truncated response is legible, which is what makes it dangerous**, so the unescape reports whether every `document.write` it read was TERMINATED and the adapter refuses a page where one was not. The markup before a cut parses, the rows before it read fine, and the crawl would take an incomplete listing for a complete one — which on a company-swept provider closes every posting past the cut. `GetText`'s own `maxTextBody` cut now names itself (a `BodyTooLargeError`, returned alongside the bytes it did read), but an origin that stops mid-response still says nothing at all. The unterminated call is the one symptom BOTH leave, which is why it stays now that only half of what it guards against is typed.
- **A posting id linked from OUTSIDE the platform can be composite.** A consortium's posting is linked as `AppliTrackJobId=6536_37241`, where the suffix selects which member district's page to land on, and the endpoint ignores it — both spellings answer the identical posting, `JobID: 6536`. The adapter's pattern takes the leading digits, which is right rather than lax: keying on the whole token would store one requisition once per district that links it. No board's own listing was seen to emit the composite form (all 367 ids linked across the 263 boards' technology slices measured in 2026-09 were plain digits), so this only matters to `linksource`-shaped paths.
- **The unfiltered listing renders the CATEGORY MENU and no postings**, ~27-38 KB. `?all=1` is the site's own "All Vacancies" link and is what lists a whole board — the URL the crawl reads (measured 2026-10-20: 8-130 postings, 32-112 KB, 0.9-1.6 s on three live boards). No listing paginates in any form. `AppliTrackLayoutMode=condensed` is what keeps a response to its rows: the detailed layout inlines every posting's BODY (737 KB for 14 postings on one board), and an unrecognised layout value is not rejected — it silently serves the detailed one, so a typo there buys every body rather than failing.
- **There is no feed and no second listing endpoint.** `rss.asp`, `rss.xml`, `feed.asp` and `JobPostings.asp` all 404 both under and beside `/onlineapp/jobpostings/`, and `/onlineapp/default.aspx` — the other path a posting URL suggests — is the applicant sign-in portal, carrying no posting link at all. `Output.asp` is the whole API.
- **The count the listing states above its rows counts ROWS, not postings.** A statewide consortium lists one requisition once per participating district and every one of those rows carries the SAME `AppliTrackJobId` — Alaska's pool states 1,229 and links 870 distinct ids. An earlier revision of this adapter used the stated count as a truncation guard, failing a board that read fewer postings than the page claimed; measured against the whole fleet that would have failed 14 boards — the largest ones — on every run forever. The walk collapses the repeats, because they are one requisition with one posting page behind them; it is the number that is not what it looks like.
- **The pages declare no character set anywhere and are served as windows-1252** (the hrmdirect case again), so the body goes through `charset.NewReader` before it is parsed. Most are pure ASCII; the ones that are not are text pasted in from a word processor, so it is exactly the smart quotes and dashes that would otherwise arrive as U+FFFD.
- **An id the board no longer carries is answered 200 with an EMPTY posting list, not a 404**, so the posting block is selected by the id that was ASKED for (`p<id>_`): a response about anything else simply has no such node. A tenant that does not exist does 404; a few answer 500 on every path, which is a retired tenant rather than a transient failure.
- **The tenant path segment is case-insensitive** (`Saskatoon`, `SASKATOON` and `saskatoon` all serve the same board), so the board file's ordinary case folding is the whole of what `boardIdentity` would have added — there is no second FORM of the id.
- **The platform states no geography at all.** `Location` is the SITE inside the district ("Central Maintenance and Operations", "Various Locations", "Petersburg High School"), and no page anywhere names the district's town. Over a 1,004-posting sample the location dictionary resolved a city for 19 and a country for 4, and what it did resolve was as often an artifact as a place — a Pennsylvania programme's acronym `(PATTAN)` reads as Pattan, India. The field is still passed through: it is what the posting says about itself, there is nothing better to feed the dictionary, and dropping it would lose the one thing a candidate has to place the job.
- **A board is sometimes a CONSORTIUM, not a district** ("Wayne County Schools Employment Network", "Suburban Cook County Online Application Consortium") — one applicant pool shared by many districts. That is the tenant the platform sells, so it is the board, and its postings file under the consortium's name. Separately, a name can be carried by two committed boards, because two districts in different states are genuinely called the same thing (two "Washington County School District", in Utah and in Georgia); that is company identity's problem, not the adapter's, and renaming either one would be inventing a fact the platform does not state.
- **Roughly one posting in a hundred has no body at all** — the employer attached a PDF instead of typing one (1 of 116 on one board, 1 of 65 on another). Such a posting is dropped rather than stored body-less, on the asymmetry gusto and seek argue: a stored row is `seen` and so is never hydrated again once it ages past `pipeline.HydrationRetryWindow`, while a dropped one costs one detail request a crawl and can still arrive.
- **Steady state is cheap**: one listing request per board, and a detail request only for a posting the catalogue has not seen.
- **No rate limiting observed**: the harvest ran 3,410 requests at 6-way concurrency over 2,905 candidates, an earlier whole-board harvest 5,521 more, and the smoke crawl hydrated every posting at the adapter's 8-way detail fan-out — all answered, none refused. So no pacer is wired; `pacer.go` is where one would go.

**WhatJobs FeedAPI traps** (its documentation is wrong in several places; all of the below was verified against the live API):

- A `/` in the `user_agent` query value makes the edge redirect with the value corrupted (`Mozilla/5.0` → `Mozilla%215.0`), which is why every code sample in the vendor's docs fails. The adapter sends no `user_agent` at all.
- `limit` above 50 is silently clamped, and `limit=1` **with** a keyword returns an empty `data` with `per_page: 0`. Worse, the feed post-filters duplicates *after* selecting a page, so a request for 50 routinely returns 44 while more pages remain — **a short page is not the last page**, and the same posting can appear on several pages of one keyword.
- Pagination dies past roughly 2000 pages regardless of the `total` reported (594k), so no keyword is ever exhaustible; keep board keywords narrow enough to fit the page budget.
- `snippet` is the FULL description HTML, not the highlighted excerpt the docs describe. The documented `onmousedown` field does not exist.
- `salary` is always `"0.000000 - 0.000000"`, `job_type` always `""`, `logo` always `null` — all three are dropped rather than stored.
- `age`/`age_days` measure the record's age in the reseller's index, not the posting date (postings from unrelated companies share one value), so `PostedAt` is left nil.
- An invalid publisher answers **410** (docs claim 422); `unique_id` does not deduplicate despite the docs.
- No country field, and its cities collide with foreign ones (London is Ohio, Vienna is Virginia) — the account's country is stated by the adapter, since the publisher id is per-country.

- **jobleads must NOT be a StreamingSource.** The pipeline prefers StreamingSource over HydratingSource (pipeline.go:486) and `FetchStream` has no `seen` predicate — eightfold, the one streaming adapter, re-fetches every detail every run. jobleads' windows run tens of thousands of postings deep, so that would cost hours per run; only the buffered `FetchNew` (seen-gated details, one batched save per board) is affordable. Both costs of that choice — no rows until a board completes, an interrupted run loses it — are repo-standard (seek, emagine, justjoin, hh).
- **Every keyword is padded deep — there is no small window.** Any keyword answers full pages of ~100 (COBOL/Rust IT still full at page 40-50, Angular ~6000+, measured 2026-08-30), the same trap as whatjobs' `total`. A scratch one-keyword board is not a fast first run; verify locally by tightening the (already present) page cap and relaxing `jobleadsRequestInterval`, then revert — how the 683-row smoke test was run.

**gupy traps** (verified live 2026-10-19, from a laptop and from the prod host):

- **A board is a career-page SUBDOMAIN (`caju` for `caju.gupy.io`), not a companyId.** The companyId-keyed listing (`employability-portal.gupy.io/api/v1/jobs`) has answered an nginx 404 to every query shape since 2026-09-30, which failed all 1,511 boards for 19 days and left 73,156 postings open on a frozen `last_seen_at`. Nothing public lists by companyId any more — `portal.gupy.io/api/job-search/jobs` (the portal's own proxy) accepts a `companyId` parameter and ignores it. What still works is the API every career page renders from: `private-api.gupy.io/job-publication/public/jobs?subdomain=<page>` lists the page's published postings in ONE unpaginated answer (3,486 on `restaurantemc`), and `/job-publication/public/jobs/<id>` is the sectioned detail.
- **One company owns several career pages, and each is a board.** On prod 916 of the 1,511 companyId boards had postings on two or more pages (an internal-mobility page, a "confidencial" one); a page two companies both published on becomes one board, attributed to whichever companyId board `cmd/rekey-gupy-boards` met first.
- **The rekey preserves posting identity.** `cmd/rekey-gupy-boards` read each posting's page from its stored URL and rewrote `external_id` from `<companyId>:<id>` to `<page>:<id>`, and only after EVERY companyId board had moved did it add the page boards (a shared page crawled between two moves would collide), so the first crawl of a page UPDATES its rows instead of inserting duplicates, and the board-scoped sweep (`gupy` is `fullBoardListing`) closes the rows the page no longer lists. The rows no sweep can reach — on a page that lists nothing, or one Gupy 404s — the rekey closed itself.
- **The listing carries no body, so this is a `HydratingSource`.** The detail is the posting's only description, so for an UNSEEN posting a failed detail is an `unreadableDetail` marker, a 404 (`"Vaga inexistente na empresa atual"`) is gone, and a detail whose sections are all placeholders (`<p>.</p>`) is a marker too. `publishedAt` also comes from the detail. A posting the catalogue already holds costs no detail request: `FetchNew` re-lists it as a `SeenRefresh`, because the listing itself proves it is still published, and the listing stays whole, so the board-scoped sweep still closes what a page stopped listing. Re-reading every detail on every run was ~72k requests per run across 3,324 page boards (2026-10-19) against a 50-minute timeout — no run could finish, and every one hammered Gupy. What it costs now is one listing request per board plus a detail per NEW posting: ~1,700 a day on average before the 2026-09-30 outage (weekday peaks ~3,900, weekends under 200). `BODY_REFRESH_DAYS` is what re-reads an edited body. The first run after this lands faces every posting published during the 19-day outage (on the order of 30k details) and will time out a few times, which is safe: each finished board is saved and its postings leave the unseen set.
- **An unknown page is a 404 (`"Career Page not Found"`), a live page with nothing published is `{"data":[]}`.** The first fails the board; the second is a zero-yield success that licenses no sweep.
- **The canonical posting URL is `https://<page>.gupy.io/jobs/<id>`** — what the career page links to. The portal's `/job/<base64>?jobBoardSource=gupy_portal` form stored before the rekey is overwritten on the next crawl.

**jobleads traps** (verified live 2026-08-30):

- **Cloudflare-fingerprint-gated end to end**: plain Go/curl → 403 "Attention Required"; Chrome-fingerprint → 200 keyless, no cookie. The adapter rides `fingerprintHTTP` (the meta/bayt/gulftalent/uber cluster); the POST-only search is what gave `PostJSON`/`fpPostHeaders` their first consumer.
- **The search endpoint IS the API** (`POST /job-search/search`, the site's own frontend call); listing content arrives client-side in a Nuxt SPA, detail over `GET /api/v3/job/detailsForAppNew/{locale}/{jobId}` (endpoint table in the frontend's `logger.*.js` chunk).
- **`totalResultCount` is the current page's count** (`preciseResultCount` always true) — cannot paginate or detect truncation; the walk ends on an empty page or at `jobleadsMaxPages`.
- **Window deep (~20-30k), volatile, off-topic past ~p50**: full at p200 and empty at p210 on one query; on-topic density 96% (p1) → 7% (p25) → ~0% (p50) on "Frontend Developer" IT. `jobleadsMaxPages` = 50 is both the loop-safety bound (adp: a tenant that ignores $skip must not loop) and the relevance cutoff. `fullCatalog` stays unsound; the 14-day `sweepGrace` absorbs the partial walk — what a keyword's cap cuts reappears in another keyword's window.
- **The vdb ranking rotates between runs** (99/100 same ids, different order, on two identical page-1 requests) — the whole case for the 14-day `sweepGrace`: 48h would phantom-close and reopen drifted postings, writing phantom removals into `job_daily_stats`.
- **Detail paywalled for logged-out callers twice**: `description` always "" and `company` = "Solo per membri registrati". Company comes from the SEARCH feed; the body is assembled from the public jobSummary/responsibilities/qualifications/benefits in render order.
- **`jobLocationType` is an array** (in_person/hybrid/remote): first-wins, in_person → onsite (not `workplaceTypeMode`, which spells "on-site"). `contractType` is structured but unmapped (no freehire value).
- **Salary stated without a period** (minSalary/maxSalary/salaryCurrency) — per the remotedotcom rule, NO salary is mapped; enrichment decides.
- **A keyword is the board, a region scopes it**: `{"alpha2Country":"IT"}` alone filters results (geo circle optional) — a board row's `Board` field carries the keyword and `Region` the alpha-2 code, added via `cmd/add-board` (there is no board file — see the block AGENTS.md's "no `sources/` directory" note).
- **A cold run is tens of hours, not minutes.** One board's walk is up to `jobleadsMaxPages` (50) pages of 100, and a first crawl details every posting it lists: 19 onboarded keyword boards × (50-page walk + up to 5,000 new-post detail calls) ≈ 96,000 requests at the shared ~1 req/s pace ≈ 24h worst case. The 14-day `sweepGrace` means nothing is falsely closed while that first pass creeps across runs, but a `Type=oneshot` timer will cut a cold run short regardless — run the first crawl of a newly added board by hand rather than relying on the schedule to finish it.
- **The feed id is NOT the detail key**: the catalog id is a 32-hex variant-scoped handle; the slug hash is 33 hex (its canonical variant = feed id plus one leading char — one live posting carried `external-4964a963…`, variant `external-cde1148…`, slug `ecde1148…`). The detail keys on the SLUG hash only — keying it on the feed id 404s everything (hit on the first prod crawl; body-less rows re-hydrated via `HydrationRetryWindow`). The feed id stays the dedup key.
- **No origin/referer needed** — the generic `fpPostHeaders` set suffices (verified by deleting both headers).

**jobappnetwork traps** (all verified live 2026-09-06):

- **The vendor is talentReef; jobappnetwork.com is only its applicant-facing brand.** `apply.jobappnetwork.com` serves talentReef's React SPA (`<title>talentReef</title>`), and its own bundle is what names the real API host — `prod-kong.internal.talentreef.com/apply`. The host's own name says "internal", but it resolves publicly and answers an unauthenticated cross-origin request (`access-control-allow-origin: *`); nothing about the SPA's own network calls needs a session, a cookie or a key.
- **The listing is a raw Elasticsearch proxy, and `clientId` is a query FILTER, not a path scope.** `POST .../apply/proxy-es/search-en-us/posting/_search` with body `{"query":{"bool":{"filter":[{"term":{"clientId":<id>}},{"term":{"internalOrExternal":"externalOnly"}}]}},"from":<n>,"size":<n>}` returns exactly one employer's postings when the filter is present — confirmed against two clients (a 2,729-posting fleet and a 1-posting one) — and a query with NO filter at all (`match_all`) returns postings across the whole platform. The filter is therefore load-bearing on every request this adapter builds, never optional.
- **`internalOrExternal` must be filtered in the query, not dropped after the fact.** Every sampled posting carries exactly `externalOnly` or `internalOnly`; an internal-transfer-only posting has no business reaching a public job board, and pushing the exclusion into the request means it can never cross the wire in the first place, rather than relying on mapping code remembering to check a flag.
- **`hits.total` is exact and stable**, the same family as Dayforce's `maxCount` and EDJOIN's `totalRecords` rather than SEEK's page-size-dependent `totalCount` — confirmed unchanged across page sizes on the sampled boards, so plain `from`/`size` pagination bounds the walk with no need for `search_after`. The largest board sampled (2,729) is far short of Elasticsearch's 10,000-result default window.
- **The listing carries the whole posting body.** `description` is full HTML matching what the posting page itself renders, with no truncation observed on any sampled posting — so, like Dayforce, this is not a `HydratingSource`.
- **One `clientId` is one employer, not an aggregator.** Every posting sampled under one `clientId` shared the same `clientName` (a company or a franchise operator running many locations under one or more brands), so `Job.Company` is the board's configured company, the Dayforce/Gusto model, not Crelate's per-posting one.
- **The catalogue skews hourly/frontline (restaurant and retail franchises) but is not exclusively that.** Full-text probes against the whole index turned up real corporate/engineering postings at non-restaurant clients ("Senior DO-178 Software Engineer" at an aerospace supplier, "Software Quality Assurance Engineer" at a furniture retailer's corporate office) alongside thousands of shift-lead and crew-member roles — the same "generic ATS carries the whole company" shape `catalogue_fit.go` exists for, needing no special handling beyond the ordinary non-tech-title rejection.
- **A discovered risk, not this adapter's to fix: the index is a shared multi-tenant cluster with weak scoping.** An unfiltered query reads across every talentReef client, and one document surfaced during this research was clearly third-party-injected content unrelated to any real posting — evidence the cluster has been probed before. Irrelevant to correctness as long as every request here carries the `clientId` filter, but a reason never to build a request from unscoped input.
- **No adapter-scale request volume was sent during this research** — unlike most other entries in this file, this one does NOT claim "no metering observed". Ship without a pacer (the default for an unmeasured provider) and watch the first real boards' `board_health` rows before onboarding many at once.

**gr8people traps** (all verified live 2026-09-06, across `etrade.gr8people.com`,
`ardene.gr8people.com` and `batesville.workgr8.com`):

- **`gr8people.com` and `workgr8.com` are one vendor, not two.** Both career-site hosts serve the
  byte-identical Next.js "app-career-site" bundle from the same asset CDN
  (`assets.gr8people.com`), the same GraphQL schema, and the same session-token mechanism —
  confirmed by running the identical query against a tenant on each domain and getting the same
  response shape back. This is the Factorial/FactorialHR case (`factorial.go`,
  `atsboard/board.go`'s `factorial`/`factorialhr` rows): one adapter, one provider key
  (`gr8people`), `modeHost` on both domain labels.
- **The board's session token comes from the tenant's OWN `/jobs` page, and it is tenant-scoped.**
  A GET of `https://<host>/jobs` is server-rendered and its `__NEXT_DATA__` script embeds
  `"token":"eyJ..."` (ES256 JWT, `iss: auth-service`, ~5h lifetime measured from `iat`/`exp`).
  Decoding two tenants' tokens shows different `org` claims (`db63c8` for etrade, `0f3be7` for
  ardene, `392f45` for batesville) — the token is NOT portable across hosts, so it must be minted
  fresh per board, from that board's own host, never cached or shared.
- **The search call is GraphQL, not REST**: `POST https://<host>/graphql`,
  `Authorization: Bearer <token>`, operation `searchJobs` aliasing the schema's
  `searchJobPostings(query, start, first, after, sort, filters)` field. The bundle actually ships
  TWO backends behind the same alias — `searchJobPostings` (the vanilla path) and
  `searchGoogleJobDiscovery` (gated by a `google-job-discovery` feature flag, `false` on every
  tenant sampled) — this adapter reads the vanilla one only.
- **The query already answers only what a real visitor sees, with an EMPTY filters object.**
  Every posting returned across all three tenants was `status: "OPEN"`, `postType: "EXTERNAL"`
  with `filters: {}` (no actual filter clause set) — unlike `jobappnetwork`'s shared Elasticsearch
  proxy, there is no equivalent unscoped-read risk to guard against here, so the adapter never
  sets a filter clause of its own.
- **Pagination is Relay-style cursors (`after`/`endCursor`/`hasNextPage`), not an exact total to
  chase.** `totalCount` is reported but the walk stops on `hasNextPage: false` or an empty `nodes`
  page — confirmed by paging a 173-posting tenant end to end with no gaps or overlap. Passing an
  EMPTY STRING as `after` on the first page is rejected by the platform's cursor decoder; the key
  must be omitted entirely until a real cursor exists.
- **`places.nodes[].name` is already a formatted "City, Region[, Postal], Country" string per
  place** — there is no separate structured country-code field on the posting — so it is passed
  through as free text (the `apploi`/`hrmdirect` posture) and `Countries` is left to the location
  dictionary. `workplaceType` (`ON_SITE`/`REMOTE`/`HYBRID`) is a genuine closed enum, confirmed by
  a validator function in the platform's own bundle, so it is a real structured work-mode signal
  unlike the free-typed `positionType.name`.
- **No adapter-scale request volume was sent during this research** — like jobappnetwork, this
  entry does NOT claim "no metering observed". Ship without a pacer and watch `board_health`.

**werecruit traps** (all verified live 2026-09-06, across `idiap`, `axiom-services`,
`alcatel-submarine-networks` and `broadpeak`):

- **The whole listing is embedded server-side, and there is no pagination to walk.** A GET of
  `careers.werecruit.io/<locale>/<tenant>` is a classic server-rendered page (jQuery/Bootstrap, no
  SPA framework) whose `<script>` block assigns `window.allOffers = [...]` — the tenant's ENTIRE
  open-postings list. The bundled `offers-widget.js` confirms this is the whole dataset, not a
  first page: it sets `this.allOffers = window.allOffers` once and every filter/paginate
  operation afterward is a client-side `.slice()` over that in-memory array — no "load more"
  request exists. The extraction takes the FIRST `window.allOffers =` match on the page — a page
  carrying an earlier decoy occurrence (a comment, an unrelated inline snippet) would silently
  decode the wrong value; not observed on any tenant sampled, and the same first-match posture
  every regex-anchored embedded-token adapter in this file already takes (cornerstone, gr8people).
- **The locale segment is load-bearing, not cosmetic — the Dayforce-culture trap on a new
  platform.** `/fr/idiap` answers ZERO postings while `/en/idiap` answers four; IDIAP's site is
  configured for `en-gb` only (its own `hreflang="en-gb"`/`x-default` tags, no alternate-language
  link). Asking an unconfigured locale answers an empty listing, not an error — so the board
  keeps the locale (`<locale>/<tenant>`) rather than folding it off.
- **Unlike Dayforce, a multi-locale tenant does NOT slice its postings per locale — unioning is
  unnecessary here.** `broadpeak` (configured for both en and fr) returns the exact SAME two
  posting ids under either locale, each stating its own full `Languages` array. One valid locale
  already gives the tenant's whole catalogue; there is no per-locale subset to union like
  Dayforce's translations.
- **`Address_State` is a two-letter ISO COUNTRY code, not a US state**, despite the field name —
  confirmed `"FR"` on every French posting sampled and `"CH"` on IDIAP's, never a US abbreviation
  on a non-US address. Read through `countryFromCode`.
- **The listing carries no description at all.** Every field this adapter maps is on
  `window.allOffers`; the body lives only on the posting's own page, in a server-rendered
  `<div class="description rich-text …">` block (confirmed on an 88 KB detail page) — a plain
  exact class-token match, distinct from an unrelated `description-blocks` div further down the
  same page. Every board found is small (single digits to low tens of postings), so this is the
  Factorial shape (hydrate every posting every crawl) rather than a `HydratingSource`.
- **`TimeTranslated` ("Full time"/"Part time") is the platform's own schedule label** and maps
  cleanly to `full_time`/`part_time` on every posting sampled; `TypeTranslated` (e.g. "Temporary
  contract - 30 months") combines a contract-type enum with a duration into one free-text
  sentence with no clean split observed, so it is left unmapped.
- **No adapter-scale request volume was sent during this research** — like jobappnetwork and
  gr8people, this entry does NOT claim "no metering observed". Ship without a pacer and watch
  `board_health`.

**Hacker News traps** (`hackernews`; verified live 2026-09-14):

- **Find the thread by the `whoishiring` account's author tag, never by free text.** `search_by_date?tags=story,author_whoishiring` lists that account's stories newest-first; a free-text query is RANKED and an unrelated recent story containing the words outranks the thread once it ages (career-ops hit exactly this). The account also posts "Who wants to be hired?" and "Freelancer?" threads every month, so the title is checked against `hackernewsHiringTitle`.
- **A crawl reads the two newest hiring threads whole** (this month's, still filling, and last month's, still live in the first weeks) — one `items/<id>` request each, ~500 KB, every top-level comment with its HTML in one document; 417 postings from the two live threads at the time this was measured. Replies are discussion and are not read. Any thread failing fails the crawl: the provider is `fullCatalog`, so a post from a thread that aged out of the window closes by source, which nothing else would do for a boardless feed.
- **The post format is a convention, not a schema.** The first paragraph (up to the first unclosed `<p>`) is read as the "Company | Role | Location | ..." header; a post with fewer than two pipe segments names no employer this catalogue could file it under and is DROPPED. The location is the first later segment that is not a commitment word, a URL or a salary. The apply link is the first anchor in the post, falling back to the comment's permalink; the whole comment is the body. Segment order varies in practice (some posts lead with the location) and that noise is left to the title dictionaries rather than guessed at.
- **A header opening with a ROLE instead of an employer shifts every later segment by one** ("Senior Software Engineer, Frontend | New York, NY (In-Office) | Full-time" would otherwise file the role as the company and the location as the role) — detected by a seniority word paired with a role noun in the first segment, and dropped rather than corrected, since nothing in that shape recovers the real employer. A seniority word ALONE is not evidence (`Lead Bank`, `Chief Industries` are real employers), so only the pair triggers the drop.
- **The salary-segment skip decodes a RUNE, not a byte.** € and £ are multi-byte in UTF-8; slicing a segment's first byte to check it against `$€£` cuts into the middle of a multi-byte symbol and never matches, so a €/£ salary segment would otherwise be mistaken for the location. `startsWithCurrencySymbol` decodes the first rune instead.

**IBM traps** (`ibm`; live-verified 2026-10-15):

- **careers.ibm.com cannot be crawled, so the posting pages are never fetched.** It is IBM's Avature tenant, and every path on it — the sitemap and each `JobDetail` page — answers `202` with `x-amzn-waf-action: challenge`, an AWS WAF JavaScript challenge, to a plain client. The un-fronted `ibmglobal.avature.net/en_US/careers/sitemap.xml` answers, but lists 45 utility routes and no postings, so the `avature` adapter cannot serve IBM either. The catalogue comes from IBM's site-search API instead (`POST www-api.ibm.com/search/api/v2`, `appId: careers`, `scopes: [careers2]`) — the same call the ibm.com/careers/search page makes — and its `body` field is the FULL posting text, so the crawl is 25 POSTs and no detail request.
- **`_source` must name every field.** `true` and `["*"]` both return hits with no `_source` at all. The `field_keyword_NN` slots are generic; what each holds is listed beside `ibmSourceFields` in `ibm.go`. `description` is a ~250-char snippet, not the body.
- **The UI's sort ties on every document.** An empty query sorted by `_score, pageviews` returns the same set in a different order on each request, so offset paging would skip and repeat postings. The adapter sorts by `id`, a unique keyword per document. Sorting by `url` or `field_text_01` is a 400 (text fields, no fielddata).
- **`size` tops out at 100** (101 is a 400) and `from` at < 9,990 (a 400 beyond). `hits.total` is exact (`relation: "eq"`) and equalled the distinct job ids read (2,434 of 2,434), which is what earns `fullBoardListing`: any page failing, a non-exact total, or a count that disagrees with the total fails the crawl.
- **The body is flattened and carries a suffix.** Newlines are gone (bullets survive inline as `•`, `·`, `*`, `●`, `▪`, often between no-break spaces), and the posting's own facet values are appended after the text — country, area, work mode, level, city, legal entity, the non-empty ones joined by single spaces. That held for all 2,434 postings; the adapter strips exactly that suffix. About a fifth of the bodies double-encode the right single quote (`â€™`), often next to correct ones, so the whole string cannot be re-decoded; that one sequence was the only mojibake in the catalogue and is the one repaired.
- **The city facet is `"BUDAPEST, HU"` or the placeholder `"Multiple Cities"`**, which names no place — the country alone is used then. The country facet is a NAME, and `"Korea, Republic of"` (17 postings) is one the dictionary does not read, so the country falls back to the city's alpha-2 code. Work mode is `Hybrid`, `Remote` or empty — on-site is never stated, so empty stays unknown.

**Garena traps** (`garena`; live-verified 2026-10-15):

- **The listing's `description` is not the posting.** `POST careers.garena.com/api/job/list` returns the whole catalogue in one response (86 postings, no pagination), but each item's `description` is only the summary paragraph, and it was EMPTY on 53 of the 86. The responsibilities and requirements are `job_description` and `job_requirements` on `GET /api/job/detail/<id>`, so every posting is fetched. 86 details at 8 workers took 9s with no refusal, so there is no pacing.
- **A withdrawn posting answers 200 with `{"job": {}}`**, not a 404. An empty `job.id` is therefore the platform's own "gone" and is dropped; a transport failure is an `Unreadable` marker.
- **`office` changes nothing in the API and everything in the URL.** Every office code, a made-up one included, lists the same postings, but the posting page lives at `/<office>/careers/<id>`, and the site's own links and `og:url` use `global`, so that is the URL stored. `apply_url` was empty on every posting, because applications happen on the same page.
- **The only structured fields are the three tags.** `location` holds one city or `Remote` (no posting carried two). `job_type` maps to seniority (`Internship`, `Entry Level`, `Experienced (Team Lead)`; `Individual Contributor` names no level). `job_category` is left to the dictionaries; `Engineering and Technology` spans too many disciplines to pin one. No posting date is published anywhere.

**Taiwanese hardware makers** (`mediatek`, `realtek`, `foxconn`, `asus`, `quanta`; live-verified 2026-10-25):

- **Chinese titles carry no category, so the source's own category is what files them.** `internal/dict/classify` reads no Chinese, and a posting with no category and no `is_tech` is excluded from search (`search.CategoryUnresolved`). Each adapter maps its site's category or recruiting track onto `vocab.CategoryValues` where it names ONE discipline, and sets `IsTechHint` for an R&D bucket that spans several (Realtek's 系統設計, ASUS's 研究發展/Data, Foxconn's server and communications R&D tracks). Buckets that span functions (sales-and-marketing, the graduate and campaign tracks) are left unmapped and those postings stay invisible until a dictionary reads them — a deliberate gap, not an oversight. Foxconn has no category field at all, only its recruiting tracks, so most of its catalogue is in that gap. MediaTek titles are English and the dictionary reads them.
- **Place names are Traditional Chinese.** `twplace.go` maps the names these sites state (台灣, 新竹科學園區, 林口研發中心, 萬錦市) to English and a country; `cncity.go` is Simplified and cannot serve them. 臺 and 台 are the same word and are folded.
- **Realtek's listing is capped at 200 rows**, the unfiltered dump included, while the board holds more (279). The search's `xp` filter splits it into two disjoint halves (`xp=0`: no experience asked, `xp=1`: a year or more); a half that returns 200 fails the crawl. An unknown `JobOppId` on the detail endpoint answers 200 with every field null — the not-found signal is a null `JobTitle`.
- **MediaTek's posting page needs the `NEXT_LOCALE=en` cookie.** Without it the locale middleware answers 307 to the same path, and a client without a jar follows it until it gives up. The search API needs no cookie. Underscores in its titles are separators ("2027 Campus_IT Engineer") and are spaced out.
- **ASUS pages five postings at a time and ignores every page-size parameter**, so the listing is ~140 requests. The language is a server-minted session cookie no URL parameter selects, so the crawl reads zh-TW. An unknown `sn` answers 500, so a withdrawn posting reads as a failed detail and is deferred until the listing drops it.
- **Foxconn and Quanta are one response each** (968 and 244 postings), and both servers ignore paging and query parameters. Foxconn's body is split over numbered `desc_N` fields whose use varies per posting (`desc_4` is always the duties); its location is only a country, or 其他 ("other"), which names nothing. Neither claims `fullBoardListing`: a single response states no total to check, and Quanta's once came back with 8 of its 244 vacancies, HTTP 200 and well-formed JSON.
- **Coverage measured on the first live crawl**: MediaTek 621 postings (502 filed by the source category or tech hint, 67 more by the English title dictionary), Realtek 279 (216 + 3), ASUS 688 (406 + 73), Foxconn 917 (241 + 51; 625 unplaced), Quanta 244.
- **Not built**: TSMC's Taiwan catalogue (Avature, `careers.tsmc.com`) sits behind a Cloudflare managed challenge from every host we have; its overseas subsidiaries are already crawled through `successfactors` `ro.careers.tsmc.com`. Delta's own portal listed 13 postings; its SmartRecruiters and iCIMS tenants are already crawled. Synopsys runs TalentBrew in front of Avature: `careers.synopsys.com` listed 698 postings while the `avature` board `synopsys.avature.net` held 668 open, so it is already covered.

**Dassault Systèmes traps** (`dassault`; live-verified 2026-10-15):

- **The Taleo section behind it is not public.** Every apply link points at `talentacquisition.3ds.com/careersection/qa/`, but that section's search page redirects to `www.3ds.com/careers` and a `jobdetail.ftl` answers 302, so the `taleo` adapter fails with "no portalNo". The catalogue comes from 3ds.com's own Exalead card search (`GET www.3ds.com/apisearch/card_search_api`), which returns XML served as `application/javascript`. Each card's `content_summary` is the posting's full body, so the crawl makes no detail requests. robots.txt disallows `*/search*`; `/apisearch/card_search_api` contains no `/search` segment.
- **Both refinements are required.** Without `r=f/card_content_type/career` the index is 3ds.com's whole card corpus. Without `r=f/card_content_categories_facet/cards language/en` every posting comes back once per site language: 8,364 cards for 697 requisitions. The English set alone covers all 697 distinct card ids. `hf` sets the page size: 100 serves 100, and 1000 served all 697 in one answer. The adapter uses 100, which makes 7 requests and ran in 3s.
- **`estimated` is not a completeness signal.** At `hf=100`, `estimated="true"` came back on 23 of 24 requests even though `nhits` stayed 697 and the walk read exactly 697 distinct cards. At the default page size it read `false`. `fullBoardListing` therefore rests on the distinct count equalling `nhits`, plus any page failure failing the crawl.
- **`content_categories` is a flat `Label/value` string** whose values contain spaces and commas. All 697 postings carried the same six labels in the same order (Category, Type, Country, City, Products, Year), and the adapter anchors on that order.
  - `City` repeats the country and often a region code (`India, MH, Pune`), so the city is its last comma-separated part.
  - Two `Country` names carry a qualifier the dictionary cannot read: `Korea, Republic of`, and `Taiwan,China`, which the location parser reads as TWO countries. Both fall back to the part before the comma.
  - `Year` is the page's "Experience level" (`4 to 5 years`, `More than 15 years`, `Not specified`) and sets `ExperienceYearsMin`.
  - `content_start_datetime` is the page's "Posted on".
- **`Products` names a brand, not an employer.** 62 postings are `MEDIDATA` and 32 are `OUTSCALE`; both are Dassault subsidiaries. They stay under the configured company, because the facet labels a product line rather than a hiring entity.

**Python.org traps** (`pythonorg`; live-verified 2026-10-15):

- **The RSS feed is not the catalogue.** `/jobs/feed/rss/` carries bodies but only the newest 20 postings, against 28 live. The listing is the server-rendered `/jobs/` (25 cards a page): page 2 held 3 cards and `?page=3` answered **404**, which the "which page failed decides" walk reads as the end. Not `fullCatalog`: nothing states a total to check the walk against. `robots.txt` disallows nothing under `/jobs/`, and the board answers our own User-Agent.
- **No schema.org JobPosting.** The posting page's only ld+json is the site-wide `WebSite` block, so the body is `div.job-description`; its leading "Job Title" section repeats the title and is dropped. Title, employer, place and date come from the listing card (`span.listing-company-name` holds the link, then the employer as bare text after a `<br>`, with a "New" badge span on recent cards).
- **The "Web" contact link is not an apply URL.** Of the 28 postings, 13 point it at the employer's homepage, 3 at one recruiter's personal LinkedIn profile, one at an Adzuna copy; only 2 named an ATS freehire crawls (one Ashby, one JazzHR `applytojob.com`). Job.URL is therefore always the python.org posting page, and the board's ATS overlap is too small to justify a coverage gate — a cold run is 30 requests in about a second.
- **The "Restrictions" flags are form defaults, not facts.** "No telecommuting" sits on a posting whose location reads "Warsaw (fully remote), Poland"; it is left in the body and not mapped to a work mode. The location is free text ("Remote, Remote/Worldwide, Remote/Worldwide", "100% Remote, USA Only") and is left to the location dictionary. Agencies post too ("mindIT HR Agency (on behalf of a confidential client)") and are stored under that name.
- **Every live posting was filed under "Developer / Engineer"**, the board is Python-only, so `IsTechHint` is set. No metering seen over ~70 requests at our UA, a full 8-way concurrent crawl included.

**Potok traps** (`potok`; verified live 2026-10-14):

- **`*.potok.io` is a wildcard in DNS and in the certificate**, so neither names a tenant; only `GET <slug>.potok.io/open/jobs/` does (200 against 404). The tenant list was found by probing guessed slugs (780 tenants, 130 with live postings).
- **A tenant with nothing open still renders `.postings-wrapper`, empty.** A 200 without it is not the career page, and `list` fails the board rather than reading it as empty.
- **The listing groups postings, and the group is a department — except on a hub.** On `tehnopark-skolkovo` each group is a resident company, so that board must be added as a hub (`cmd/add-board --hub`); the adapter then files each posting under its group and falls back to the configured company for a blank one.
- **Neither page states a salary or a publish date.** The posting page's headline renders the city and the schedule as two `sort-by-time` categories in that order; the schedule is an hh-style picklist and only `Удаленная работа` says anything about the arrangement, so only it sets `WorkMode`.
- **No throttling at ~12 req/s from 3 workers**, the only rate measured, so `limitedPotokGetter` holds the run to 3 in flight.

**FriendWork traps** (`friendwork`; verified live 2026-10-14):

- **`page`/`perPage` are ignored** — the response says `perPage: 20` and carries 151 postings, and `page=2` returns the same 151 — so one request is the whole board.
- **The API meters simultaneity:** two parallel workers drew 429s, sequential ~2 req/s did not. `limitedFriendWorkGetter` runs one request at a time at that pace; harvest probes through the same registry, so they inherit it.
- **Salary is monthly by the platform's own rendering** (`<from> <to> <currency>/month`, read off the portal bundle), so `SalaryPeriod` is `month`; `0` means unstated, and `currency` is a numeric enum (0 RUB … 7 UZS).
- **Unset enums are the STRING `"-1"`**, not a number or null; they map to nothing. Dates carry no zone and are read as UTC.
- **Tokens are case-insensitive** (`TeDo` = `tedo`); the catalogue's unique index on `lower(board)` already folds them. Many portals still list postings years old — the platform says they are published, and the listing is the only evidence there is.

**Torre traps** (`torre`; live-verified 2026-10-15):

- **The search API is gone and the search moved behind robots.txt.** `POST search.torre.co/opportunities/_search` answers 400 `{"meta":{"message":"Invalid request"}}` to every body, `{}` included; torre.ai's job search redirects into `/app`, which robots.txt disallows (as it does `/api/` and `/search/jobs?*`). What robots.txt leaves open, and names, is the sitemap index and the `/post/<id>-<slug>` pages.
- **The sitemap index is the live catalogue, nearly.** 34 shards (`sitemap-en-N`, `sitemap-es-N`) named 2,440 distinct postings; shards are pruned as postings close (the 2023 shard still names one posting, whose `validThrough` lies ahead). Nearly is not exactly: 12-13 of 2,440 were closed (the page state reads `status="closed"`) and such a page carries NO JobPosting, so the posting is deferred and re-requested every run. Hence no `fullCatalog`. Two shards the index names (`sitemap--1.xml`, `sitemap--2.xml`) answer 404 and are skipped; any other shard failing fails the run, because a skipped shard reads as its postings closing.
- **The external id is the 8-character id before the slug.** Four entries carry no slug at all, and one id appeared in two shards.
- **The JobPosting description is escaped twice.** Once JSON-decoded it still holds `\uXXXX` for every non-ASCII character, `\/` and `\t` (37 of 61 sampled), so it is decoded again as a JSON string. Postings Torre crawled from elsewhere (identifier `spider:external:…`, all 67 from Blue Recruitment Personnel) instead carry their markup entity-escaped inside a `<p>`, which is unescaped once; their identifier is not the page id, so the id comes from the URL.
- **A hidden salary is the placeholder 1-1000.** Checked against the page state on 61 postings: every `baseSalary` of exactly `minValue: 1, maxValue: 1000` belonged to a compensation with `visible: false` and different real figures; every other range equalled the visible one. `maxValue: 0` means no upper bound.
- **Places.** `TELECOMMUTE` is remote and its places are the countries it hires from (up to 27). A remote posting with no place is the site's "Remote - Anywhere", whose `applicantLocationRequirements` is a fixed 49-country filler and is not read. An on-site place's `streetAddress` is sometimes the city ("Butte") and sometimes a street ("Cra. 14 # 97-63"), so it is used only when it holds no digit. One posting whose body says "Hybrid / New York" is `TELECOMMUTE`; the platform's flag is taken as stated.
- **A broad marketplace, mostly remote LatAm and US.** The title dictionary calls 276 of 2,427 technical and 133 confirmed non-technical; the latter are never stored, so their pages are re-bought every run (~133 requests). No posting page named an external apply URL (`directApply: true`), so Job.URL is the Torre page and there is no coverage gate — nor could there be, since nothing names the employer before the page is read.
- **No metering seen**: a full cold crawl (36 sitemap requests, ~2,440 pages at 8-way) took ~17 minutes with no refusal; steady state is the sitemaps plus new postings (~30 a day) and the re-bought rejects.

**PeopleSoft Candidate Gateway traps** (`peoplesoft`; live-verified 2026-10-15, on careers.dc.gov, cg.sandia.gov, employment.case.edu, erecruit.umsystem.edu):

- **A board is `<host>/<site>/<portal>/<node>`, and all four vary.** The search page is `https://<host>/psc/<site>/<portal>/<node>/c/HRS_HRAM_FL.HRS_CG_SEARCH_FL.GBL?Page=HRS_APP_SCHJOB_FL&Action=U&FOCUS=Applicant&SiteId=1`. Most tenants are `EMPLOYEE/HRMS`, but the University of Missouri System is `tamext/COLUM/HRMS` and Phoenix `hcmprodtam/EMPLOYEE/COP_TAM`. A wrong site answers HTTP 200 with the one line "Site name is not valid", which has no `win0` form and therefore fails the crawl instead of reading as an empty board. Every tenant is on its own host, so `atsboard` cannot recognise one from a URL.
- **The list grows by a form POSTBACK, inside the session the first GET opened.** "Load more" re-submits the whole `win0` form (ICSID and ICStateNum among it) with `ICAction=HRS_AGNT_RSLT_I$hdown$0`. Each answer is the WHOLE list so far, 50 rows more each time, so the last page read holds every row. The adapter therefore runs on a cookie client (`cookieSessionSource`). The ~100-row anonymous cap a third party described was not seen: Sandia read 215 of 215 rows and Missouri 600 of 600.
- **The stated count is ROWS, not postings.** One opening can be listed twice, with identical rows (Missouri: 600 rows, 514 distinct openings; Sandia 215 and 201). The walk proves completeness against the row count (`fullBoardListing`) and then dedups by opening id. If a postback adds no row before the count is reached, or the postback cap is hit, the crawl fails.
- **The posting page is a plain GET by opening id** (`Page=HRS_APP_JBPST_FL&JobOpeningId=<id>&PostingSeq=1`).
