The first request answers a 302 to itself that sets the session cookie, so the URL works as a public link. An opening the page does not serve lands on the SEARCH page under HTTP 200, so the page counts only when its own `HRS_SCH_WRK2_HRS_JOB_OPENING_ID` matches. Case Western lists one opening (14685) that no `PostingSeq` from 1 to 3 serves. It is deferred on every run and costs one request each time.
- **The body is the `HRS_SCH_PSTDSC_DESCRLONG$<n>` sections** (with headings in `HRS_SCH_WRK_DESCR100$<n>lbl`), joined in order. The list's "Posted" date is M/D/YYYY on every tenant that shows one. Western University shows none.
- **No `robots.txt` on any tenant** (each answers 404). No metering was seen at 4-way posting-page fan-out. The first crawl of Missouri's 514 postings took 9 minutes. After that a crawl is the list walk (~12 postbacks, up to 2.5 MB each) plus the new postings only.

**BeeSite traps** (`beesite`; live-verified 2026-10-15, on the Mercedes-Benz, Porsche, Daimler Truck, UKE, Allianz, BKM and BAM search hosts):

- **A board is the tenant's `*.app.beesite.de` SEARCH host**, which is not the career site. jobs.mercedes-benz.com searches through `mercedes-benz-beesite-production-gjb.app.beesite.de`, Porsche through `porsche-beesite-production-gjb`, Daimler Truck through `daimler-truck-beesite-production-gjb`. A host named `…-frontend` or `…-intranet` is a different thing (a portal frontend, or the internal-only jobs: Mercedes's intranet host states 2,399 postings linking to `internal-jobs.app.corpintra.net`). Nobody pastes these hosts, so `atsboard` does not try to recognise them. To find one, read a tenant career page's source for `app.beesite.de`, or Common Crawl's `*.beesite.de`.
- **`GET /search?data=<url-encoded JSON>` is keyless and answers whole records.** `SearchParameters.FirstItem` is 1-based and `CountItem` 100 is served. Leaving out `MatchedObjectDescriptor` returns every field. `SearchResultCountAll` is exact, and `LanguageCode` EN and DE return the same set. The default order paged all seven tenants without a repeat or a gap; no sort is sent. A total that moves mid-walk (Mercedes went 2,755 → 2,761 in minutes) fails that crawl rather than passing a skipped posting as complete.
- **Only some tenants put the body in the record.** Mercedes-Benz carries `PositionFormattedDescription[].Tasks`/`Qualifications`, so its 2,761 postings cost no posting request. Porsche, Daimler Truck, UKE and BKM carry none (asking for the field by name returns nothing), so their body comes from the posting page (`PositionURI`, the branded portal page), read only for postings the catalogue lacks.
- **The posting page's JobPosting is malformed on some tenants.** Porsche's block opens with a stray template token (`entryLevel [{...}]`), so it is read from its first bracket. Its `description` is entity-encoded HTML. A posting handled outside the portal (apply by email) renders no JobPosting at all, and its body is read from `div.jobad-dynamic-html-container` instead.
- **Facets are named, not coded, and per tenant.** `PositionSchedule` reads "Full time", "full-time" or "Full time (part time possible)"; `CareerLevel` "Internship" marks an internship. `PositionLocation` carries `CityName`, `CountryName` and an ISO `CountryCode`.
- **`career-ops` names TKMS, Heckler & Koch, Rheinmetall and Deutsche Bahn beside BeeSite, but none of them is on it.** Their providers read a `subclient` job-board API, server-rendered Nuxt pages, Cornerstone TalentLink and an Avature-backed search respectively. Allianz's tenant (1,052) is entirely insurance-agency sales.
- No metering seen: Porsche's 755 posting pages at the shared 8-way fan-out took 2m46s. Mercedes's 28 search pages took 2m37s, since each 100-record page carries the bodies (~1 MB).

**Eploy traps** (`eploy`; live-verified 2026-10-15, on jobs.ocs.com, careers.pei.group, www.homeservejobs.co.uk, jobs.manchester.gov.uk, jobs.le.ac.uk, careers.allpay.net):

- **A board is a careers HOST.** It can be the employer's branded domain or its `<tenant>.eploy.net` host (often `<name>web.eploy.net`); both serve the same site. Only the eploy.net form can be derived from a URL, so `atsboard` recognises that one. A branded domain is invisible to it, like every vanity host.
- **The list is `/live-jobs.xml`**: a sitemap of every live vacancy in one unpaginated document. That avoids the results pager, which is an ASP.NET Web Forms postback. A branded site's sitemap commonly names the tenant's eploy.net page (careers.pei.group lists `peigroupweb.eploy.net/vacancies/...`). That is the same vacancy, so a `<loc>` on the board host or any `*.eploy.net` host is read, and nothing else is. The external id is the number in `/vacancies/<id>/<slug>.html`.
- **The sitemap lags the site.** On OCS, 51 of 1,583 listed vacancies answered 410 Gone. On HomeServe, three redirected to `vacancies/vacancy-not-found.aspx`, which is a 200 page without a vacancy. Both cases are deferred, never stored. `fullBoardListing` rests on the sitemap being one document: a failed or undecodable one fails the crawl, and so does a sitemap INDEX.
- **Not every template renders a JobPosting.** HomeServe's customised site has no ld+json, so the title comes from the page's `h1` (id ending `h1JobTitle`) and the body from `div.vac-details__description`, which every template measured carries. ld+json, when present, adds the date, address and employment type. `employmentType` is tenant free text, and its usual value "Permanent" states tenure, not hours, so it is left unmapped. `baseSalary.value` is free text ("Grade 7 - £39,906 to £46,049 per annum") and is not read as structured pay.
- **No metering seen**: OCS's 1,583 vacancy pages at the shared 8-way fan-out took 4m51s with no 429. The sitemap is ~1 request per board.

**HiringRoom traps** (`hiringroom`; live-verified 2026-10-15, across 15 tenants including tatachile, stefaniniitsolutionsperu, grupopetersen, humanbrandconsulting):

- **A board is the tenant label** of `<tenant>.hiringroom.com`. There are two portal layouts. On the legacy one, `/jobs` renders cards. On the newer one, `/jobs` 302s to `/portal`, which lists only featured vacancies as schema.org JSON-LD. Neither page is the list: both render at most the first 20 vacancies.
- **The list is the portal's own pager, `POST /jobs/getVacanciesForPortal/<page>`**, form-encoded `selectedPage=<page>&typePortal=external`. It answers JSON with the page's cards as an HTML fragment (`data.htmlContent`) and the portal's `data.total_vacancies`. It serves both layouts. The same parameters as JSON or as a query string answer `"Los parámetros enviados son inválidos"`, hence the shared client's `PostFormJSON`. Leave out `typePortal` and the page comes back empty with a null total. The walk proves completeness against the total (`fullBoardListing`). A microsite portal (`<tenant>.hiringroom.com/jobs/microsite/<name>`, `typePortal=microsite`) is a different list and is not read.
- **Each card renders its tags twice** (desktop and mobile copies). The tags are icon-keyed picklists: `hr-Clock` gives the schedule (Full-time, Part time, Freelance, Pasantía, Eventual, No especificado), `hr-Company`/`hr-Remote` the work mode (Presencial, Híbrido, Remoto), and `hr-Campus` the level (Junior, Semi Senior, Senior; Jefe, Responsable, Gerencia and other management levels are left unmapped). `hr-Work-area` holds the platform's own area taxonomy, `<area> / <sub-area>`. "Tecnología, Sistemas y Telecomunicaciones" sets `IsTechHint` (TCS Chile 30/32, Stefanini 30/31).
- **The vacancy page has no JSON-LD and no date.** The body is the `div.job-description-content` sections ("Descripción del puesto", "Requisitos", "Beneficios"), each after an `h6` heading. A tenant's catch-all "Postulación en base" vacancy has an empty section: it is read with no body rather than deferred forever. The card's only date is relative ("Hace 7 meses"), so `PostedAt` stays nil. Old vacancies stay listed for years (humanbrandconsulting shows some marked "Hace 3 años").
- `robots.txt` is not served (the path answers the portal page). No metering was seen: humanbrandconsulting's 241 vacancies read in 33 s at the shared 8-way fan-out.

**Pandapé traps** (`pandape`, InfoJobs Brasil's ATS; live-verified 2026-10-23 from the fsn host, every one of the 3,347 tenants the sitemap names):

- **A board is the tenant label** of `<tenant>.pandape.infojobs.com.br`, one career site per employer — the gupy/hiringroom shape, not an aggregator. The same tenant answers on `<tenant>.pandape.catho.com.br` and `pandape.com.br` with the identical listing, so `atsboard` maps all three domains to one board and the adapter reads only the infojobs host. Staffing agencies (randstad-brasil, adecco, manpowergroup, gigroup1) are tenants too and post under their OWN name ("Randstad - Matriz" in every JobPosting sampled), so the board's company is the right attribution; there is no client name to recover.
- **Why not the sitemap.** robots.txt names a gzip sitemap index (115 shards, 113,583 URLs, 93,872 distinct vacancies, 3,347 tenants) and it would make a tempting boardless crawl, but it is not the catalogue: every tenant's own listing is a SUPERSET of its sitemap entries, and a large one. braspress lists 54 against 20 sitemap URLs, atacadao 2,119 against 976, randstad-brasil 3,827 against 3,033; across the fleet the listings carry **248,248** vacancies against the sitemap's 93,872 (38%). A sitemap-swept provider would miss most of the catalogue and, swept by source, close what it merely failed to list. 35 entries are host-less (`<loc>/Detail/3552612</loc>`), so they name no employer at all. The sitemap is used for DISCOVERY only (`cmd/harvest-boards pandape`).
- **The list is the career site's own pager, `POST /ListVacancies`**, form-encoded `PageNumber=<n>&PageSize=20`. It answers JSON: the page's cards as an HTML fragment (`view`), the tenant's total (`subtotal`) and `isLast`. `PageSize` above 20 is ignored. The walk ends only on `isLast` and fails unless the distinct vacancies reach the first page's `subtotal`; any page failing, an empty page before the end, and a stated total past the pager's 500-page window (see the LatAm section) all fail the crawl (`fullBoardListing`). An unknown tenant answers 404 with "A Página de Carreiras desta empresa não está mais disponível no Pandapé", wrapped as `ErrBoardGone`.
- **Cards are icon-keyed picklists**: `icon-location-pin-1` the place ("Guarulhos - SP", or "Todo Brasil" for a nationwide one), `icon-buildings`/`icon-building-house`/`icon-user-with-headphones` the work mode (Presencial / Híbrido / Home Office), `icon-sheet` the contract (Efetivo – CLT, Prestador de Serviços (PJ), Autônomo, Temporário, Estágio, Jovem Aprendiz, Outros). The vacancy page uses DIFFERENT icon names for the same facts, so facts are read from the card only.
- **The vacancy page (`/Detail/<id>`) carries a schema.org JobPosting** with `datePosted` (no offset — read as Brasília time, UTC-3), `jobLocation.address` (locality, region, `addressCountry` BR → `Countries`), `employmentType` (`full-time`/`partial-time`, the SCHEDULE: a PJ contract still says `full-time`, so the card's contract wins) and, on about two thirds of postings, `baseSalary` with `unitText: MONTH`. Its `description` replaces every emoji with `?`, so the body is the page's own `#description` HTML plus its structured "Requisitos"/"Valorizado" sections. A 404 is a posting gone; robots disallows only `/Apply/`, which the adapter never requests.
- **`empregos` is not a tenant.** `empregos.pandape.infojobs.com.br` (10,484 sitemap URLs, 11% of the sitemap) is the platform's bucket for vacancies posted as "Empresa com nome confidencial": its root 404s while its `/Detail` pages answer. It has no listing to prove completeness against and names no employer, so `atsboard` declines it and the harvest never proposes it.
- **One origin, and it answers a burst with 403, not 429.** The shared client's per-host backoff only sees 429, so the adapter paces every request of a run through one limiter at 2/s: that rate sustained for ~40 minutes drew no refusal, while ~6-7/s for a minute drew a run of 403s on listings and details alike, which cleared within seconds. The limiter is per PROCESS, so concurrent shards multiply the rate — run the provider UNSHARDED. A full listing pass is ~14.3k pages, ~2 hours at that rate; a first crawl of a tenant hydrates at most 300 vacancies (`pandapeMaxNewPerBoard`, ~2.5 minutes), so the largest tenants converge over several runs. The ~41k card titles sampled hit `classify.ConfirmedNonTech` zero times, so no hydrated body is thrown away by the catalogue filter.
- **Onboarding** is `go run ./cmd/harvest-boards pandape` (discovers tenants from the sitemap, probes each career site's `/Vacancies` page for its name and open count, reports — held to the same 2 req/s whatever `-pace` says, so ~30 minutes: its first run went out unpaced at ~35 req/s, the 403s it drew were read as absent boards, 886 of 3,420 came back live against the 3,334 the survey measured, and the next run's sitemap fetch was refused outright) then the same with `--apply` (inserts `pending`), then `go run ./cmd/schedule-board --provider=pandape --manage --heavy --timeout=10800s --cadence=12h --notes=... --apply`. A tenant whose label is longer than a DNS label allows (two in the 2026-10-23 sitemap) cannot be reached and is skipped by the probe.

**Pandapé LatAm traps** (`pandapelatam`, Computrabajo's deployment of the same ATS; live-verified 2026-10-24):

- **Same software, separate provider.** `<tenant>.pandape.computrabajo.com` serves the identical `POST /ListVacancies` pager, cards and `/Detail/<id>` pages, so it is the same adapter (`pandapeDomain`). It is a SEPARATE tenant namespace — `netcall` 404s on the infojobs domain and `braspress` 404s here — which is why the jobgether harvest's 18 hosts all probed dead under `pandape`, and why it is its own provider: a board label alone does not say which domain it lives on, and the vacancy ids are a separate sequence (13.9M here, 3.8M in Brazil).
- **The edge filters on the User-Agent**, unlike the Brazilian one: measured 2026-10-24 from fsn, `freehire/0.1 (+https://freehire.me)` and curl's default get 403 on every path, while `Mozilla/5.0 (compatible; freehire/0.1; +https://freehire.me)` is served — so the provider rides `compatibleUASource` and its harvest prober the same client (`probeClienter`). robots.txt disallows only `/Apply/`. The first harvest went out on the default client and read every tenant as a probe failure.
- **The JobPosting is a Brazilian template** (`templatedJobPosting`): every one sampled says `addressCountry: BR`, `currency: BRL` and `employmentType: partial-time` — a Lima call centre's full-time job paid in soles included — and drops the last letter of a locality with no region (`Ayacuch`, `Santiago Centr`). Some vacancy pages carry no JobPosting at all; there a page with a body is still a vacancy (`PostedAt` stays empty). Only `datePosted` and the salary bounds are read from it.
- **The country is the page's analytics props**, `window.dataLayerProps.country = 'Chile'` (an English name, through `location.NormalizeCountry`) — the tenant's country, on both the career site and every vacancy page. The place is the card's location; the work mode its picklist (`Presencial` / `Presencial y remoto` = hybrid / `Remoto`).
- **The currency comes from the country**, and only when the card's salary (`icon-wallet`) is written in that country's own symbol (`S/ 1,230` for Peru, `1.000.000 $` for Chile/Colombia/Argentina/Mexico/Uruguay); a card in `US$` keeps no salary. A country outside `pandapeLatamCurrencies` keeps no salary either — add it there when one appears.
- **Employment type is the card's schedule** (`icon-clock`: `Tiempo completo` / `Jornada completa` / `Jornada de N horas semanales` with N ≥ 40 → full time; `Tiempo parcial` / `Jornada part time` / `Por horas` → part time). The many contract wordings (indefinido, a plazo fijo, de obra o labor, …) are employment and say nothing about the schedule; only `Contrato civil por prestación de servicios` is a contract.
- **The pager serves 500 pages (10,000 vacancies) and no more**, on both domains: page 501 answers `{"isLast": true}` with no cards and no subtotal. A tenant stating more (activoschile 55,483, adecco-colombia 10,733) can never be listed whole, so its crawl fails on the first page and `harvest-boards` does not propose it.
- **Discovery is Common Crawl** (`*.pandape.computrabajo.com`): the domain has no sitemap of its own (its robots.txt names the Brazilian one). `www` redirects to the marketing site and `empleos` is the confidential bucket (root 404), so `atsboard` declines both. The harvest probes `/Vacancies`, not `/`: a tenant on the richer home template renders no counter on its home page.

**onlyfy traps** (`onlyfy`, onlyfy by XING — formerly prescreen — at `<board>.onlyfy.jobs`; live-verified 2026-10-25 across the 143 hosts Common Crawl names on `onlyfy.jobs` and `jobbase.io` — 74 distinct live sites, 1,788 postings, mostly Germany and Austria):

- **The listing is an HTML fragment**, `<site>/candidate/job/ajax_list?display_length=<n>&page=<p>`, the one the career site's "load more" button reads. Its pager script states the board's posting count (`$('#pageInfoTotalCount').text(185)`), so the walk must reach it or the board fails (`fullBoardListing`); a fragment without that script is not a listing. A page past the end, and an empty board, answer a "There are currently no vacant positions." row under HTTP 200; an unknown site answers 404 (`ErrBoardGone`).
- **`<board>.jobbase.io` is the old domain** and redirects to `<board>.onlyfy.jobs`; a renamed site redirects to its new name (`lloyd-fonds-ag` → `laiqon-ag`). The harvest prober skips a candidate whose listing redirects to another site name, so one site cannot become two boards; the destination is a candidate of its own.
- **A posting is read from its schema.org JobPosting** (`<site>/job/<id>`, redirected to a language-prefixed path) when the page renders one — about half the sites. A site with its own job template renders none (de-cix), and the first paced crawl left 1,422 of 3,004 postings unreadable on that alone; there the body is the platform's own job-ad view, `<site>/job/show/<id>/full`, whose `#jobAdContainer` every template shares, and the title, place and date are the listing row's. The listing names no employer, so the harvest prober reads the first posting's `hiringOrganization`.
- **Every site shares one platform edge that answers 429 to the run's aggregate rate.** The first unpaced crawl (125 boards, boards and posting pages fanned out) had 37 boards fail their listing on 429 and 1,544 posting pages come back unreadable; the adapter is now paced to ~3 req/s (`onlyfyRequestInterval`).
- **`jobLocationType: TELECOMMUTE` is not a remote signal.** It sits on a petrol-station clerk and a construction engineer alike: of 26 sampled postings carrying it, 16 mention no remote work anywhere in the body. The adapter leaves the work mode to the description parser.

**Polymer traps** (`polymer`, a startup ATS at `jobs.polymer.co/<board>`; live-verified 2026-10-24 across the 91 boards remoterocketship apply links and eight Common Crawl snapshots name — 61 live, 216 open postings, 101 of them `Remote`):

- **The board page is behind a Cloudflare challenge; its API is not.** `jobs.polymer.co` answers a plain client 403 "Just a moment...", while the page's own data source, `api.polymer.co/v1/hire/organizations/<board>/jobs` (listing) and `.../jobs/<id>` (one posting with its HTML `description` and a `kind` enum), is unauthenticated JSON. The global `/v1/hire/jobs` wants an API key, so there is no boardless crawl.
- **"Not found" is HTTP 422**, for a board (`no careers_page found`) and a posting (`no job found`) alike — `detailUnreadable` would read it as a failed request, so `polymerNotFound` spells it out: a 422 board is `ErrBoardGone`, a 422 posting is dropped.
- **The pager's `is_last` is only true on a real page.** `meta.count` is the board's posting count and `meta.total` its page count; a page past the end answers an empty list with `is_last: false` and a `next_page`. The walk ends on `is_last` or `page >= total` and fails unless the distinct postings reach `count` (`fullBoardListing`). The largest board measured holds 11 postings on one page.
- **`remoteness_pretty` is a four-value enum**: `Remote`, `Hybrid`, `No remote`, and `Remote friendly`, which says remote work is possible without naming the arrangement and stays `""` (jobspage's enum, word for word). A `Remote` posting with `remote_restriction_country_residency_is_required` names its countries in `remote_restriction_country_list`; one without names none, and its office country is deliberately not used.

**Flatchr traps** (`flatchr`, a French ATS at `careers.flatchr.io/<lang>/company/<board>`; live-verified 2026-10-24 across 306 board slugs from remoterocketship and Common Crawl — 220 live, 5,558 published vacancies, 5,290 of them in France and ~1% technical):

- **The board is one unpaginated JSON document**, `careers.flatchr.io/company/<board>.json`, which the client-rendered careers site loads itself: every published vacancy with its three body sections (`description`, `mission`, `profile`) and its structured fields, so there is no detail request. It is read through `GetJSON` and never `GetText`: `albconciergerie` (2,264 vacancies) is 22 MB, past the text cap.
- **A switched-off careers site answers HTTP 200 with an HTML fragment** (`<section class="notFound">`, "Votre recherche est tombée à l'eau") at the `.json` address; an unknown slug answers a JSON 404. The adapter re-reads the address as text only when the JSON fails to decode, and only the fragment is `ErrBoardGone` — anything else that fails to decode stays an ordinary failure.
- **A board can be a holding's.** `additi`'s site lists its subsidiaries' vacancies (`medialex`, `regieradioregions`) beside its own, and the same vacancy id then appears on both boards, so the employer is each vacancy's `company.name`. The harvest prober takes the employer name only from a vacancy filed under the board's own company, so a subsidiary's name never trips the name gate.
- **`remote` is the telework enum**: `fulltime` → remote ("Télétravail total"), `parttime` → hybrid, `notime` → onsite. Measured: 22 of 37 `fulltime` bodies say "100% télétravail", 1,742 of 2,957 `parttime` bodies name a number of telework days.
- **Salary is not read.** `salary`/`salary_max` sit beside a period letter (`mensuality` y/m/h/d) that contradicts the amounts on sampled rows (`800-1200` filed yearly), so a structured salary would publish wrong figures; enrichment's own reading decides instead.

**d.vinci traps** (`dvinci`, a German ATS; live-verified 2026-10-25 across the 102 tenant hosts Common Crawl names — 93 live, 5,336 publications, ~7% with a technical-looking title, ~90% in Germany):

- **The board is the careers HOST, and the whole board is one keyless JSON document**, `https://<host>/jobPublication/list.json`, with every live publication's body sections, places (with ISO country), working times, salary and employer — no detail request. `<tenant>.dvinci-hr.com` (the full product), `<tenant>.dvinci-easy.com` (the small-business one) and `<tenant>.dvinci.de` serve the same document, and so does an employer's own domain on the platform (`dertouristik.dvinci-hr.com` 301s to `career.dertour-jobs.com`) — which is why the board is the host and not a slug. A host-keyed board cannot be folded by URL, so the same tenant under a vendor host and its own domain would be two catalogue rows; the harvest seed should name the host the vendor host redirects TO.
- **One opening, several publications.** An opening (`jobOpening.id`) is published once per language (weig: `Ausbildung zum Papiertechnologen` beside `Training as a paper technologist`) AND sometimes several times in one language under different titles (cadenas: `(Senior) IT Administrator` and `DevOps Engineer` on one opening). Only the first kind is one job: the adapter keeps the lowest publication id of an opening whose publications are all in different languages, and keeps every publication of an opening that repeats a language. Measured: 19 translation groups, 44 same-language ones.
- **The body is `renderedContent` when it has text**, in page order (`introduction`, `tasks`, `profile`, `weOffer`, `additionalInformation`, with their headlines); `position` is the page header and `contact` names a recruiter with a phone number, so neither is body. A tenant on the plain template has no `renderedContent` and only the raw `introduction`/`tasks`/`profile`/`weOffer`/`closingText` fields. ~2% of publications have no body at all.
- **There is no work-mode field.** "Mobiles Arbeiten"/"Homeoffice" appear mostly in `weOffer` as a perk (364 of 4,535 bodies mention one), so nothing is set structurally and the description parser decides. `workingTimes` is a tenant-extensible picklist (`TEILZEIT_5075`, `VOLL_ODER_TEILZEIT_NACH_VEREINBARUNG`); only a single `FULL_TIME`/`PART_TIME` (or `VOLLZEIT`/`TEILZEIT`) is mapped. `contractPeriod` is free-form too and is not read.
- **Posted date is the publication's `startDate`** (`2018-09-10T22:00Z`, no seconds) and falls back to the opening's `createdDate`.
- **A tenant with the document switched off answers HTTP 422** `{"errorMsg":"An error has occurred"}` (doppelmayr, landgard, soprema). It stays an ordinary failure, not `ErrBoardGone` — the same body could be a transient server error.
- **The document can be large** (toom-baumarkt: 910 publications, 22 MB), so it is read through `GetJSON`, never `GetText`.
**coveto traps** (`coveto`, a German ATS at `k<customer number>.coveto.de`; live-verified 2026-10-25 — a DNS sweep of `k1`…`k99999` found 1,191 hosts, of which 972 answered, 850 had open postings, 66,443 in all, ~1.5% technical by title):

- **The tenant label is the customer number, and coveto's DNS has no wildcard**, so resolving `k<n>.coveto.de` across the number range IS the tenant list — six Common Crawl snapshots named only 100 of them. That sweep is the seed `harvest-boards coveto <file>` was first run from; the prober's own `discover` reads Common Crawl and finds the long-indexed few.
- **The whole board is one RSS document**, `/public/rss/`, unpaginated: every open posting with its title, link, `pubDate` and a `job-id-<n>` guid. Measured on a 71-posting board, its ids are exactly the ones the paged `/public/jobs/` listing walks, and the feed is not capped on the largest tenants either: k61491 lists 4,375 items against 175 listing pages of 25, k43552 6,123 against 245 (`fullBoardListing`). The body is on the posting page as a schema.org JobPosting, so the adapter hydrates; the employer is the posting's `hiringOrganization`, because a tenant posts for subsidiaries (k60360 is Stadtmission Nürnberg; its postings name PROSUM GmbH and DNE Catering).
- **An open application ("Initiativbewerbung") is a feed item whose page carries no JobPosting** — 40 of 40 sampled, across the 158 tenants that list one; coveto withholds the block because it is not a posting. Only an item whose TITLE names an open application is dropped for that; any other page missing its JobPosting is still an `unreadableDetail` marker. Marking open applications would cost every such board its unseen sweep for good: a dropped item is never stored, so never `seen`, so it is re-read and re-marked every run, and a small board's marker share stays above `maxUnreadablePercent`.
- **A white-labelled tenant links its postings on its own domain** (k43552 → `jobs.rocket-match.de`): 131 of the 850, mostly recruitment agencies, holding ~21k of the postings. The link is fetched as given.
- **Two HTML notices stand where the feed was, both under HTTP 200.** "Diese Seite ist nicht mehr aktiv." is a switched-off tenant (`ErrBoardGone`, 7 seen); "Diese Funktion wurde nicht aktiviert." is a tenant that never enabled the feed (1 seen) — its postings are live and only this path is closed, so it stays an ordinary failure.
- **`jobLocationType: TELECOMMUTE` is the only work-arrangement statement, and it is a remote one**: all 17 sampled "(Remote) …" titles and the "100% Home Office" ones carry it, "Homeoffice möglich" postings and a random sample of 85 do not. Its absence never becomes `onsite`. `employmentType` is a comma-joined list when a posting offers several (`FULL_TIME,PART_TIME`); only a single value is mapped.
- **One origin serves every tenant**, so the posting pages share one in-flight cap (`covetoMaxInFlight`, the measured 8) across the whole provider — a first crawl is ~66k page requests.

**concludis traps** (`concludis`, a German ATS at `<tenant>.concludis.de`; live-verified 2026-10-25 across 47 tenant hosts in Common Crawl — 28 serve the list, 23 have openings, 2,778 positions):

- **Every tenant serves its complete list at one fixed address**, `/prj/lst/a181a603769c1f98ad927e7367c7aa51/GesamtlisteOffenePositionen.htm` — the same hash on every tenant — paged under `?page=N` at a size the tenant picks (15 or 25), stating its extent in `.stellensum` ("46 Stellen gefunden", "1 Stelle gefunden", "Derzeit haben wir keine offenen Stellen."). A page past the end repeats the last one. The walk must collect exactly the stated number (it did on all 23) or the crawl fails (`fullBoardListing`).
- **A tenant that publishes on its own site redirects the list there** (`edeka` → `verbund.edeka`); leaving the tenant's host is `ErrBoardGone` with the destination in the message. An unknown label also resolves (wildcard DNS) and redirects onto the vendor's marketing site, which answers 404.
- **A tenant can redirect single POSTINGS to its own site while the list stays on concludis.** The first production crawl (2026-10-25) read 1,772 positions and marked 988 unreadable: `edeka-rr` 668/668, `smurfitkappa` 239/251 and `bofrost-at` 28/28 redirect every posting (`www.smurfitkappa.com/de/people/jobad#job=/prj/shw/…`), `transdev` routes some to its subsidiaries' sites. No request variant (`ie=1`, `iframe=1`, a Referer) stops the redirect. Where the landing page states a JobPosting it is read; where it does not, the posting is dropped, not marked — a marker would recur every run and hold the board's unseen sweep shut, and a dropped posting was never stored. A page on the tenant's own host missing its JobPosting is still a marker.
- **The body and the employer are on the posting page's JobPosting**; a group tenant posts for subsidiaries (`hhla` → METRANS Rail). `employmentType` is a string or a list; only a single value is mapped, `OTHER` never. No sampled posting (90) states `jobLocationType`.
- **The list page's keywords meta names the employer** as its first entry on 28 of 28 tenants (or the bare label, which names nobody) — that is the prober's company name; one tenant pads it with byte-order marks.

**Kenjo traps** (`kenjo`, the recruiting module of a German HR suite at `<site>.kenjo.io`; live-verified 2026-10-25 across 74 site labels in Common Crawl — 55 with openings, 505 positions, small and mid-sized employers):

- **The career site reads one JSON API on its own host**: `/api/controller/career-site/public/<site>/positions` (the site and its open positions, unpaginated, no body) and `…/positions/<customUrl>` (one position with `jobDescription.html`, `officeAddress`, `datePosted`). The same paths on `api.kenjo.io` answer 400 "Not allowed by CORS"; the tenant host answers a plain request. A position's page is `https://<site>.kenjo.io/<customUrl>`; its id is `_id`, not the `customUrl` slug, which carries edit suffixes (`…-archived-2409180733` on a live position).
- **Every label resolves**; an unknown one is a 404 "Company career site was not found." (`ErrBoardGone`).
- **A position can be published without a body**: its detail answers with no `jobDescription` key at all (five1's three). That is stored body-less, not marked — the answer is the same every run. Only a detail that is not the position (no matching `_id`) is a marker.
- **`positionType` is localised in the listing ("Vollzeit") and canonical in the detail ("Full-time")**; measured values: Full-time, Flexible, Freelance, Apprentice, Intern, Student, none. Kenjo records no work arrangement at all, so `WorkMode` is the parser's.
- **`officeName` is free text** (a city, "Deutschlandweit", or a company name); `officeAddress.city` is preferred as the location and the office name only fills in.

**Homerun traps** (`homerun`, a Dutch ATS for small and mid-sized employers at `<tenant>.homerun.co` or the employer's own domain; live-verified 2026-10-25 — 874 tenant labels in five Common Crawl snapshots, 623 serve a feed, 515 have openings, 2,499 vacancies, the largest `douglas` at 106; ~15% technical by title):

- **The listing is one open Atom feed per tenant, `feed.homerun.co/<tenant>`**, whatever domain the career site lives on: every open vacancy with its full body (`<description>`; `<content>` is only the teaser), `location>name`, `type>name`, `department>name` and `<updated>` (the publication time, `2006-01-02 15:04:05`). The career page itself renders its list in the browser, so it is no listing.
- **The feed's location is the employer's NAME for an office** ("Douglas Hoofdkantoor - Nijmegen", "Preston Palace", "Hybride", "Remote", "Other"). The vacancy page's JobPosting carries that office's postal address and ISO country on ~65% of pages; the rest carry none or an empty ld+json block (an office without an address). So a NEW vacancy costs one page request, and the page is only ENRICHMENT: a refused page stores the feed entry as it stands, never a marker. TELECOMMUTE (47 of 1,332 JobPostings) is read as remote; the office name stays in `Location` beside the page's town, so the parser still reads "Hybride".
- **The feed keeps vacancies whose page is gone**: 62 of 2,499 entries (2.5%) named a page that answers 404. A 404 on the page drops the vacancy; a stored vacancy is never re-fetched, so one that dies later stays open until the feed drops it.
- **A gone tenant's feed redirects to `404.homerun.co`, which answers 404** (`ErrBoardGone`). The tenant HOST is no signal: 115 tenants whose site redirects to `404.homerun.co/working_at/<tenant>` still serve live vacancies on their feed. DNS answers every label.
- **Every feed and vacancy page is served by one AWS origin, and it refuses by RATE**: an unpaced harvest of 893 feeds from fsn drew 429s within 8 seconds and then a 403 on every request from that address for over an hour. Every request the adapter makes draws on one provider-wide budget (`homerunRequestInterval`, 2/s), and `harvest-boards homerun` must run with `-pace 2` or slower.

**EasyCruit traps** (`easycruit`, Visma EasyCruit at `<site>.easycruit.com`; live-verified 2026-10-25 — 193 site labels in five Common Crawl snapshots, 158 with openings, 1,837 vacancies, NL 563 / NO 659 / SE 69 / DK 79 by country, ~5% technical by title, the largest `lentis` at 134):

- **Two open XML exports**: `/export/xml/vacancy/list.xml` (every open vacancy, no body) and `/export/xml/vacancy/<id>.xml` (one with `Description`, `CandidateProfile`, `JobOffer`, HTML entity-encoded). The career site's HTML is behind an AWS WAF browser challenge (HTTP 202 and a script); the exports are not.
- **An unknown label answers the list with a plain 404** (`ErrBoardGone`); a site with no openings answers an empty `<VacancyList/>`.
- **A vacancy published under several departments has one page URL per department, in no stable order** between two reads of the same list; the lowest department id's page is kept so the URL does not flip.
- **The country is named in the site's language** ("Nederland", "Norge", "Noreg", "Sverige") and goes through `location.NormalizeCountry`, so a name that dictionary lacks yields no country and the location parser decides; the numeric country id beside it is EasyCruit's own, not ISO.
- **`duration` and `extent` are picklists keyed in English** whatever the label language (`permanent`, `fixed-term-contract`, `temporary`, `extra-help`, `fulltime`, `parttime`, `internship`, …). A placement is an internship, a fixed or temporary engagement a contract, otherwise a single extent decides.
**HR Office traps** (`hroffice`, the recruiting module of a Dutch HR suite at `<site>.inhroffice.com`; live-verified 2026-10-25 — 46 site labels in five Common Crawl snapshots, 31 with openings, 560 vacancies, NL 533 / BE 21 / DE 6, the largest `cardia` at 168 and `sawiday` at 105):
- **One open JSON listing on the site's host, `/api/nl/vacancies`** (a bare array: `id`, `title`, `city`, `country` in Dutch, `publishDate` as `02-01-2006`), no body. The body is the server-rendered page `/nl/job/<id>`, in `.vacancy-main-container > section`; that section ends with the "Solliciteren" button block, which is cut.
- **An unknown or switched-off label answers HTTP 500** with a `MiniJobPortalNotFoundException` body (the home page too). The shared client cannot tell that from a real server error, so it stays an ordinary failure and the chronic-board close retires the board.
- **Many employers show the same vacancies on their own domain** (careers.fitforme.com, werkenbij.jaarbeurs.nl). Those sites pull from HR Office and do NOT serve the API, so the board is always the inhroffice.com label; the custom domain names no board. `link.inhroffice.com` is the vendor's shortener, not a tenant.
- **`city` is null on about half the vacancies.** The location is written as the page shows it, "City, Country"; the Dutch country name also goes through `location.NormalizeCountry`.

**Darwinbox traps** (`darwinbox`, the HRMS most large Indian employers run, at `<tenant>.darwinbox.in` or `<tenant>.darwinbox.com`; live-verified 2026-10-25 — 140 tenant hosts in Common Crawl, 79 with openings, 5,004 postings; India ~65%, then the Philippines, Malaysia, Singapore):

- **The board is the whole host**: the TLD is the tenant's data region, and one tenant label does not say which. The same host is also the tenant's employee HRMS login, so `atsboard` reads a Darwinbox URL as a board only when it carries a `careers` segment.
- **The listing is `POST /ms/candidateapi/job/alljobs` behind Cloudflare**, which 403s Go's default TLS+HTTP/2 fingerprint and curl's alike and serves the Chrome one from both the laptop and the crawl host — hence the shared `fingerprintHTTP`. It pages at **100 whatever limit is asked for**, states the board total as `job_counts`, and every row already carries the whole description (`jd`), so there is no detail request. A walk that does not add up to `job_counts` fails the crawl (`fullBoardListing`).
- **An unknown tenant is HTTP 500** `Internal Server Error - Invalid subdomain: <label>`; the fingerprint transport's POST keeps no body, so it reads as an ordinary failure, not `ErrBoardGone`.
- **`emp_type_name` is the tenant's own HR category** ("Permanent", "Probationary", "BRANCH RESOURCE", "P-P8-Probationer-HO Executive" — measured over 2,700 postings), so only a label that states the arrangement is mapped. `is_remote` is 0 by default on tenants that never set it, so only 1 is a statement. `country` is a structured country name.
- **The API names no employer**; the portal page served to a non-browser client is a shell whose `<title>` is the legal entity ("Ather Energy Limited"), which is what `cmd/harvest-boards` stores.

**greytHR Hire traps** (`greythr`, the recruiting module of the payroll/HRMS most Indian small and mid-sized employers run, at `<tenant>.greythr.com/hire/jobs/`; live-verified 2026-10-25 — 119 tenant hosts in Common Crawl, 57 with openings, 843 postings):

- **Two open JSON endpoints on the tenant host, no key, plain client**: `POST /hire/api/career/published_jobs/` (body `{}`; every published opening in one unpaginated `data` array, description included) and `GET /hire/api/greythr/emp-category/` (the tenant's category values, which is where an opening's `locations` ids get their names — `"519"` is `KARAIKUDI`). The employer's name is `GET /hire/api/career/get_company_details/` → `company_name`, which `cmd/harvest-boards` stores.
- **The board is the whole host**, and the same host is the tenant's payroll login (`/uas/portal/auth/login`), so `atsboard` reads it as a board only under `/hire/`. A label no tenant holds and a tenant without the Hire module both answer the listing with a 404 (`ErrBoardGone`).
- **`job_type` is a fixed picklist** (Full-time, Part-time, Internship, Contract, Direct Contract); `is_remote` is false by default, so only true is a statement.

**Keka note** (`keka`): Keka's DNS is a wildcard, and a label with no careers portal redirects to an HTML page under HTTP 200 — `/careers/Content/TenantNotFound.html` ("Invalid Tenant") for a label no organisation holds, `/careers/Content/403.html` ("Forbidden Access") for an organisation that runs Keka's HRMS without its hiring module (ebay, brisa). Both are "no board", not a refusal.

**onapply traps** (`onapply`, a German ATS at `<tenant>.onapply.de`; live-verified 2026-10-25 — 4,131 postings from 553 employers, DE 92% / CH 4% / AT 3%, ~5% technical by title):

- **It is boardless although the ATS has tenants**, because the vendor's own job board (`jobboard.onapply.de`) searches one keyless index over every tenant: `https://api-jobboard.onapply.de/jobboard/production/listing/search/<from>/<size>?jobtitle=&location=`. 1,130 of the 1,191 posting ids harvested from tenant links were in it (the rest had closed). Each hit carries the whole posting, so the crawl is a walk of the index and nothing needs a detail request.
- **The first path segment is an OFFSET, not a page number.** The board's own JS sends `<page>/5`, which reads as a page, but `1/10` starts at the second row. `size=500` answers in ~1 s; `size=5000` is a 502 from the gateway.
- **The search sends no sort, so one walk is not the index.** Elasticsearch orders tied scores per shard and a page boundary repeats one posting and skips another: one walk yielded 4,037 and another 4,082 distinct ids of the 4,131 stated, and their union was exactly 4,131. A run therefore walks again while it holds fewer distinct ids than `total`, at most four times.
- **The contact section is left out of the body**: it names the recruiter and their address. `employmentTypes` is a list; a posting whose types disagree ("Vollzeit" + "Teilzeit") states no employment type, and the training types (Ausbildung, Duales Studium, Werkstudententätigkeit) have no counterpart.

**Workwise traps** (`workwise`, www.workwise.io; live-verified 2026-10-25 — 4,015 postings, ~10–14% technical by title, DE only):

- **The listing is ONE sitemap, `enquiryFocusPagesSitemap.xml`**, reached through `/sitemap.xml`. It is complete: 25 of 25 job-board short links (`/s/<code>`, which 302 to `/bewerben?id=<id>`) sampled from the harvest named an id it lists. The search API (`api.workwise.io/v2/…`) answers 405 to a plain GET and is not needed.
- **A closed posting answers 200** with "Dieser Job ist nicht mehr aktiv" and no JobPosting; a posting whose page carries none is dropped. Ids outside the sitemap resolve the same way — the sitemap is the open set.
- **The work mode is read from the line the platform renders from its own form field** at the top of the description: "N - M Tage pro Woche Homeoffice" and "Remote möglich" are hybrid, `jobLocationType: TELECOMMUTE` with "Remote Job" is remote. "Kein Homeoffice" (70% of sampled postings) is NOT read as onsite — the form cannot tell a choice from an untouched field, the same reason XING's `NON_REMOTE` maps to nothing.
- `addressRegion` is a German state abbreviation ("NRW", "BY") and is not used; `baseSalary` states one `value` or a `minValue`/`maxValue` pair with `unitText` YEAR, MONTH or HOUR.
**Dutch public boards** (`werkenvoornederland`, `gemeentebanen`, `academictransfer`; live-verified 2026-10-25). Each is one posting sitemap naming the open set, and each new posting is hydrated from its page's JobPosting at one request a second (ten seconds on AcademicTransfer). A government or university appointment starts as a fixed-term contract, so `employmentType: TEMPORARY` is NOT contract work there — `schemaScheduleType` maps only an internship and a single full- or part-time value.

- **`werkenvoornederland`** (central government, 1,364 postings, ~40 a day; vakgebied ICT on 15 of 60 sampled; "thuiswerken"/"hybride" in the body of 0 of 60). The identity is the code that ends the path (`CJIB-2026-9570`, `DEF2644-2005-6416`), not the title slug. The JobPosting `description` is a one-line summary, so the body is the page's sections ("Dit ga je doen", "Dit vragen wij", ...) — every `<section>` holding an `.s-article-content`, minus `stel_gerust_je_vraag`, the recruiter's contact card with phone numbers. The field (vakgebied, possibly several, comma-joined) is read from the page's `dataLayer.push`; ICT or "Development en testing" sets `IsTechHint`. `addressLocality` may carry the office street in brackets. A closed posting is a 404.
- **werkenbijdeoverheid.nl is NOT a second source**: its vacancy and internship sitemaps (1,584) hold every werkenvoornederland posting (1,364 of 1,364) plus ~220 from provinces, municipalities and water boards — but its pages carry no JobPosting, and a municipal posting names only the department ("Control") as the employer. Municipalities come through `gemeentebanen` instead.
- **`gemeentebanen`** (municipalities, 603 postings, ~40 a day; ~10% technical by title). `/vacatures/<id>/<employer>/<title>`, id is the identity; the sitemap also names `/vacatures` itself. The JobPosting carries the whole body. `employmentType` is FULL_TIME on 40 of 40 sampled, 32-hour ones included, so it is not read. the Dutch word for hybrid ("hybride") appears in ~70% of bodies, as a benefit line, and no structured field states a work mode. A closed posting 301s to `/vacatures/`; the redirect is the verdict.
- **`academictransfer`** (universities, UMCs, institutes; 767 postings — 497 academic, 291 support; ~20% technical by title). robots.txt sets `Crawl-delay: 10`, honoured for every request, so one run hydrates at most 240 (40 minutes) and the first catalogue takes four runs. The JobPosting is the `mainEntity` of a WebPage block, which `jobPostingNodes` unwraps. The search API (`api.academictransfer.com/vacancies/`) answers 401 and its robots.txt disallows everything. A posting is listed once, under `/en/` or `/nl/`; a closed one is a 404.
- **Not built, with numbers.** `werk.nl` (UWV, 269,655 postings): of 197 sampled details none was a UWV-native posting — every one came from a partner feed (`source` DGR 69%, then JIT, IGB, JOO, RUS, KNO) with an external apply URL, and every description is cut at ~2,000 characters; ~2% technical, ~6% mention remote or hybrid. That is the opposite of `arbeitsagentur`'s rule (only first-party postings), and the listing also needs an Oracle Access Manager cookie dance plus an XSRF header, pages 20 at a time behind a 10,000-page cap, and `www.werk.nl` serves the wrong intermediate certificate (DigiCert Global G2 instead of the "G2 TLS EU RSA4096 SHA384 2022 CA1" its leaf names), which Go on the crawl host rejects. Nationale Vacaturebank (~90k): every page answers DPG Media's consent gate, and werk.nl itself lists it as an apply target, i.e. it is an aggregator.

**CHECK-IN traps** (`meincheckin`, mein-check-in.de; live-verified 2026-10-25 across the 160 tenants named by links in the German board harvest — 157 with openings, 2,338 menu entries of which ~7% are open applications, ~7% technical by title; savings banks, cooperative banks, insurers, councils):

- **Three branded hosts, and only two of them talk to each other.** www serves its own tenants (mostly councils) and redirects the sparkasse. ones to `sparkasse.mein-check-in.de/<tenant>`; the vr. tenants (cooperative banks, 25 of 40 keyed by their bank code, 85095004) are a 404 on www and on sparkasse. No slug was measured on two hosts, so the board is the bare slug and the start page is asked of www, then of vr.
- **Position ids are numbered per HOST and the ranges overlap** (sparkasse 120473 beside vr 120315), so the external id is `<tenant>/<id>`.
- **The posting is MICRODATA** (`itemtype="http://schema.org/JobPosting"`), not ld+json, and `name` occurs in the identifier, the organisation and the place — each property is read inside its own scope. The microdata `title` `<h2>` also holds the "Jetzt hier bewerben" button, so the title comes from `og:title` minus " | <tenant> Onlinebewerbung".
- **`employmentType` is the tenant's menu SECTION** ("stellenangebote", "ausbildung", "praktika"), not an employment type; only "praktika" maps (internship). `addressRegion` is a key ("baden_württemberg") and is not read; some tenants put soft hyphens in `addressLocality`.
- **The open application ("Initiativbewerbung") is listed among the positions** by most tenants and is skipped at the menu, costing no request. A closed position is a 404.
- `<name>-karriereportal.mein-check-in.de/detail/<uuid>` is a DIFFERENT CHECK-IN product and is not read.

**helixjobs traps** (`helixjobs`, the job portal Perbility runs for its concludis employers at `helixjobs.com`; live-verified 2026-10-25 by walking the whole portal list — 1,001 pages, 25,021 postings, 590 instance ids):
- **The portal is concludis under another name, so the board is the concludis INSTANCE ID (`iid`), not a tenant label.** A posting is `/_/jobad?prj=<iid>P<position id>` and the position id is the one the employer's `<tenant>.concludis.de` list shows (Vitanas: iid 147, concludis position 130755, portal posting `147P130755`; 230 of the tenant's 231 positions are on the portal). The portal names a tenant's concludis label on no page it serves, so it reaches the employers the `concludis` adapter cannot — 590 iids against 23 labels. The external id is the whole `prj` value with the separator folded to `P` (harvested links spell `100007p36`). **Onboarding must skip the iids whose employers are already `concludis` boards**: the same position under two providers is a cross-source duplicate; check a sampled position id against `jobs.external_id` for `source = 'concludis'`.
- **The list is `/_/joblist?iid=<board>&page=N`, 25 lines a page, stating `Suchergebnis: <strong>N</strong> Treffer`**; a page past the end carries no line. The walk must reach exactly that count (`fullBoardListing`). An iid with no posting and an iid that does not exist both answer "Keine Ergebnisse gefunden", so neither is `ErrBoardGone`. A vanity label (`apetito-ag.helixjobs.com`) serves the same pages and is not the board.
- **The body is the posting page's JobPosting, in exactly the concludis shape** (`concludisJobPosting`: employer, workplaces with ISO countries, `employmentType` as one enum or a list), so this is a HydratingSource. A missing posting is 404. No sampled posting (119) states `jobLocationType`. **A posting the portal relays to another platform redirects off `helixjobs.com`** (iid 500000's postings land on a P&I LOGA site) and is dropped, the concludis rule.
- **One origin serves every iid**, so the whole provider shares 4 requests in flight (`helixjobsMaxInFlight`), the measured width; a first crawl is ~25k posting pages and outlasts one run.

**talentstorm traps** (`talentstorm`, by ontavio, at `<site>.talentstorm.de`; live-verified 2026-10-25 across 127 labels from the German harvest and Common Crawl — 122 with offers, 1,355 offers, mostly industrial mid-sized employers, ~5% technical):
- **The career site reads `api.talentstorm.de/api/v1/joboffers/basic/<site>`, which answers only a request whose `Origin` is the site itself.** Without it, and for a label no tenant holds, it answers 401 "Authentication Required", so a 401 is a plain failure, never `ErrBoardGone`. Paged it serves 10 offers; `?pagination=false` returns the whole list in one answer, which is the listing (`fullBoardListing`). The `Accept: application/ld+json` variant that states `hydra:totalItems` is unreachable through the shared client, which always sends `application/json`.
- **An offer carries its assembled body (`description`), workplaces, employment and offer type**, so nothing is hydrated. Offer type 6 is an open application ("Initiativbewerbung", 75 of 1,355) and is dropped; `speculativeApplication` is NOT that signal — it is set on ordinary positions too. Type 4 (Praktikum) maps to `internship`, 7 (Freelancer) to `contract`, a position's `ft`/`pt` to full/part time; Minijob, Ferienjob, apprenticeship and study types stay `""`. Talentstorm records no work arrangement.
- **`transparencyOfPay` states a wage range with no currency**; it is kept as EUR only where every workplace is in Germany or Austria, and dropped elsewhere. The API names no company; the prober takes the headquarters workplace's label.

**rexx traps** (`rexx`, rexx systems' recruiting portals; live-verified 2026-10-25 across the 152 portal hosts Common Crawl names — 119 answer a listing, 101 with postings, 3,126 postings, largely healthcare, retail and public sector):

- **The board is the portal BASE: a host, optionally with a path** (`zieglersche-portal.rexx-recruitment.com`, or `jobs.newyorker.de/karriere` for a portal on the employer's own domain). The adapter lists `<base>/stellenangebote.html?start=<offset>`, 100 postings a page; the walk ends on a page with no new posting and fails the board if any page fails or the 100-page bound is reached (`crawlAllPagedLinks`). The largest portal measured (newyorker) has 918 postings.
- **The listing template is per tenant** (a table on one, cards on another), so postings are read from their permalinks, `/<slug>-<lang>-j<id>.html`, which every template shares. The slug and language are decoration: the portal serves an id under any slug, so the id is the identity. **The permalink host is NOT checked**: a portal on the vendor host routinely links its postings on the employer's own domain (`jobs.zieglersche.de`). Every link carries a per-request `?sid=`, which is dropped — kept, each page would look entirely new and the walk would run to its bound.
- **A posting is read from its schema.org JobPosting**, whose `description` already holds the task/profile/benefit sections (`responsibilities`/`qualifications`/`jobBenefits` repeat them). 176 of 178 sampled posting pages carry one; `voss` renders none, so its postings come back as unreadable markers — the harvest prober reads one posting page and skips such a portal. `hiringOrganization.name` is the PORTAL's name ("… GmbH Jobportal" on a third of portals), so the catalogue's employer is used. No portal sampled sets `jobLocationType`; `TELECOMMUTE` would map to remote, nothing else is set structurally.
- **The Atom feed is not the listing.** `rss_generator-rss0.php?unit=<unit>&lang=de` lists the same postings, but `unit` is a per-portal name the root page does not always link, and the feed carries no body.

**Jobboardly traps** (`jobboardly`; live-verified 2026-10-17, on hire-mamas, kubernetes-jobs, qajobsboard, pcdn-global, drone-vet-1, remotejobs):

- **A board is a NICHE JOB BOARD, not an employer, and its key is the tenant subdomain** of `<board>.jobboardly.com`. Many boards sit behind the owner's own domain (kubernetes-jobs serves `kubernetesjobs.com`, remotejobs redirects to `www.remotejobs.com`); the subdomain still answers and the client follows the redirect. Every posting names its own employer, and much of a board is backfilled from first-party ATS postings (Workday, JazzHR, Microsoft's own site), so the provider is an `aggregator` but not `boardless` — the getro shape. A board that is not one DNS label fails the crawl before any request; an unknown subdomain answers 404. Aggregators are absent from `atsboard`, so a pasted link is not recognised there.
- **`/jobs.json` is the whole catalogue in one unpaginated array, bodies inline.** `?page=2` is ignored and returns the identical array; on hire-mamas (141), kubernetes-jobs (173) and pcdn-global (1,332) the array held exactly as many postings as `sitemap.xml` lists. That is what earns `fullBoardListing`. The document is rendered on request and is large — remotejobs is 1,723 postings, 31 MB, ~28s to first byte — so the provider rides `NewLongTimeoutClient`, the HumanBit case.
- **There is no posting id field; the identity is `links.self`'s last segment**, a title slug plus an 8-hex suffix. Neither half resolves alone (`/jobs/<hex>` and `/jobs/x-<hex>` both 404), so the whole segment is the external id. `links.self` is also Job.URL, on whichever host the board publishes under.
- **`application_link` is never the URL.** It is the employer's apply target as typed: an email address on some postings, a Workday login redirect on others, and on hire-mamas' elmotalent imports two hosts glued together (`https://cea.elmotalent.com.auhttps://cea.elmotalent.com.au/...`). `application.url`/`application.email` were null on every posting measured.
- **`location` is the ARRANGEMENT** (`onsite`/`hybrid`/`remote`, a closed picklist) and sets `WorkMode`. The place is `company.location`, which the posting page renders as the job's location ("Fort Bragg, North Carolina, United States" for a Booz Allen posting), and is empty or null on a remote one. `location_limits` are the countries a remote posting hires from — alpha-2 codes (`US`, `DE`), the non-ISO `UK`, a name (`United States`) or `Worldwide` — read through `countriesFromCodes`, and stand in as the location text when there is no place. The page's ld+json expands `Worldwide` into every country in the world, so it is not read.
- **`arrangement` is a picklist** (`fulltime`, `parttime`, `contract`, `internship`, `temporary`, `volunteer`, or null); the first four map, the rest are left to the description. `salary` is `{schedule, minimum, maximum}` with bounds in CENTS and an ISO currency (USD, GBP, EUR, COP, ZAR, CAD seen); `schedule` is `yearly`/`monthly`/`daily`/`hourly`, often with both bounds null. Employer-typed values are published as stated — one qajobsboard posting says `yearly` 22-25 USD.
- **Not an IT platform.** hire-mamas is a job board for mothers (lifeguards, HR partners), pcdn-global social-impact work; kubernetes-jobs, qajobsboard and drone-vet-1 are technical niches. Nothing sets `IsTechHint`; the catalogue filter judges each title. No metering seen over ~20 requests.
**HerKey traps** (`herkey`; live-verified 2026-10-18):

- **The page is a client-rendered shell; the catalogue is the site's own backend.** `www.herkey.com/jobs` carries an empty `pageProps`, and `sitemaps/jobs-1.xml` (8,371 URLs, last written 2026-03) is stale. The frontend lists through `GET api-prod.herkey.com/api/v1/herkey/jobs/es_candidate_jobs?page_no=&page_size=`, which answers 401 without `Authorization: Token <session>`. The session is the frontend's own anonymous one, `GET /api/v1/herkey/generate-session` (`body.session_id`, a JWT good for 30 days), minted once per run. `robots.txt` disallows nothing under `/jobs`.
- **The index is the whole catalogue in four requests.** `page_size=500` is served; `pagination.total_items` was 1,560 and the walk read 1,560 distinct ids. Any page failing, a total that moves mid-walk, or a distinct count off the total fails the crawl, which is what earns `fullCatalog`. Rows are ordered by `modified_on`, newest first.
- **The index keeps expired postings.** 332 rows carry a `modified_on` from 2025-11 or earlier; their posting resource (`GET /api/v1/herkey/jobs/jobs/<id>`, keyless) says `status: "inactive"` with an `expires_on` months past. They are JobsPikr-scraped copies (`ats_type: jobspikr_external_jobs`) and Talent.com links. The posting resource decides: anything not `active` is dropped. A dropped posting is never stored, so each run re-asks for these; the budget below bounds that. A stored posting that later expires stays open while the index lists it, since a seen posting costs no detail request.
- **Most bodies are only on the posting resource.** 1,495 of 1,560 listing rows carry no `requirements`/`responsibilities`; the body is the resource's `description` (+ those two). Imported pharma bodies are newline plain text and are rebuilt into paragraphs. 10 of 65 sampled pharma postings read the literal `"TBD"`; such a posting is dropped as having no description. An unknown id answers HTTP 400.
- **The posting resource is slow: 4-15 s a request**, measured sequentially. The provider rides the 45 s long-timeout client, four detail workers and a 1 req/s limiter, and hydrates at most 600 postings a run, so a cold start (~1.5k) accretes over three runs instead of outliving its unit.
- **Not an IT board, and a third of the rest is ATS copies.** 1,021 of 1,560 are one pharma employer (Cadila Pharma, field sales and plant roles, from its own careers site). `classify.IsTech` accepts 99 titles (Aristocrat, Ericsson, Credit Karma, GoDaddy, UnitedHealth...). 186 of the 539 others link to greenhouse, smartrecruiters, workday, taleo, lever, workable or zoho, so the adapter is `CoverageGated`. `redirect_url` is Job.URL when it is the posting's own: recruitics' `rx_url` redirect is unwrapped to Aristocrat's Workday posting, and a link two postings share keeps the HerKey page. 151 rows are HerKey-native and have no link.
- **Structured fields are form picklists with defaults.** `work_mode` (`work_from_home`/`work_from_office`/`hybrid`) is set on ~130 rows; `job_types` spells `full time` and `full_time`, and `returnee_program` is a programme flag, not a schedule. `min_year`/`max_year` default to 0-2 (1,092 rows), so only a minimum above zero is mapped. `location_name` is one city or `Any` (no place). Timestamps are zone-less India time. `skills` is free text and is not mapped.
**WORK180 traps** (`work180`; live-verified 2026-10-18 from the crawl host):

- **Reachable only from our hosts.** From a residential IP every path on `work180.com` — `robots.txt` and static assets included — answers 403 `cf-mitigated: challenge`, and neither `fingerprintHTTP` (every tls-client profile) nor a headless Chrome clears it; archive.org has been refused (429) since 2026-09-15. From the crawl host and from fsn, plain HTTP with our own User-Agent answers 200. Validate from the crawl host. `api.work180.com/graphql` is not behind Cloudflare but answers `401 You are not authorized` to every operation, `{ __typename }` included; the site calls it server-side.
- **A board is a regional site** — `au` (`en-au`, which also lists New Zealand), `uk` (`en-gb`), `us` (`en-us`), the API's own `Region` enum. Three distinct board names with an empty region, so the board-scoped close's region-ambiguity refusal never applies. A board's postings are not confined to its country (the au site lists Hatch roles in Montréal), so geography comes from each posting, never the board.
- **The listing is `/<locale>/jobs-sitemap.xml`, not the search.** The search page answers `jobCount: 100, totalPages: 10` whatever the catalogue holds, and a page past 10 repeats page 10. The sitemaps named 5,168 (au), 1,729 (uk) and 1,699 (us) posting URLs; the remaining entries are search category pages. Posting ids are unique across the three, and the URL carries the employer's WORK180 slug but not its name.
- **The posting page has two structured sources.** The schema.org JobPosting carries title, body, `datePosted`, employer, places (`address.name` is the display string; no `addressCountry`), `jobLocationType: TELECOMMUTE` and `employmentType`. The React Server Components payload (`self.__next_f.push` chunks) carries the posting's own record — `expired`, `applicationType`, and `applicationLink`, the employer's own ATS posting (Workday, SuccessFactors, PageUp, Ashby, contactrh, …). An `EMAIL` application's link is a mail relay, so the WORK180 page stays the URL.
- **It is an aggregator of first-party ATS postings, hence `CoverageGated`.** The probe is asked about the employer's WORK180 slug — the only employer identity the sitemap states — and a covered posting is yielded body-less under that same slug. A slug that spells the employer differently from freehire's company key only reads as uncovered (a page bought and then discarded by the pipeline's gate); an alias in `company_slug_aliases` fixes such a pair.
- **`robots.txt` declares `Crawl-delay: 5`, and the adapter honours it**: one posting page every 5 s across all three boards, at most 120 new pages per board per run (30 minutes), newest first. The ~8.6k first backfill therefore accretes over a couple of dozen runs; a deferred posting is never stored body-less. robots.txt disallows nothing the adapter reads.
**Naukri traps** (`naukri`; live-verified 2026-10-18 from a residential IP, prod and the crawl host):

- **Reachable only through `SOURCES_PROXY_URL`.** From prod and the crawl host every path — `robots.txt` included — is tarpitted by Akamai: HTTP/1.1 hangs to the timeout, HTTP/2 is reset with `INTERNAL_ERROR`, and a headless Chrome gets `ERR_HTTP2_PROTOCOL_ERROR`, so no transport on those IPs helps. Through the proxy plain HTTP answers in ~1-2 s, so no browser is needed either. Hence `proxiedProviders`; without the proxy the provider fails every run, which is the honest outcome. `robots.txt` disallows nothing under `/jobapi/` for `User-agent: *`.
- **The listing is the ANDROID APP's search, not the website's.** The site calls the same `GET /jobapi/v3/search` and `GET /jobapi/v4/job/<id>`, but a browser User-Agent gets `406 {"message":"recaptcha required"}` on both, our own User-Agent gets 403 Access Denied, and only the app's HTTP stack (`okhttp/4.9.0`) plus `appid: 105` / `systemid: 105` is served; without those headers the API answers 400 "Please provide the valid App Id and System Id". The client's User-Agent always wins over per-request headers, so the string is set on a copy of the transport (`withUserAgent`) — applied again over the proxied client in `proxiedProviders`, or the rewire would silently restore ours. The listing and every page the SPA renders are client-side shells, and the job sitemaps carry URLs only.
- **A board is a FUNCTIONAL AREA id** (`functionAreaIdGid`): `5` Engineering - Software & QA, `8` IT & Information Security, `3` Data Science & Analytics, `4` Engineering - Hardware & Networks — the ids the search's own `functionalAreaGid` facet reports. An unknown id answers 0 rows (not the unfiltered catalogue), a non-numeric one an Elasticsearch 400; the adapter refuses anything but digits. The filter was checked against the posting API's own `functionalArea` on a sample. `3`/`5`/`8` set `IsTechHint`: `classify.IsTech` accepted 80 of 300 of their titles and `ConfirmedNonTech` none. `4` mixes in hardware roles and gets no hint.
- **The search window is 15,000 rows.** `noOfResults` serves up to 100 and page 150 is the deepest: page 151 answers 400 "Invalid value" at any page size. The walk reads `jobAge=7` sorted by freshness (`sort=f`, newest first). On 2026-10-18 the 7-day window held 18,920 postings in area 5 (truncated to its newest 15,000, logged), 5,177 in 8, 2,724 in 3 and 1,815 in 4; the whole catalogue held 102,872 in 7 days, so the technical areas are about a quarter of it. Area 5 held 56,950 over 30 days — no single query can read a month.
- **Not `fullBoardListing`, and a 14-day sweep grace.** A posting the employer does not refresh leaves the 7-day window while still live, and area 5 is truncated anyway, so "not seen this run" is not evidence. A later search page failing ends the walk with what was read. The posting API answers an expired or unknown id with `303 {"metaSearch":{"isExpiredJob":"1"}}` and no Location (read as gone), but nothing asks it about a stored posting, so the grace is what closes one.
- **A search row is a snippet; the posting API is the body.** `jobDescription` in the search is ~300 characters; `/jobapi/v4/job/<id>?microsite=y` carries the full HTML `description`, the places (`locations[].label` — the search's location placeholder can read `Hybrid - Bengaluru`), `wfhType` (`0` office, `2` remote, `3` hybrid, `1` the covid-era temporary WFH and left unmapped) and `employmentType` (`Full Time, Permanent`; a `Temporary/Contractual` or `Freelance/Homebased` nature is a contract). New postings only (`HydratingSource`), newest first, at most 1,500 a RUN — one counter on the adapter, which cmd/ingest builds once per process and crawls every board through — at 2 req/s over four workers: 150 details at ~4 req/s through the proxy were all served. One board may spend at most a third of it, so area 5's 15k cold backlog cannot take the whole run from `8` and `3`; a fourth board gets only the leftover until the saturated backlogs drain (area 5's in ~30 runs), after which nothing is starved. A cold start is ~23k details across `3`/`5`/`8`; steady state is a few hundred a run.
- **Salary is per annum in rupees when shown.** 75 of 300 rows publish `salaryDetail` (`hideSalary: false`) with the label `95 Lacs-2.5 Cr PA`; only a label ending in ` PA` is published, as `year`. `createdDate` is epoch milliseconds and moves when the employer refreshes the posting — the id's leading `DDMMYY` is the original date. `minimumExperience` is mapped when above 0. `tagsAndSkills`/`keySkills` are free text and are not mapped. Consultancies post under their own name (`consultant: true`, about one row in six); the client is usually unnamed.
- **The proxy's exit IP lost the posting API on 2026-10-19.** Every prod run failed: the search answered through `SOURCES_PROXY_URL` (until page ~143 of area 5, which also turned 406), while EVERY posting request answered `406 {"message":"recaptcha required"}` from the first one of the first run. Reproduced by hand through the same proxy (exit `45.61.127.48`, AS62874 Web2Objects — a hosting network, not residential) with the exact app User-Agent and headers: search 200, posting 406 on `v4`, `v3` and every appid; the same requests from a residential IP served 400 of 400 postings at ~1.5 req/s. So it is the IP's reputation with Akamai, not the adapter's headers or wiring, and no code reaches past it — the hosted tier is out too (the Firecrawl account has no credits). The adapter now treats a 406/403/429 from the posting API as a refusal of the IP: the first one ends hydration for the whole run (one log line), the board fails naming the refusal unless it still refreshed stored postings, and `board_health` cools it. Before that each run sent 1,500 refused requests through the shared proxy. **Crawling naukri needs a different egress** (a residential exit); until there is one, disable its schedule rather than let it fail every two hours.
- **The rotating gateway is that egress (2026-10-21).** The same Webshare account's rotating endpoint (`<user>-rotate@p.webshare.io:80`) hands out a fresh exit per request, and through it 15 of 20 posting requests answered 200 and five timed out, none refused. naukri therefore egresses through `SOURCES_ROTATING_PROXY_URL` when it is set (`rotatingProxyProviders` in proxy.go) and through the fixed exit otherwise. A refusal used to end the run's hydration: with a fresh IP per request it is one bad exit rather than the IP's verdict, so if refusals show up in the run logs that rule is the next thing to loosen. They did: on 2026-10-25 two of four runs met one 406 each and stopped hydrating, one of them 30 seconds in. It now takes `naukriRefusalLimit` (10) refusals in a run; a refused posting is deferred either way.
- **The coverage gate ate the whole budget (audit 2026-10-25).** The adapter hydrated newest first and the pipeline then discarded every posting whose employer a non-aggregator source already covers — unstored, so never `seen`, so bought again on the next run. Once the uncovered postings were stored, the newest unseen ones were exactly the covered ones: every board logged `skipped 500/… postings — company already covered`, i.e. its whole 500-detail share, every run, and the catalogue sat at 3,993 open postings for four days while 9,166 area-5 postings waited. The adapter is now `CoverageGated`: a covered or catalogue-rejected posting is yielded body-less with no request. Measured the same day: the technical areas held 16,131 postings in the 7-day window (5: 11,621, 8: 2,521, 3: 1,989) and 74,720 in 30 days; area 4 1,364 / 5,406; the whole site 69,073 / 329,083, ~7k new a day (a Sunday). So even a fully drained 7-day window is ~22% of the live technical catalogue — widening it needs slices, since one query stops at 15,000 rows.

**Instahyre traps** (`instahyre`; live-verified 2026-10-25 from a residential IP and the crawl host):

- **The search is open, the pages are not.** `GET /api/v1/job_search` answers plain HTTP from the crawl host under our own User-Agent, while every page of the site — the posting pages included — answers Cloudflare's 403 "Just a moment..." to any plain client, proxied or not. Scrapling's impersonating fetcher (`http`, no proxy) is served the pages from the crawl host's own IP at ~0.4 s each, so the provider has a `scraplingProviders` row for its pages only and keeps a plain client for the search. Without `SCRAPLING_FETCH_URL` every page read fails and the board fails as "none answered", which is the honest outcome. `robots.txt` names a sitemap and disallows nothing.
- **A board is a COMPANY-SIZE bucket** (`company_size`: `1` small, `3` medium, `2` large; anything else answers 400 "Invalid company size"). The search stops at 10,000 rows (`offset + limit` past it answers 400 "Result window is too large"), and the whole catalogue held 12,724 postings on 2026-10-25, so it has to be cut. Company size is the one filter found that PARTITIONS it — 3,705 + 6,900 + 2,119 = 12,724 exactly — where the job-function filter overlaps (backend 4,360 and full-stack 3,934 together are 5,293). A bucket past the window is read to the window and logged. The page size is 35 whatever `limit` asks; a full walk is ~365 pages.
- **A search row is everything but the body.** Title, employer, places (`locations`, comma-separated) and the public URL; the body is the page's schema.org JobPosting (`description` HTML, `datePosted` as a day, `employmentType`, `jobLocation[].address`). New postings only (`HydratingSource`), at most 600 page reads a run (the ~365 search pages answer in ~3 s each, so a full walk is already ~18 minutes of the scheduler's 2,400 s crawl budget), claimed before any request goes out, all requests paced to one a second. `CoverageGated`: a covered or catalogue-rejected posting costs no read. A closed posting's page still answers 200 but drops its JobPosting; a page without one is deferred, never read as gone, since a challenge page lacks one too and a listed posting is open.
- **"Work From Home" is one of the search's places** (1,106 of 12,724 postings; 199 of 2,119 in the medium bucket). As the only place it is remote work; beside an office it is kept as "Remote" for the location parse. `FallbackCountries` is `in`.
- **Mostly technical, not only.** The largest functions are backend (4,360), full-stack (3,934), other software (1,972) and data/ML (1,875), but support and sales roles appear ("Chat Support Associate"), so no tech hint is set. `is_sourced` was false on every sampled row: postings are the employers' own.

**Hirist / iimjobs traps** (`hirist`, `iimjobs`; live-verified 2026-10-25 from a residential IP and the crawl host):

- **Two sites, one backend, two providers.** hirist.tech (IT) and iimjobs.com (management, finance, IT) run the same Next.js app over the same API, each on its own host (`gladiator.hirist.tech`, `gladiator.iimjobs.com`) with its OWN posting database — a hirist id answers 404 on iimjobs. One adapter, parameterised by `hiristSite`. Plain HTTP passes from the crawl host under our own User-Agent; no proxy, no Scrapling. Both `robots.txt` ask for `Crawl-delay: 10` on the web host and the API host publishes none; every request of a run is paced to one a second (`hiristRequestInterval`).
- **A board is a CATEGORY id**, the `categoryId` of `GET /job/category/?categoryId=N&page=P&size=100` (header `version: 2`, as the web app sends). hirist's live categories are 1–17 (~29.6k postings on 2026-10-25, matching its 29,853-URL job sitemap); iimjobs' are 13 Banking & Finance, 14 Sales & Marketing, 15 IT & Systems, 16 Consulting, 17 HR, 19 SCM & Operations, 21 Legal, 22 BPO and 25 GenAI (~25.5k, matching its 25,654-URL sitemap). An unknown id answers 0 rows. Categories do not overlap (0 shared ids between 1 and 6 on page 0).
- **`totalPages` rounds DOWN.** Category 9 held 103 postings, answered `totalPages: 1`, and page 1 still served the last 3. The walk follows `hasMore` and an empty page instead. Page 0 is promoted postings first, not newest first, so the rows are re-sorted by `createdTimeMs` before the detail budget is spent.
- **A listing row is everything but the body.** `GET /job/detail?jobcode=<id>` carries it in `introText` (HTML, ~0.6–4.5k characters). A gone id answers `404 JOB_NOT_FOUND`; the web app also sends its reader home on `hasExpired`, `permanentlyRemoved` or `state == 2`, and so does the adapter. New postings only (`HydratingSource`), newest first, at most 1,200 detail requests a RUN and 300 a board, claimed in a sequential pass before any request goes out, so concurrency cannot spend the budget on an older posting. A cold start is ~30k (hirist) and ~25k (iimjobs) details, ~25 runs each.
- **Roughly half of hirist is copied from employers' own careers sites.** The detail's `crawled` flag was set on 23 of 40 sampled hirist postings (12 of 40 on iimjobs), and the employers are the Procores and Cargills we already crawl from their ATS. The adapter is `CoverageGated`: a posting whose employer a non-aggregator source already covers, or whose title the catalogue filter rejects, is yielded body-less with no detail request, so the pipeline counts it and the budget is not re-spent on it every run (the trap the naukri audit above describes). Native postings name agencies as often as employers (`HiringBlaze`, `HR LOBBY`); a `confidential` row hides the employer and is dropped before any request.
- **The title carries the employer.** Most titles read `PTC Software - Technical Lead - Full Stack Development` under the company `PTC`. The prefix is dropped when its first word is the company's (skipping a leading "The": `Hartford - Senior Director` under `The Hartford`), so the role matches the same posting elsewhere; a head that is not the employer (`Manager/Senior Manager - Full Stack & Generative AI`) stays.
- **Places are the site's own vocabulary.** `Gurgaon/Gurugram`, `Cochin/Kochi`, `Trivandrum/Thiruvananthapuram` and `Bangalore` already resolve through the location dictionary to their canonical cities. `Anywhere in India/Multiple Locations` would read as remote work ("anywhere") and is rewritten to `India`; `Delhi NCR` is rewritten to `Delhi`; `Others`, `Any Location`, `Metros` and `Overseas/International` are dropped. `Remote` is a place: a posting whose ONLY place is Remote is `remote`. The posting-level `workFromHome` flag is NOT mapped — it is set on postings whose only place is an office city (19 of 300 rows, Bangalore and Pune among them). `FallbackCountries` is `in`, since a few postings are in the Gulf.
- **Salary is in lakhs per annum.** `minSal: 30, maxSal: 45` is the "30-45 LPA" the site shows, so the figures are multiplied by 100,000 and published as INR a year — only when `hideSal` is 0 (9 of 300 hirist rows; 3 of 40 iimjobs details). `min`/`max` are the experience range in years; `min` above 0 becomes `ExperienceYearsMin`, and seniority is left to the title dictionary.
