- **Tech hints.** hirist's categories are all IT functions except 4 (semiconductor/VLSI — hardware), 10 (product design), 12 (product management) and 13 (business analysis/project management); the rest set `IsTechHint`. On iimjobs only 15 (IT & Systems) and 25 (GenAI) do.
- **Not `fullBoardListing`.** Nothing states that the walk saw the whole category, and a later page failing ends the walk with what was read.

**Shine traps** (`shine`; live-verified 2026-10-25 from a residential IP and the crawl host):

- **Plain HTTP under our own User-Agent passes**, from the crawl host as from anywhere: the sitemaps and the posting pages answer 200 in ~1 s. `robots.txt` disallows `/api/*` (so no JSON search), `/job-search/` variants and two id prefixes under `/jobs/1*` / `/jobs/9*` that are not posting URLs; the posting pages (`/jobs/<title>/<company>/<id>`) are allowed, and the only named user agents are `Scrapy` and `Trabajo`.
- **The sitemaps are the listing.** `index-all-sitemap.xml` names `all_jd_sitemap.xml` and `all_jd_<n>_sitemap.xml` (10,000 URLs each): 222,925 distinct postings on 2026-10-25, ids 19.4M–19.93M bar a few dozen older ones, `lastmod` spread over the last week (~10k a day). The index is regenerated daily and names only the files that exist — `all_jd_23_sitemap.xml` answered 500 the same afternoon it had answered 200, after the index stopped naming it. Any NAMED file failing fails the crawl. Not `fullCatalog`: nothing states a total the walk could be checked against.
- **Both slugs are cosmetic; the id is the identity.** `/jobs/x/y/19513074` serves the posting; a gone id answers 404, read as gone. The URL spells the title and the employer (`/jobs/senior-data-engineer-1/mastercard/19679658`), which is what makes the adapter `CoverageGated` without a request: the gate is asked about the employer the URL spells and judges the title it spells, and a condemned posting is yielded body-less.
- **The page's schema.org JobPosting is the posting.** `description` is the body (plain text with line breaks); `datePosted` is RFC 3339 in IST; `experienceRequirements.monthsOfExperience` is the floor of the range the page shows ("4 to 8 Yrs" is 48) and becomes `ExperienceYearsMin`; `baseSalary` (6 of 40 sampled) is INR a YEAR in rupees, mapped as stated; `jobLocation[]` carries locality and region per place ("All India" names no city and is rewritten to `India`). `occupationalCategory` is NOT a signal — 28 of 40 sampled postings, hotels and banks among them, read "General / Other Software" — so no tech hint is set; ~7 of 40 sampled titles were technical. `addressCountry` is always `IN`, including on reposted US adverts, so it only feeds `FallbackCountries`.
- **Many posters are consultancies or individual recruiters** ("Headstart Manpower Consultants.", "MD SHAKEEL AHAMED"), and some repost adverts from other aggregators. They are filed under the name the posting gives; the cross-source duplicate suppression is what handles a copy.
- **The run is bounded.** One limiter paces every request at one a second with two workers, and a run reads at most `shineMaxNewPerRun` (1,800, 30 minutes — inside the scheduler's default 2,400 s crawl budget) unseen postings, newest id first; at the default two-hour cadence that is ~21k a day against ~10k new, so the cold backlog drains in about three weeks, less whatever the gate condemns.

**engage traps** (`engage`; live-verified 2026-10-25 from a residential IP, fsn and host2):

- **A board is an OCCUPATION CATEGORY, not a tenant.** engage (en-gage.net, エン・ジャパン's free ATS) gives every tenant a careers page at `en-gage.net/<slug>/`, but that page caps each employment type at 100 postings and paginates nothing, and reaching a tenant needs its slug. The cross-tenant search the site's own UI runs on, `GET /user/api/search/result_work_list/?job=<code>`, reaches every tenant at once: `400000` (IT) held 17,152 postings, `350000` (creative/web) 11,045, out of ~741k across all categories. Top codes are six digits in steps of 50,000 (100000-650000), with middle codes under them (`401000` IT development 7,156, `401500` 614, `402000` 706, `402500` 3,590, `403000` 1,311, `403500` 567, `404000` 154, `404500` 366, `405000` 575, `405500` IT support 1,242, `409000` 1,179 — together the 17k of `400000`). **An unknown code is not an error: it is ignored and the WHOLE catalogue (~741k) answers** — measured on `405515`, `405520` and friends, which are not codes while `405510` is. Every engage row carries `occupation_detail_category`, so a first page with a posting outside the board's prefix (`401000` -> `4010`) fails the board.
- **The search pages at most 10,000 rows.** Past row 10,000 `hasMorePages` turns false whatever `totalCount` says (`400000` stopped at page 167 of 286). A board whose first page reports more than that fails rather than crawling a slice: **the boards are the MIDDLE codes, never `400000` itself.**
- **The backend nodes disagree on order.** Two requests for the same page land on different nodes behind an AWS load balancer and answer different rows (page 2 shared nothing between two calls), so a cookie-less walk is a random sample with ~30% repeats. With the `AWSALB` sticky cookie every page answers the same rows on repeat, so the adapter runs over a cookie client (`cookieSessionSource`). Rows still repeat within one sticky walk (the syndicated rows fill whole pages and the engage rows around them recur), which the adapter dedups by id; a sticky walk of `405500` found 510 distinct postings, the same as the walk of its one detail code `405510` (508).
- **Paging is a stitch, not an offset.** Page 1 needs `f_t=0`; every later page needs `p_t` set to the `totalCount` the previous page reported — without it the search answers `result: success` with no rows, which reads as the end. A page answering `result: error` is a failed page. 60 rows a page; the end is `paginator.hasMorePages: false` or an empty page.
- **A row is the whole posting.** `work_contents`, `qualification`, `recruitment_bg`, `office_hours`, `holiday`, `treatment` and the salary text were compared with the posting page's JSON-LD and are not truncated, so the crawl makes no detail requests. The posting page is `en-gage.net/<company_url_root_dir>/work_<work_id>/`.
- **Two row shapes, and one is not ours.** Rows disagree on scalar types (`work_id` a number in one shape, a string in the other; booleans as `"True"`), so every scalar is read tolerantly. About 6% of rows are syndicated from another job site (`is_kb_work`): a hex `work_id`, no tenant slug, a click-tracking URL off-site. They are skipped.
- **The company is the REGISTERED name, width-normalized.** `company_name` often carries the hiring office (`ポールトゥウィン株式会社　仙台センター`, `パーソルテンプスタッフ株式会社／東海`); `official_corporate_name` does not, but is sometimes typed in full-width letters (`株式会社Ｗｉｄｓｌｅｙ`), so it goes through NFKC. The title is `official_occupation_name` (`システムエンジニア`), not the catch line in `occupation_name`.
- **Salary.** `salary_amount_from`/`_to` are annual yen for 月給 (the monthly figures sit in `_monthly` fields), 年俸 and 年収, and the wage itself for 時給 and 日給. A 0 ceiling is open-ended.
- **Places.** `address_1` is prefecture and city in Japanese, which the location dictionary does not read, so the country is set as Japan. A fully remote posting has `full_remote_office_address` set (its `address_1` reads `フルリモート（完全在宅）`) and is `remote`; `foreign_address` marks an overseas posting and sets no country.
- **Employment.** `正社員` and `正社員（無期雇用派遣）` (permanent dispatch) are full-time, `契約社員` contract, `アルバイト・パート` part-time; `派遣社員`, `業務委託（フリーランス）` and `新卒` are left to the pipeline.
- **Tech hint on every `40xxxx` code.** `350000` mixes UI engineers with video and print staff.
- **Not `fullBoardListing`.** A later page failing ends the walk with what was read.
**Mynavi traps** (`mynavi`; live-verified 2026-10-25 from a residential IP, fsn and host2):

- **The crawl hosts are refused at the origin.** Both fsn and host2 get `403 Forbidden` from Mynavi's own Apache behind CloudFront on every page, listing and detail alike; a residential IP and `SOURCES_PROXY_URL` are served 200 (~3.5 s a page through the proxy). The provider is in `proxiedProviders`, so without the proxy configured it cannot crawl at all.
- **IT is in a separate edition.** The main `/list/` edition (~62k direct postings) redirects its IT (`o16`) and electrical/mechanical (`o17`) occupation codes to `/engineer/list/<code>/`, a different template ("エンジニア求人サーチ"). A board is one of those two codes; the main edition is not crawled, because a Japanese non-IT title mostly resolves no category and would be stored only to stay out of search.
- **The engineer edition is 94% agency referrals.** Unfiltered, `o16` counts ~104.6k postings, ~98k of them employment type `e05` (人材紹介): cards that link off-site to `scouting.mynavi.jp`, with the employer hidden as `社名非公開`. The walk asks for `e01+e02+e03` (the site's direct postings) and skips any card that does not link a `/jobinfo-<a>-<b>-<c>-<d>/` page anyway. `o16` held 6,802 direct postings (137 pages of 50), `o17` 8,836.
- **The end is an empty page.** A page past the last answers 200 with no cards. The first page must carry its `全N件中` counter or the walk fails — a page with cards and no counter is not the results template.
- **A card is everything but the body.** The detail page carries a schema.org JobPosting (beside a BreadcrumbList): body, places (`addressRegion` prefecture, sometimes `addressLocality` ward, `addressCountry: JP`; `jobLocation` is ONE Place object on a single-place posting and an array otherwise — read as an array only, every single-place posting failed its parse and was deferred forever, which the first prod crawl caught), salary (`JPY`/`YEAR` strings), `employmentType`. The body is `description` plus the requirements, hours and benefits fields as headed sections; its own line breaks arrive double-escaped (`&amp;lt;br&amp;gt;`) and are restored. An expired posting answers 404. New postings only, 800 a run and 600 a board, claimed before any request goes out; every request is paced to one a second.
- **Places stay in Japanese.** The location dictionary knows no kanji place names, so the country comes from the structured `addressCountry` (`Countries: jp`), with Japan as every card's fallback country.
- **Tech hint on `o16` only.** `o17` is hardware engineering. `リモートワーク可` on a card is "remote work possible", not a work arrangement, and is not read.
- **Not `fullBoardListing`.** Nothing proves the walk saw every posting, and a later page failing ends the walk with what was read.

**Golang Jobs traps** (`golangjobs`; live-verified 2026-10-18 from a residential IP and from fsn):

- **The site is a client-rendered shell over Supabase, and that table is the only listing.** `sitemap.xml` names five section pages and no posting; the home page is 7 KB with an empty `#root`. The frontend reads `GET <project>.supabase.co/rest/v1/jobs` (PostgREST) with the project's public anon key in `apikey` and `Authorization: Bearer`. A wrong key answers 401 `Invalid API key`. `robots.txt` disallows only `/admin/`.
- **The project URL and anon key are read from the app bundle on every run** — the techinasia shape. The home page names `/assets/index-<hash>.js`, whose Supabase client is built as `new X("https://<ref>.supabase.co","<jwt>")`. A rotated key or a moved project reaches the next crawl by itself; a bundle that stops carrying that construction fails the crawl by name, and a key the backend refuses fails it with the 401. Only a `supabase.co` host is accepted.
- **The whole table is a couple of requests, bodies inline.** The project's max-rows is 1,000 (a larger `limit` is clamped), so the walk advances by the rows it RECEIVED, ordered by `id`. The count comes from the aggregate `select=count` — the only form of it in the body rather than a `Content-Range` header — read before and after the walk; any page failing, a count that moves, an empty table, or a distinct count off the total fails the crawl, which is what earns `fullCatalog`. Archived rows are invisible to the anon key (`is_archived=eq.true` answers `[]`).
- **The board never archives a posting, so age is the only staleness signal.** All 1,199 listed rows on 2026-10-18 were unarchived, going back to 2025-04; only 4 were posted in the last 30 days and 27 in the last 60. Checked against the postings they link to: of the Greenhouse and Lever ones, 8 of 17 posted within 60 days were still open against 18 of 269 older than three months, and every one of 106 Ashby postings was gone. `golangJobsMaxAge` (60 days) drops the rest on the way in, and since the provider is swept by source, a posting that ages past it is closed by the next run.
- **Half the board is ATS copies, all of them on boards we already crawl.** 642 of 1,199 `application_url`s are recognised by `atsboard` (greenhouse 270, ashby 107, lever 96, workday 32, workable 27, …) across 272 distinct boards, 216 of them already in `boards`. Inside the 60-day window it is 14 of 27, every one on a board we crawl, so the aggregator coverage gate discards them. Of the 56 boards we lack, every greenhouse/lever/ashby/workable one answered 404 or zero postings — they are not missing, they are dead.
- **The row's place is a city row, and "Remote" is a city.** A remote posting is filed under the city `Remote` with its hiring scope as the country: a country, a region (`Europe`, `APAC`, `EMEA`) or `Global`. Any other city states no arrangement, so `WorkMode` is left to the description.
- **Salary is yearly by the site's own markup**: the posting page's JobPosting publishes every range with `unitText: "YEAR"`, and the amounts agree (USD 220,000-280,000; INR 15-25 lakh). `salary_currency` defaults to `USD` on every row, so it counts only beside a bound. 229 of 1,199 rows carry one.
- **One job is sometimes re-posted as a second row under the same link** (Happl, 2026-09-08 and 2026-10-06). A link two rows share keeps the Golang Jobs page as `Job.URL`. Every posting is filed as a "Golang developer position" by the site's own page title, so each sets `IsTechHint` and the `go` skill.

**Harri traps** (`harri`; live-verified 2026-10-20 from a residential IP):

- **The board is a brand's career-page slug (`harri.com/<slug>`), and the data is the keyless JSON API on `gateway.harri.com`** the single-page site calls: `GET /core/api/v1/profile/slug/<slug>` names the brand id, `POST /core/api/v1/harri_search/search_jobs` with `{"brand_level_ids":[id],"start","size",...}` lists, and `GET /core-reader/api/v1/profile/job/<id>` carries the HTML body. The slug lookup is case-insensitive; an unknown slug answers **HTTP 400** ("Slug not found"), read as `ErrBoardGone`.
- **A brand is a tree.** A group's id lists every posting of every brand under it, and those brands are STORES (Jollibee North America: 198 postings under 120 brands such as "JB Mira Mesa"). So the employer is the catalogue company, never the posting's brand — but the posting URL carries the store's slug, which is why an apply link can name a slug (`Taco-Social`) other than the career page that listed it (`bicos`). Two catalogued slugs of one tree would list the same posting twice under two board-namespaced ids.
- **`start` pages the search; `from`, `offset` and `page` are silently ignored** and answer page one again. `hits` is exact (Panera: 183, read back as 183 distinct), so the walk earns `fullBoardListing` by requiring distinct ids == hits.
- **The detail answers for expired postings too** (`status: EXPIRED`), so anything not `PUBLISHED` is gone. Hydrating: a seen posting costs no detail request.
- **`Timing` holds both schedule and arrangement**: `FULL_TIME`/`PART_TIME` beside `FULLY_REMOTE`/`HYBRID_REMOTE`. Most postings carry both schedules, which states nothing, so an employment type is taken only from a lone one. `country_code` is "UK" for Britain, normalised through the location dictionary.
- **Compensation is not mapped**: "Up to" postings carry the ceiling in `compensation_from`, and the currency lives only in the detail's brand settings, null on some brands.
- **No metering met**: 100 detail requests at 8 in flight (~23 req/s) all answered 200. Paced at 5 req/s anyway (`harriRequestInterval`), since a group's bootstrap is one detail per posting (KFC's brand lists 953). An hourly frontline segment — expect near-zero technical yield.

**Work-mode and country fields the listing hides** (`teamtailor`, `paylocity`, `workable`, `greenhouse`; live-verified 2026-10-18 against four moderator reports):

- **Teamtailor's remote status is in `jobs.rss`, not on the page.** The job page renders it only in a `<dl>` whose label and value are in the site's language ("Remote status: Hybrid", "Status Homeoffice/Vor Ort: Hybrid"), drops the row for on-site, and its ld+json carries `jobLocationType: TELECOMMUTE` for `fully` and nothing for hybrid. The feed's `<remoteStatus>` is a closed enum (`none`/`onsite`/`hybrid`/`fully`; `temporary` documented) and the feed honours `per_page` and `offset`, so the adapter reads it once per run for the postings it hydrates, best-effort. A site that disables `/jobs` (jobs.proxify.io) 404s the feed too.
- **Paylocity's `LocationName` is a label, `JobLocation.Country` is the place.** "Remote Worker" and "Concord Office" name no place; the structured record behind them carries `Country` ("USA" on all 392 briefs sampled across 40 boards), which becomes `Job.Countries`.
- **Paylocity's `IsRemote` is a toggle, not an arrangement; `IndeedRemoteType` says which kind.** The posting page labels `IsRemote` + type `1` "Hybrid Remote" and `IsRemote` + type `2` "Fully Remote", and renders no arrangement label when `IsRemote` is false (live-verified 2026-10-23). Type `2` alone means nothing — 731 of 733 briefs across 290 boards carried it, nearly all non-remote — so the type is read only behind the toggle, and a false toggle leaves `WorkMode` empty rather than asserting onsite. Reading `IsRemote` alone filed every hybrid posting as remote (report 108; 35 of 88 stored-remote rows in a 616-posting sample). The adapter is not hydrating, so every crawl rewrites a posting's `work_mode` without `INGEST_REFETCH_ALL`.
- **Workable's widget has no workplace field.** `telecommuting` is the only arrangement signal on `/api/v1/widget/accounts/<board>`; the `workplace` enum (`hybrid`/`on_site`/`remote`) exists only on the v3 jobs list, which pages ten postings per request — not worth a request per ten postings on every board. A non-telecommuting posting is left to the description.
- **A Greenhouse restriction stated only in an application question is out of reach.** Kard's "We are a fully remote company hiring in the US, Canada, Argentina, or Brazil only" is the label of a form question, absent from `content`; reading it would cost a `?questions=true` request per posting, which the apply-form rule above forbids.
- **A bare-"Remote" Greenhouse posting's country is in its `offices[]`, and it is a FALLBACK, not `Countries`.** The listing already carries each posting's offices (`location` is Greenhouse's geocoded "City, Region, Country", often null; `name` is the employer's label, "Remote US" or "Headquarters"). `greenhouseOfficeCountry` reads them only when `location.name` is a bare remote marker and every office that is not itself just "Remote" places exactly one shared country, and yields `Job.FallbackCountries`, which `jobderive` consults after the location, the title marker and both description rescues — because an office is sometimes the headquarters, not the hiring scope. Measured 2026-10-23 over the 1,612 boards holding an affected posting (62,172 live postings, 8,737 bare-remote): it pins 1,570 on 451 boards, 92% `us`; against enrichment's own pinned country it agreed 179 times and disagreed 7, and against a title/description restriction 63 to 3 (the restriction wins those). The disagreements are an HQ abroad from the team and one label the dictionary misreads ("ID - Boise" as Indonesia). A scoped location ("Americas Remote", "Remote - EMEA") is never read, since one office would narrow a region to a country. Stored rows need `INGEST_REFETCH_ALL=1`: the fix changes what the LISTING yields.

**HigherMe traps** (`higherme`, live-verified 2026-10-20):

- **The board is a BRAND id, and a posting link does not carry it.** `app.higherme.com/jobs/<id>` names a posting; the brand behind it is only in the posting's record (`api.higherme.com/classic/jobs/<id>?includes=location.brand` → `relations.location.relations.brand.id`). The brand's own career page, `app.higherme.com/careers/<brand>`, is the one URL that names a board, so it is the only shape `atsboard` recognises. A seed built from apply links resolves each posting to its brand first.
- **An unknown filter key answers with the WHOLE platform** (~31.5k postings) instead of an error: `filters[company.id]` is silently ignored. `filters[brand.id]` is honoured — an unknown brand answers an empty listing — but the adapter still checks every posting's brand against the board and fails the crawl on a mismatch, because the other reading files tens of thousands of postings under one employer.
- **The brand is the board, not the per-posting company.** A brand can span several franchisee accounts, and the account name is an internal label ("Dunkin' | THG Patterson, LLC"), so postings carry the configured company. The platform's own JSON-LD names that account label as `hiringOrganization`; it is not used.
- **No posting date.** The listing carries only `updated_at`, and the posting page's `date_posted` is the same re-stamped value (a posting created in 2021 read as posted two days ago), so `PostedAt` is nil.
- **Salary has no currency field.** The platform's own posting page derives it from the location's country (USD for US, CAD otherwise) and the period from `type` (`hourly` → hour, anything else → year); the adapter does the same, for US and CA locations only.
- **`limit` is clamped to 100**, and the API sits behind an AWS load-balancer WAF with a rate-based rule that 403s the whole IP — tripped by ~70 requests in about six minutes during the spike. The adapter is paced (`highermeRequestInterval`).
**OCC Mundial traps** (`occ`; live-verified 2026-10-15, transport and scope re-measured 2026-10-21):

- **The listing is behind Cloudflare now; the posting JSON is not.** By 2026-10-21 every listing page answered the "Just a moment..." challenge (403, `cf-mitigated`) to the prod host and the crawl host alike. Through the rotating proxy a plain request passed 4 of 10 pages and the Scrapling tier's impersonating fetcher 7 of 10; its browser from the crawl host's own IP read 10 of 10 (~6 s a page). The walk needs every page, so the listing is a `scraplingProviders` row in `browser` mode with no proxy, while `oferta.occ.com.mx` answered 5 of 5 direct and keeps a plain client (`occSplit`).
- **Every category is a board** since the catalogue became broader than IT: the site lists 23 (`/empleos/trabajo-en-<slug>/`, the slugs in its own category links). Only `tecnologias-de-la-informacion-sistemas` (`occITBoard`) sets `IsTechHint`; the rest are left to the classifier. A board hydrates at most `occMaxNewPerBoard` (2,000) new postings per run, since the first run meets the whole catalogue as new.

- **The search pages refuse the project's bare User-Agent.** `www.occ.com.mx/empleos/…` (allowed by robots.txt, which disallows only account, help and `/rest` paths) answers a plain 403 to `freehire/0.1 (+https://freehire.me)`, to a bare `Mozilla/5.0` and to the project string with `Mozilla/5.0` appended, and 200 to `Mozilla/5.0 (compatible; freehire/0.1; +https://freehire.me)` — so the provider is a `compatibleUASource`, still naming us. The posting JSON host answers either. A third party saw a geo-scoped Cloudflare challenge from non-Mexican egress (career-ops, 2026-09-04); none was seen from Brazil on 2026-10-15.
- **The board is a CATEGORY, the site's own.** `/empleos/trabajo-en-tecnologias-de-la-informacion-sistemas/?page=N` lists the IT category, 20 cards a page: 4,361 postings over 219 pages, page 220 empty. Keyword paths (`/empleos/de-<keyword>/`, career-ops' approach) are a search, not a slice. Only the IT board sets `IsTechHint` (see above).
- **Two cards repeat at the top of every page**, past the last one included (data-id 21367373 and 21375686 all day). The walk ends on the first page adding no NEW id — page 220 carries no card at all. A full walk read **4,360 distinct ids against the stated 4,361**, so the stated total cannot prove the walk and the provider does not claim `fullBoardListing`.
- **The posting page is not the source; the split view's JSON is.** `/empleo/oferta/<id>/` answered `scraping abuse` (403) to every User-Agent after a handful of requests, while `oferta.occ.com.mx/offer/<id>/d/j?ipo=41&iapo=1` (the URL template in the listing's `#hduod` input, `j` for JSON) answers the posting with our own UA: 162 sequential requests, 0 refusals. Its keys are abbreviations: `t` title, `c.cn` employer, `ld` body (HTML), `pt` posted day, `c`/`l` city/state, `smin`/`smax`/`lss` pay, `uwt`/`uet` the work-arrangement and schedule FILTER links (`/empleos/tipo-home-office-remoto` etc.), which are mapped rather than the Spanish labels beside them. The JSON also carries the recruiter's name, phone and relay e-mail; none of it is read.
- **"Empresa confidencial" is a hidden employer**, ~8% of cards (402 of ~4,800 card renders). The card says so, so such a posting is skipped before its JSON is bought: it names no employer to file it under.
- **Pay**: `lss` is the display ("$45,000 - $50,000 Mensual") and the only place the period is stated; no currency is named anywhere, and on this Mexican board the `$` is the peso. 121 of 162 sampled showed a monthly range; the rest read "Sueldo no mostrado por la empresa" or "Si el reclutador te contacta podrás conocer el sueldo" with zero bounds.
- **No ATS overlap observed**: `uaext` (an external apply URL) was empty on all 162 sampled postings, so Job.URL is the OCC posting page (the detail's `ur` without its tracking query) and there is no coverage gate.

**PowerToFly traps** (`powertofly`; live-verified 2026-10-23):

- **The feed grew past its page cap and nothing said so.** `/api/v1/jobs/` reported 35,405 postings over 709 pages of 50 (`meta.total`/`meta.pages`; page 709 held 5 with `next_page: null`, page 710 was empty), against ~192 pages at onboarding. The cap was 400 and ended the loop as a success, so every run read exactly 20,000 postings and never reached employers listed past page 400 (Deloitte, Morgan Stanley). The cap is now 1000 and reaching it FAILS the crawl.
- **`fullCatalog`, and it is the only close this feed's stale rows can get.** A run's listing is ~98% ATS-covered: 19,607 of 20,000 were skipped as "company already covered by a non-aggregator source", and only 393 written. A skipped posting is neither written nor touched, so the copies stored before its employer's own ATS was crawled never refresh, and the company-scoped sweep — which reaches only companies a run wrote — never closed them: 10,946 open rows against 398 seen in two days. The walk ends only on `next_page: null` and fails on any page error, on an empty page that still names a next page, and at the cap, so a run is the whole feed or an error and the source-scoped sweep may close what it did not see.

**Jobindex traps** (`jobindex`; live-verified 2026-10-25 from a residential IP, fsn and host2):

- **No API; the search page's `var Stash = {...}` object is the feed.** `/jobsoegning.json` answers 204. The object carries every posting's id, title, employer, area and date, plus the rendered card.
- **A query reaches 50 pages of 20 — 1000 postings — against a ~17.6k catalogue.** The crawl walks the ~78 subcategories (`subid`) the page itself lists, so a new one is crawled without a release. Three (Pædagog, Pleje og omsorg, Detailhandel, 1.1k-1.5k each, none technical) are larger than the window; they are walked newest-first so what is out of reach is always their oldest tail.
- **Boardless on purpose, although the subcategories look like boards.** A posting is filed under several (the seven IT subcategories sum to ~1.5k for ~975 distinct IT postings) and the pipeline namespaces `external_id` by board, so per-subcategory boards would store most postings twice. One crawl that de-duplicates by posting id stores each once.
- **The description is a ~340-character teaser and nothing longer exists on Jobindex.** `/vis-job/<id>` renders the same card; the body lives on the employer's site behind "Se jobbet", on hosts that carried no schema.org JobPosting in a sample. So the adapter spends no detail request.
- **Any failed page fails the crawl.** The sweep closes what a run did not see, and ~1.2k pages a crawl make a quietly-missing subcategory likely; the crawl hosts answered every request in ~1s with no refusals.
- **Overlap with the other Danish feeds is partial.** Of 790 distinct IT postings, at most 285 (36%) share an exact title with an open `jobnet`/`jobdanmark` row; 153 apply through hr-manager, which nothing else crawls.

## Limitations
- The ingest sweep has a trade-off: a missed run can leave an orphan open until a future reconcile; the change window is sized wide enough to absorb a skipped cron.
- Self-closing sources: a missed `removed` event from the feed can leave a vacancy open until the next reindex.

## A crawl that reads nothing of what it listed must fail

`echojobs` published `ingested=0 failed=0`, exit 0, green unit, every four hours for 19 days
while its pages sat behind a bot wall (freehire#2588). A dropped posting never reaches the
pipeline — `Stats` counts `saveOne` failures, not candidates an adapter discarded — so from
every side but the adapter's, a total outage and a source with nothing to offer are the same
result.

So a hydrating adapter that listed candidates and read **none** of them returns an error.
There is deliberately no threshold: "some candidates, none read" is the whole signal, and how
many consecutive failures matter is `board_health`'s question. No candidates at all still
succeeds — a quiet source is not a broken one.

## The Scrapling tier (the sources no plain client reaches)

`scraplingProviders` (scraplingtier.go) sends a provider's requests through
[services/scrapling-fetch](../../../services/scrapling-fetch/README.md) on the crawl host, over
[internal/platform/scraplingfetch](../../platform/scraplingfetch/AGENTS.md). Each row names a
mode — `http` (one request with Chrome's TLS fingerprint, ~0.2-2 s) or `browser` (a stealth
Chromium navigation that solves Cloudflare challenges, ~5-9 s once the first page on a host has
paid ~110 s for the challenge) — and whether it egresses through the proxy the service holds.
Every row is the cheapest route the 2026-10-20 spike measured to pass;
[the design](../../../docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-20-scrapling-fetch-design.md) has the
per-source, per-mode table, including the sources that spike did NOT rescue (echojobs, neogov,
dodo).

Two of those were rescued on 2026-10-21, once the proxy came back from a Webshare outage that
had poisoned the spike's measurements: `neogov` (the crawl host's IP times out at TCP connect;
`http` + proxy reads the XHR listing and the detail pages) and `echojobs` (Vercel's checkpoint:
`http` + proxy drew a 429 about half the time, `browser` + proxy passed 10 of 10). echojobs used
to have a chromedp browser tier of its own (`browsertier.go`, freehire#2588); the checkpoint
began answering it with a 403 and it was deleted in favour of this row.

It replaced the hosted Firecrawl tier, which carried bayt, gulftalent, wellfound, wantapply's
sitemap and hh's detail pages at a credit per page and ran out of credits in 2026-10.

**Measure what is behind the wall before adding a provider**: `bayt`'s IT category passed
**0 of 33** titles through `classify.IsTech` and `gulftalent`'s postings **5 of 400**
(2026-09-07). A browser page is not free — ~1-1.7 GB per live session on the crawl host.

**`ApplyScraplingEgress` must run LAST**, and `cmd/ingest` does. `gulftalent` is in
`proxiedFingerprintProviders` and `infojobs` in `proxiedProviders`, so both tiers write the same
registry entry — the override is *wanted* (those transports are measured as refused, this one as
served), so it is ordered instead of being a silent last-write-wins. Without `SCRAPLING_FETCH_URL` the override does not happen
and each keeps its earlier transport — which is also the rollback.

No adapter changed: the tier is a `*Client` whose transport forwards to the service, so every
getter, the retries and the body cap are the same code every other provider uses.

**Some providers send only PART of a crawl through it.** `wantapply` reads only the .com sitemap
there (~2 755 vacancies against the free .cy mirror's ~605) and every page on the free client.
`startupjobs` sends only its Cloudflare-challenged site pages; its sitemap CDN and Algolia index
stay plain. `hh` sends only detail hydration: its listing works on the plain direct IP while its
detail pages redirect through the proxy to a DDoS-Guard captcha, so its build hands
`NewHHWithDetailGetter` a fresh, always-unproxied `NewClient()` for listing — deliberately NOT the
`direct` parameter, which is the PROXIED client whenever `SOURCES_PROXY_URL` is set for any
other provider.

**Cake traps** (`cake`; live-verified 2026-10-25 from a residential IP and from fsn):

- **The search reads at most 499 rows per query.** `page * per_page` must stay below 500 and `per_page` is capped at 50, so the adapter reads 45 rows over at most 11 pages. A query matching more answers `total_entries: 500, total_relation: "gte"`, so `"eq"` with a total inside the window is the only proof a slice was read whole. The whole catalogue (~16k postings, ~3.3k under the `it_` family) is read as slices split recursively over the platform's own facets — professions, then job type, seniority, experience bracket, remote mode, management span. Each is a partition: the 392 leaf professions' totals summed exactly to their 20 families', and every other facet's values summed exactly to the slice they split. Only LEAF professions (`family_leaf`) are queried; a family value overlaps its leaves. `available_facets` lists the whole catalogue's values whatever the filter, so a split also queries values the slice does not hold (an empty answer costs one request).
- **Two slices exceed the window on every facet** — a retail chain's full-time entry-level store vacancies and a process-engineering associate block, hundreds of near-identical postings each. Their first 495 rows are read and the run logs the count; postings past that depth are not seen.
- **The crawl hosts' IPs are Cloudflare-challenged** (403 `cf-mitigated: challenge` on api and www alike), and one exit is refused with 429 / `Retry-After: ~50` after ~40 requests a minute — the fixed proxy exit too. The rotating gateway was served 60 of 60 searches, so `cake` is in both `proxiedProviders` and `rotatingProxyProviders`, and a refused request (403/429) is retried through a fresh exit. The project's own User-Agent is accepted.
- **The search's description is plain text; the detail (`GET /jobs/{path}`) carries the HTML body, a separate `requirements` section, `created_at`, each location's `country_code` and the `remote` mode.** It is bought only for postings the catalogue lacks (HydratingSource), at most 2,500 a run. A gone posting answers 404 `"The job is not found"`. The detail carries no company name (`page_path` only), so the employer comes from the listing row.
- **`path` is the identity**: the detail resolves by it alone, so it is unique across employers, and the listing carries no numeric id to use instead.
- **A 0.00–0.00 salary means not stated**, and `hide_salary_completely` / `hide_salary_max` hide what the employer typed. `profession_v2` under `it_` sets `IsTechHint` — all 49 IT leaves are technical crafts — and is otherwise left to the dictionaries, as are the `associate`, `mid_senior_level` and `director` seniority levels, which each span two of ours.
**104 Job Bank traps** (`job104`; live-verified 2026-10-25 from a residential IP, fsn and the crawl host):

- **The board is a 104 AREA CODE, not a category.** A posting has one address (`jobAddrNo`) but up to three job categories (`jobCat`), and the pipeline namespaces a posting's identity by board — category boards would store most postings two or three times. Areas partition the catalogue. A board is a district (`6001001005`), or a whole county/overseas region (`6001023000`, `6003002000`) where that reads in under 100 pages; `job104AreaPrefix` turns the code into the address prefix its postings share.
- **The search serves at most 100 pages of ~30 rows** and its `total` saturates at 1001, so `lastPage < 100` is the only proof a slice was read whole. A board at the cap is split down the category tree (`static.104.com.tw/category-tool/json/JobCat.json`: 18 families → 44 groups → 619 leaves) and then over the five `jobexp` brackets, which are disjoint (their totals sum to the unfiltered one). A posting met in several category slices is kept once. Measured over the areas: Taiwan alone is ~12k listing pages per full pass with 57 districts still at the cap, so this provider needs shards.
- **The filters are soft.** Promoted rows from other areas are mixed into a page (an area holding 5 postings answered 11 rows), so a row is kept only when its own `jobAddrNo` lies inside the board's area.
- **The listing row is the whole posting.** Its `description` is the detail endpoint's `jobDescription` verbatim, so a crawl issues no detail requests. Identity is the job code at the end of `link.job` (`/job/8puve`); the row's `jobNo` is a different internal id the detail endpoint 404s on.
- **Salary:** `s10` 10 is "negotiable" (both bounds 0), 30 hourly, 40 daily, 50 monthly, 60 yearly, 70 part-time monthly; `salaryHigh` 9999999 is "and up". Always TWD. `period` is the minimum years of experience PLUS ONE, 0 meaning no requirement. `jobRo` 3 (高階, "senior") is a pay band, not an employment type, and is not mapped.
- **Transport:** both crawl hosts get a custom Cloudflare 403 for a plain client and through BOTH proxies; the Go Chrome-fingerprint client (`fingerprinthttp`) gets the "Just a moment" challenge instead. The Scrapling service's `http` mode (curl_cffi impersonation) from the crawl host's own IP is served at ~2-4 s a page, so `job104` is a `scraplingProviders` row. Requests carry a same-origin `Referer`. **The search rate-limits, and the shards add up**: the scheduler runs several of the provider's shards at once, each with its own limiter, and five shards at one request every 0.8 s each (~6/s together) drew 429s (no `Retry-After`) within minutes — 25 of shard 1's 69 boards failed, shards 2-5 almost whole. A repeat seconds later is served. The adapter paces each run to one request every 4 s and retries a 403/429 itself with a doubling 5 s backoff (the Scrapling client retries nothing). At that pace a full pass (~12k pages) needs 12 shards under a 4500 s budget, and the cadence is set long enough that the shards do not monopolise the heavy pool. `static.104.com.tw` has no such edge and is read on a plain client.
- **`jobCat` under 2007 (資訊軟體系統類) sets `IsTechHint`**; its 37 leaves are all technical crafts. Location is the area table's English name (`Zhongzheng District, Taipei City, Taiwan`), and only a Taiwanese address states a country.
